PrimusNeo Commercial Engagement Model — Overview

Document set: Engagement Models v0.1 (May 2026) Status: Draft for internal alignment. Numbers are anchors, not quotes. Audience: Founding team, partner ecosystem (Escherbridge, OmniOne, PLAN, AZOA), enterprise prospects.

1. Why this document exists

PrimusNeo is a federated coordination stack — not a product, not a SaaS, not a blockchain. Four sovereign technology layers (built and stewarded by four sovereign organizations) compose into a single operating fabric for any group of people, machines, and capital that needs to coordinate at scale.

That structure makes pricing genuinely hard:

  • Four layers with different licensing postures (two creative-commons, one closed/partial, one Apache-2.0 open source with a commercial managed tier).
  • Ten verticals with wildly different willingness-to-pay, procurement norms, and service multipliers (a hospital is not a DAO is not a port).
  • Four classes of service (customization, integration, deployment, sustained upkeep) plus infra pass-through.
  • A founding commitment to “Proof Over Promises” — pricing must be defensible against the actual friction-cost the buyer is already paying (McKinsey, IMF, UNIDO, sector-specific sources).

This document set translates that complexity into a bounded, value-based commercial model that any deal team can use to quote a number, defend it, and close it without burning the open-source ecosystem that feeds the stack.

2. The four layers (and their licensing posture)

Layer Steward Function Licensing Relative Price
AMP PLAN Systems Spatial OS · privacy-first 3D collaboration · offline-capable community OS · Operational Access Management (OAM) Closed-core / partial-OSS (community runtime open, enterprise spatial + multi-tenant + OAM kernel closed) $$$$ — top of stack (most mature layer; ships two production-grade capabilities — Spatial OS + OAM — in one runtime)
Fractal Engine Escherbridge Intelligence · historical archive · analytics · actionable signals on operational telemetry Creative Commons with Commercial Use Restrictions (CC-BY-NC + commercial license tier) $$$
AZOA AZOA Federation rails · self-sovereign identity & consent · durable quest workflows · settlement across chains and fiat · SurrealDB data engine Open-source engine (Apache-2.0); commercial tier = PrimusNeo-managed nodes, enterprise federation gateway, certified rail adapters $$$
NEOS OmniOne Governance · councils · charters · contribution accounting · holonic orgs Creative Commons with Commercial Use Restrictions (CC-BY-NC-SA + commercial license tier) $$

Naming note: The workflow/interoperability layer is AZOA (Autonomous Zones of Action) in all PrimusNeo commercial documents and product copy. AZOA supersedes the layer formerly branded OASIS / OAS; the legacy names are not used in PrimusNeo deliverables. (“GSA OASIS+”, the unrelated U.S. federal contract vehicle, keeps its name in market-research citations.)

See 02-licensing-model.md for the legal mechanics of the CC-NC + dual-license arrangement, and the per-layer files in layers/ for engagement-level pricing per layer.

3. The pricing philosophy in one paragraph

Price against the friction we remove, capped by the value we create, floored by the cost-to-serve. Every quote is built up from three numbers: (a) the buyer’s current annualized cost of the coordination friction we displace (McKinsey/UNIDO/sector benchmarks), (b) the defensible value share we can capture (typically 5–25% of friction removed, depending on layer and attribution clarity), and (c) the floor cost of infra, support, and stewardship payouts to the federated stewards. We quote inside that range. We never quote a number we cannot defend with a sourced friction-cost.

That philosophy is operationalized in 01-pricing-model.md.

4. The engagement-model matrix

PrimusNeo engagements are quoted on three axes:

                    LAYERS (4)
                   ┌───────┬───────┬───────┬───────┐
                   │  AMP  │ NEOS  │ AZOA  │FRACTAL│
                   ├───────┼───────┼───────┼───────┤
INDUSTRIES (10)    │       │       │       │       │
  Agriculture      │   $   │   $$  │   $$  │   $$$ │
  Construction     │   $$$ │   $$  │   $$  │   $$  │
  Energy           │   $$  │   $$  │   $$$ │   $$$ │
  Finance          │   $   │   $$$ │   $$$ │   $$$ │
  Governance       │   $$  │   $$$ │   $$  │   $$  │
  Healthcare       │   $$  │   $$$ │   $$$ │   $$$ │
  Logistics        │   $$  │   $$  │   $$$ │   $$  │
  Manufacturing    │   $$$ │   $$  │   $$  │   $$$ │
  Robotics         │   $$$ │   $$  │   $$  │   $$$ │
  Technology       │   $$  │   $$  │   $$$ │   $$  │
                   └───────┴───────┴───────┴───────┘
                       ×
                  SERVICES (5)
        Customization · Integration · Deployment
              · Sustained Upkeep · Infra

Each industry doc in industries/ gives the actual dollar ranges for that vertical at Small / Mid / Large scale per layer, plus the recommended bundle pricing.

5. The five service classes

These are priced separately from license / subscription and scoped per engagement. See 03-services-catalog.md for full SKU detail.

Class What it covers Typical pricing mechanic
Customization Vertical-specific schemas, council templates, twin extensions, custom Fractal models, WASM extensions T&M day rate or fixed-fee scope
Integration IT/OT bridges, ERP/MES/EHR/CRM/TMS connectors, identity federation, AZOA connector buildouts Fixed-fee per connector or T&M
Deployment Reference architecture rollout, environment provisioning, data migration, cutover, training, change management Fixed-fee bundle or per-site
Sustained Upkeep Tier-1/2/3 support, version upgrades, security patching, AZOA connector maintenance, model retraining cadence Annual % of license (typically 18–25%) or all-in MSA
Infra (pass-through) Cloud (AWS/Azure/GCP), edge compute, storage tiers, observability, chain gas, optional Tie Town reference colo Pass-through + 10–18% mgmt fee

6. Tie Town as the reference architecture

Every engagement is grounded in the Tie Town [TX] deployment as living proof-of-capability. Quote language: “This is the same stack running the Tie Town media and creator house in McDade, Texas — extended for your context. We don’t ship slideware; we extend a system that’s already in production.”

This collapses the buyer’s pilot-purgatory risk (McKinsey: 70% of Industry 4.0 pilots never scale) into a known, working reference — which is the single biggest enterprise-deal de-risker we have.

7. How to use this document set

  1. Start with 01-pricing-model.md — the value-based pricing formula and the layer/scale anchor grid.
  2. Read 02-licensing-model.md to understand the CC-NC + commercial-license dual track for NEOS and Fractal Engine, the closed-core arrangement for AMP, and the Apache-2.0 + managed-service arrangement for AZOA.
  3. Read 03-services-catalog.md for the service SKUs and day rates.
  4. For anything smaller than a Small-tier engagement — community deployments, SMBs, or a first paid pilot inside an enterprise — start from 04-community-pilot-pricing.md, the entry on-ramp that ladders into these anchors.
  5. Pick the relevant industry file in industries/ for the vertical you’re quoting. Each file gives Small / Mid / Large engagement bundles with $-range, mechanics, and the friction-cost anchor used to defend the number.
  6. Pick the relevant layer files in layers/ if the buyer wants single-layer adoption only (common for AZOA-only data-fabric deals and Fractal-only analytics deals).

8. Revisions

Version Date Author Change

PrimusNeo Value-Based Pricing Model

Status: Draft v0.1 Dependencies: 00-overview.md, 02-licensing-model.md, 03-services-catalog.md, research/layer-comparables.md

1. The pricing formula

Every PrimusNeo quote is built from three numbers:

                  ┌───────────────────────────────┐
                  │  FRICTION_COST  (anchor)      │   ← sourced from McKinsey, UNIDO, IMF,
                  │  =  buyer's current annualized│      sector research per industry doc
                  │     cost of the coordination  │
                  │     problem we displace       │
                  └────────────┬──────────────────┘
                               │
                  ┌────────────▼──────────────────┐
                  │  VALUE_SHARE   (capture)      │   ← 5–25% of friction removed,
                  │  =  what PrimusNeo can        │      adjusted for attribution clarity
                  │     defensibly capture        │      and layer mix (see §3)
                  └────────────┬──────────────────┘
                               │
                  ┌────────────▼──────────────────┐
                  │  COST_FLOOR   (must clear)    │   ← infra + steward royalty +
                  │  =  cost-to-serve including   │      service delivery cost +
                  │     steward royalties         │      target gross margin
                  └────────────┬──────────────────┘
                               │
                       Final quote sits in
                  [ max(COST_FLOOR, 0.5 × VALUE_SHARE),
                          1.0 × VALUE_SHARE ]

We never quote below COST_FLOOR. We rarely quote above VALUE_SHARE. The defensible quote band is the intersection.

Entry engagements — community deployments, paid pilots, anything below the Small anchors in §3 — are priced by 04-community-pilot-pricing.md: the same formula, pre-clamped into published flat tiers so there is nothing to negotiate at the low end.

2. Why value-based, not cost-plus

Cost-plus pricing under-prices the federated stack. The four-layer architecture replaces between 4 and 12 point solutions in a typical enterprise stack, with friction-cost displacement that runs 5–50x our delivery cost. Cost-plus would leave 80%+ of the captured value on the buyer’s table — which is fine for commodity infrastructure but the wrong default for coordination infrastructure that takes years to build and has no clean substitute.

Value-based pricing also forces us to do the work of grounding every quote in a sourced friction-cost (per industry doc). That discipline is what makes the quote defensible in front of a procurement committee that has read McKinsey too.

3. Layer pricing anchors

These are the per-layer anchor ranges derived from research/layer-comparables.md. They are the starting position for any quote and the upper bound on what we charge without a documented friction-cost override.

3.1 AMP — Spatial OS

AMP is the top-priced layer in the PrimusNeo stack because it is the most mature layer (years of PLAN Systems hardening + live Tie Town deployment) AND it delivers two production-grade capabilities in one runtime: Spatial OS + Operational Access Management (OAM). The OAM kernel is what justifies AMP sitting above Fractal Engine on the price ladder; without it, AMP would be a Unity-Industrial competitor priced at $4,950/seat/yr. With it, AMP also displaces Okta/CyberArk/BeyondTrust-class operational IAM/PAM spend.

Scale Definition Annual License Anchor Services Multiplier (Y1)
Small 1–3 sites, <50 concurrent spatial users, <200 OAM identities $30K–$75K/yr 1.5–2.5x
Mid 4–15 sites, 50–500 concurrent, 200–2,500 OAM identities $150K–$450K/yr 1.5–2.5x
Large 15+ sites, multi-org federation, 500+ concurrent, 2,500+ OAM identities $600K–$2.5M/yr 1.5–2.5x

Comparable anchors (combined surface area AMP displaces): NVIDIA Omniverse Enterprise ($4,500/GPU/yr) + Unity Industry ($4,950/seat/yr) + Unreal Enterprise ($1,850/seat/yr) on the spatial side; Okta Workforce Identity ($5–$15/user/mo + add-ons), CyberArk PAM ($50–$150/user/yr), BeyondTrust Privileged Remote Access (similar) on the operational access side.

OAM-only entry SKU available at 70% of the Spatial-OS line-item for buyers (regulated FS, defense-adjacent, NERC CIP-driven utilities) where the access-management value lands before the spatial twin value.

3.2 NEOS — Governance

Scale Definition Annual License Anchor Services Multiplier (Y1)
Small Single org, 1 council, <50 members $6K–$18K/yr 0.5–1x
Mid Multi-council, 50–500 members $30K–$90K/yr 0.5–1x
Large Federated governance, 500+ members, multiple orgs $120K–$400K/yr 0.5–1x

Comparable anchors: Diligent ($12K–$60K+/yr), Microsoft Viva Suite ($144/seat/yr), Loomio ($1.2K–$7.2K/yr), Aragon/Snapshot/Tally (free). CC-NC dual-license: free for non-commercial; commercial use at these rates.

3.3 AZOA — Federation Rails (Workflows & Settlement)

AZOA (Autonomous Zones of Action) is the open-source (Apache-2.0) engine for structured financial workflows: self-sovereign identity & consent, durable quests, and settlement rails across chains and fiat, with SurrealDB as its sole data engine. The license line here is the commercial managed tier — PrimusNeo-managed nodes, the enterprise federation gateway, and certified rail adapters — not the engine source, which is free to self-host.

Scale Definition Annual License Anchor Services Multiplier (Y1)
Small <10 integrations, single domain, <1M API calls/mo $24K–$60K/yr 2–3x
Mid 10–50 integrations, multi-chain/cloud, 1M–50M calls/mo $80K–$250K/yr 2–3x
Large 50+ integrations, full federation engine, >50M calls/mo, cross-org $350K–$1.5M/yr 2–3x

Comparable anchors: MuleSoft ($1,250/vCore/month → $80K–$2M+/yr), Boomi ($6.6K–six-figure), Workato (20–30% below MuleSoft). Gas/chain fees pass-through at cost + 12% mgmt fee.

3.4 Fractal Engine — Intelligence

Scale Definition Annual License Anchor Services Multiplier (Y1)
Small <5 data sources, departmental analytics, <1TB managed $30K–$75K/yr 1–2x
Mid 5–20 data sources, operational telemetry + historical archive, 1–20TB $100K–$350K/yr 1–2x
Large 20+ sources, cross-org intelligence, real-time + archive, >20TB $500K–$2M+/yr 1–2x

Comparable anchors: Palantir Foundry ($250K–$10M+/yr commercial), Snowflake ($25K–$10M+/yr), Databricks (DBU + cloud compute), Splunk ($30K–$60K/month at mid-scale). CC-NC dual-license: research/nonprofit free; commercial production at these rates.

4. Bundle pricing (the deal you actually sign)

Most engagements adopt 2–4 layers as a bundle. Bundles are quoted as a single PFEL line item (see 02-licensing-model.md §5) with a small bundle premium and a clear bundle discount that nets favorably to the buyer relative to single-layer sum.

Bundle Composition Discount vs single-layer sum Premium for federation
Duet Any 2 layers 10% off +5% for federation engine
Triad Any 3 layers 18% off +5%
Full Stack All 4 layers 25% off +0% (federation is the value)

Worked example: a Mid-tier full-stack engagement totals roughly:

AMP   Mid:  $300K  (midpoint of $150K–$450K — spatial OS + OAM)
NEOS  Mid:   $60K  (midpoint of $30K–$90K)
AZOA   Mid:  $160K  (midpoint of $80K–$250K)
Fract Mid:  $220K  (midpoint of $100K–$350K)
                ─────
Subtotal:   $740K
Bundle:     -25% → $555K
Services Y1 (~2x license blend): $1.11M
Sustained Upkeep Y1+ (22% of license, Silver SLA): $122K
Infra (pass-through + 12% mgmt fee): $60K–$180K
                ─────
Y1 TOTAL:   $1.85M–$2.0M
3-yr TOTAL: $3.4M–$4.0M  (Y2–Y3 mostly license + upkeep, services taper)

This lands inside the Mid-tier 3-yr TCO bands for nearly every vertical (logistics $900K–$2.7M, manufacturing $1.5M–$8M, healthcare $8M–$25M at the high end, agriculture $1.2M–$6M, energy $2.5M–$10M). Where it lands at the high end of a band (manufacturing, energy, healthcare-mid), the friction-cost anchor justifies the position — especially given AMP’s dual Spatial-OS + OAM displacement. Where it lands at the low end (logistics, agriculture), bundle discount + outcome-based component keeps the buyer engaged.

5. Modulating the quote by vertical

The same Mid-tier quote modulates ±30% based on three vertical-specific signals:

Modulator Increase quote Decrease quote
Regulatory tailwind Energy (NERC CIP, CSRD, CBAM), Healthcare (HITRUST, EHDS), Finance (MiCA, SAB 122) Construction (mostly voluntary ESG today)
Procurement maturity Finance, Energy, Public Governance (formal RFP + Big-4 benchmarking) Cooperatives, DAOs, early-stage startups
Friction-cost density per $1 of buyer revenue Manufacturing (70% pilot purgatory, $50B downtime), Healthcare ($50M–$500M EHR integration tax) Technology buyers (will build it themselves; lower friction relative to their internal eng capacity)

Adjust by ±10% per modulator that fires, capped at ±30% in either direction.

6. Standard commercial terms

These are the default contract terms. Negotiable but defensible:

Term Default Notes
Initial term 3 years Mirrors enterprise software norm; aligns with sustained upkeep math
Auto-renewal 1-year auto-renew with 90-day notice Standard
Annual escalator CPI + 3% (capped at 7%) Below the ServiceNow 5–10% renewal-uplift norm we’ll be benchmarked against
Payment terms Net 30; quarterly invoicing Annual prepay = 5% discount
Bundle discount 10/18/25% per §4 Hard floor — no further discount on bundle without VP+ sign-off
Multi-year discount 5% / yr (Y2 = -5%, Y3 = -10%) Stacks with bundle discount
Steward royalty Internal — see 02-licensing-model.md §4 Invisible to buyer; affects our COGS, not the quote
IP indemnity Capped at 2× annual fees for the affected layer Industry standard for enterprise software
Liability cap 2× annual fees, excluding gross negligence + IP indemnity Standard
Data export Customer data exportable in machine-readable form at any time Non-negotiable; preserves no-lock-in promise
Source escrow Available for closed-core AMP at >$500K ARR via Iron Mountain; not needed for AZOA (engine is Apache-2.0) Pass-through escrow agent fee
Termination for convenience After Y1, with 90-day notice, no refund of prepaid amounts Aligned with comparable enterprise software

7. Discounting authority

Approver Max additional discount Notes
Account Executive 5% (against bundle-discounted price) Standard concession
Sales VP 10% Plus a strategic-account flag for the deal review
Co-founder / CEO 15% Steward orgs must be notified — affects their royalty share
Steward Council vote Above 15% Required for any deal where bundle discount + multi-year + concession exceeds 35% net off list

No deal closes below COST_FLOOR. A losing deal at COST_FLOOR is better than a winning deal that bleeds money for three years.

8. Outcome-based / gainshare addenda

For verticals where attribution is feasible (manufacturing OEE, energy VPP, logistics on-time delivery, finance straight-through processing), we can offer an outcome-based addendum on top of the base license:

  • Base license priced at 80% of standard anchor (the buyer takes some discount up front).
  • Performance fee of 15–30% of documented annualized savings above an agreed baseline.
  • Baseline established by Fractal Engine measurement in a 60-day instrumentation phase before the contract goes hot.
  • Performance fee billed quarterly in arrears.
  • Cap on performance fee at 2× foregone base license in any year (so worst case the buyer ends up paying ~140% of standard, only when we’ve delivered 4–7x that in savings).

This addendum is opt-in for the buyer and is the right structure for CFO-led conversations where the buyer is willing to pay more only if we deliver. It is not offered into highly regulated rate-recovery contexts (energy utility rate cases) because the regulatory cost-allocation math becomes too tangled.

9. Worked friction-cost anchors per vertical (summary)

The full math per industry lives in industries/*.md. Headline anchors:

Vertical Friction anchor Source Typical PrimusNeo capture
Manufacturing $50B/yr global unplanned downtime; 70% pilot purgatory McKinsey, Siemens 5–15% of plant-level capture
Healthcare $50M–$500M EHR integration tax; $200K/yr HITRUST gate Becker’s, HITRUST 8–20% of integration spend
Finance $200K–$500K/yr/bank attestation premium; MiCA non-compliance €15M/3% revenue OCC, MiCA 10–25% of attestation premium
Energy $13.2M/yr industry-wide NERC CIP-015-1 alone; $5M–$50M IOU ADMS NERC, DOE 5–15% of CIP + governance spend
Logistics TradeLens-pattern wasted $1B+; $300K+/yr existing visibility spend Industry analysis 10–20% of consolidated platform spend
Construction 0.1–0.3% of ACV typical PM spend; 1–2% capex on digital programs McKinsey, Procore 5–15% of program tech budget
Agriculture 60% of profits on FSMA compliance at SMB scale; $84.9M → $36.1M MRV market USDA, GMInsights 8–18% of compliance + MRV spend
Robotics $50K–$200K+ ISO 10218 cert per cell; pilots fail 70% ISO, McKinsey 10–20% of fleet coordination spend
Governance $400K–$900K FedRAMP Mod; Llama at $2.5M/yr for Aave alone FedRAMP, public DAO governance 15–30% of governance services spend
Technology $2M+ 3-yr TCO on self-hosted Backstage at scale Roadie, Humanitec benchmarks 8–18% of platform-eng spend

PrimusNeo Licensing Model

Status: Draft v0.1 — needs legal review before any external commitment. Scope: Defines the legal and commercial licensing posture for all four layers and the federated stack as a whole.

1. The two-track licensing structure

PrimusNeo runs a two-track licensing model that mirrors successful open-core precedents (Elastic SSPL, MongoDB SSPL, HashiCorp BSL, Grafana AGPL, Sentry FSL) while honoring the federated steward arrangement.

Track Layers Default license Commercial trigger
Open / CC-NC track NEOS, Fractal Engine Creative Commons BY-NC-SA 4.0 (or CC-BY-NC 4.0 where derivative concerns apply) Any commercial use, redistribution, or hosted-as-a-service offering
Closed-core track AMP Closed-source enterprise license; community runtime / SDK selectively open-sourced under Apache-2.0 or MIT Any production deployment beyond community / non-commercial pilot scope
Open-source + managed track AZOA Entire engine Apache-2.0 — self-hosting is free and unrestricted PrimusNeo-managed nodes, enterprise federation gateway, certified rail adapters, SLA-backed support

The whole stack composes into commercial PrimusNeo Engagement Bundles (see 01-pricing-model.md) — and the bundle license is what enterprise buyers actually sign.

2. Track A — Creative Commons with Commercial Restrictions

2.1 Layers under this track

  • NEOS (steward: OmniOne / Green Earth Vision)
  • Fractal Engine (steward: Escherbridge)

2.2 The license

Primary: Creative Commons Attribution-NonCommercial-ShareAlike 4.0 International (CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0) for NEOS source, schemas, and reference implementations. CC-BY-NC 4.0 for Fractal Engine (no ShareAlike on derivative models because training data may carry separate licenses).

What this permits without payment:

  • Personal, academic, and research use.
  • Non-profit community deployments (cooperatives, neighborhoods, civic associations) where the deploying entity has no revenue from the deployment.
  • DAOs and protocols below a treasury threshold (see §2.4).
  • Forks, modifications, and republication — provided attribution and the same CC-BY-NC-(SA) license terms are preserved.

What this prohibits without a commercial license:

  • Hosting the software as a paid service (SaaS, managed service, RaaS).
  • Embedding in any commercial product or platform.
  • Use by for-profit entities above the de-minimis thresholds (§2.4).
  • Use by government or public-sector entities that procure it through a paid procurement vehicle (those buy under the commercial license).

2.3 The commercial-license tier

Any of the above prohibited uses are unlocked by a PrimusNeo Commercial License (PCL) for that layer. The PCL:

  • Removes the NC restriction for the specified scope (entity, sites, user count, treasury size).
  • Adds standard commercial protections: warranty disclaimers fit for enterprise procurement, IP indemnity capped per 01-pricing-model.md §6, data-processing addendum.
  • Routes a royalty share to the layer steward (OmniOne for NEOS, Escherbridge for Fractal Engine) per the Steward Royalty Schedule in §4 below.

2.4 De-minimis thresholds (no PCL required)

A commercial entity may use the CC-NC track without a PCL only if all of the following hold:

Threshold Limit
Annual gross revenue ≤ $1M USD
Headcount ≤ 10 FTE
Connected users / members ≤ 250
Treasury (for DAO / protocol) ≤ $5M USD equivalent
No resale or service-bureau hosting required

Above any threshold, a PCL is required. This pattern mirrors the Sentry FSL free-tier-for-small-teams approach and avoids weaponizing the license against the legitimate cooperative / startup ecosystem the layers are intended to serve.

3. Track B — Closed-core / Partial-OSS

3.1 Layers under this track

  • AMP (steward: PLAN Systems) — closed-core / partial-OSS
  • AZOA (steward: AZOA) — open-source engine (Apache-2.0) with a commercial managed tier

3.2 What’s open

  • AMP: PLAN core runtime (Hexon file standard v1.0.0), client SDKs, WASM extension interface, reference 3D viewer scaffold — typically Apache-2.0 to maximize adoption in spatial-computing toolchains.
  • AZOA: The entire engine — identity & consent, quests, holons, swaps, STAR dapp generation, settlement bridges — plus the @azoa/sdk TypeScript SDK, all Apache-2.0. AZOA is designed to be self-hosted and extended: new chains plug in via ChainProvider, DEXes via DexAdapter, and new settlement rails behind the same bridge seam.

3.3 What’s closed

  • AMP: Enterprise spatial-collaboration server, multi-tenant federation, privacy-preserving mesh routing, premium PLAN 3D interface components.
  • AZOA: Nothing in the engine is closed. The commercial surface is operational: PrimusNeo-managed AZOA nodes (hosted, multi-tenant, SLA-bearing), the enterprise federation gateway service, certified settlement-rail adapters that carry commercial partner cost, and enterprise support.

3.4 The commercial license

Closed-core components ship under a PrimusNeo Enterprise License (PEL), which is a traditional commercial license:

  • Per-named-user, per-site, per-API-call, or enterprise commit (mechanic varies by layer — see layer files in layers/).
  • Source available under NDA to enterprise tier customers above a threshold (typically $250K ARR).
  • IP indemnity and warranty per 01-pricing-model.md §6.

4. Steward royalty schedule

To preserve the federated four-org arrangement and avoid the single-entity-captures-the-stack failure mode that has killed every prior attempt at this kind of stack, every PrimusNeo commercial engagement pays a Steward Royalty into a transparent treasury distributed quarterly.

Steward Layer Default royalty share of net license revenue
OmniOne / Green Earth Vision NEOS 18% of NEOS line-item license rev
Escherbridge Fractal Engine 18% of Fractal Engine line-item license rev
PLAN Systems AMP 15% of AMP line-item license rev
AZOA AZOA 15% of AZOA line-item license rev
PrimusNeo (integrator) Stack-wide Residual (~34%) plus 100% of services margin

Services revenue (customization, integration, deployment, upkeep) does not carry a steward royalty unless the work is performed by the steward themselves on subcontract, in which case it is invoiced through normally.

Infra pass-through and mgmt fee carry no royalty.

Quarterly reporting: PrimusNeo publishes (under NDA to stewards) a deal ledger reconciling royalties and pays out within 45 days of quarter close.

5. Bundle licensing

Most enterprise engagements adopt two or more layers as a bundle. The bundle ships under a single PrimusNeo Federated Engagement License (PFEL) that:

  • References the underlying PCL / PEL for each layer (so the steward royalties flow automatically).
  • Gives the buyer a single signature surface and a single counter-party (PrimusNeo) for procurement, support, and indemnity.
  • Specifies the deployment scope (entity, named subsidiaries, geographies, named user count, named site count) as a schedule that can be expanded under a Change Order without re-papering the entire contract.

The PFEL is the document procurement actually negotiates against. The underlying single-layer licenses are referenced exhibits.

6. Forking and derivatives

This is the part that determines whether the open-source ecosystem survives commercial scale-up, so it is spelled out explicitly:

  • NEOS and Fractal Engine forks are explicitly permitted and encouraged. They must retain the CC-BY-NC-(SA) license, attribute the original stewards, and may not represent themselves as “PrimusNeo NEOS / Fractal” (trademark restriction, not license restriction).
  • Commercial use of a fork requires a PCL with the steward of the original layer, not the forker. (This is the Elastic SSPL pattern: forks exist; commercial hosted forks pay.)
  • Derivative Fractal Engine models trained on a customer’s data remain the customer’s IP. Models trained on Escherbridge’s public corpus remain Escherbridge IP and are licensed back to the customer under the PCL scope.
  • AMP forks are constrained by the closed-core boundary. The open SDKs are forkable under Apache-2.0; the closed components are not. This is the standard open-core pattern (cf. GitLab CE/EE, Sentry, HashiCorp).
  • AZOA forks are unrestricted under Apache-2.0. The commercial moat is the managed service, the certified rail adapters, and the trademark — not the source.

7. Trademark

“PrimusNeo,” “AMP,” “NEOS,” “AZOA,” “Fractal Engine,” and “Tie Town” are trademarks of their respective stewards. The license grant does not include trademark rights. Compliant use of marks in compatibility statements (“works with PrimusNeo NEOS”) is permitted under standard nominative-use fair-use principles; product naming and badging is not.

8. Open questions for legal

  1. CC-BY-NC-SA vs. a custom Functional Source License (FSL) with a 2-year-delayed Apache-2.0 conversion (Sentry’s playbook). FSL gives a stronger commercial moat at the cost of slightly more legal friction. Recommendation: stay on CC-BY-NC for v0.1 (it’s a known quantity to the cooperative/research audience these layers serve); revisit FSL at v1.0 if commercial poaching becomes a real problem.
  2. Whether to define “commercial use” by revenue (clean, easily verified) or by purpose (closer to the CC NC spirit, but ambiguous). Recommendation: revenue + headcount + connected-users, per §2.4.
  3. Whether enterprise customers can request escrow of closed-core AMP source for business-continuity purposes. Recommendation: yes, above $500K ARR threshold, through a standard third-party escrow agent (e.g., Iron Mountain). AZOA needs no escrow — the engine is Apache-2.0.
  4. Whether DAO treasuries denominated in volatile assets are valued at acquisition cost or marked-to-market for the §2.4 threshold. Recommendation: trailing-90-day VWAP; documented in the PCL itself.

9. Revisions

Version Date Author Change
0.1 2026-05-23 Founding team Initial dual-track structure
0.2 2026-07-15 Founding team OASIS → AZOA rename; AZOA moved to an Apache-2.0 open-source + managed-service track

PrimusNeo Services Catalog

Status: Draft v0.1. Scope: Defines the five service classes, SKUs, day rates, and bundling rules that wrap PrimusNeo license / subscription revenue.

1. Why services matter (and how they relate to license)

Across our four layer comparables (Palantir, Cognite, Aveva, AspenTech, Innovaccer, Procore, MuleSoft, Snowflake), the services-to-license ratio in year one ranges from 0.5x to 5x. PrimusNeo’s structural target is 1.5x – 2.5x in year one, taping to 0.3x – 0.6x by year three as the customer’s internal team takes over. Anything outside that band is a signal to revisit the engagement design.

Year 1:  License $1.00  ::  Services $1.50–$2.50
Year 2:  License $1.00  ::  Services $0.80–$1.20
Year 3+: License $1.00  ::  Services $0.30–$0.60   ← steady-state upkeep only

Services are sold under the same PrimusNeo Federated Engagement License (PFEL) as the bundle, with a Statement of Work (SOW) per engagement.

2. The five service classes

2.1 Customization

What it covers: Vertical-specific schemas, council templates, twin extensions, custom Fractal Engine models, NEOS charters, AMP scene authoring, AZOA connector buildouts, WASM extension development.

Pricing mechanic: T&M day rate OR fixed-fee scope after a paid discovery sprint.

Day rates (standard):

Role Day rate (USD) Notes
Principal Architect $3,200 Founding team, capped allocation per engagement
Senior Engineer (any layer) $2,200 Federated steward orgs share this rate
Engineer $1,600
Fractal Engine ML Engineer $2,800 Specialty: Escherbridge talent
NEOS Governance Designer $2,400 Specialty: OmniOne ETHOS methodology
AMP Spatial / 3D Engineer $2,400 Specialty: PLAN systems
AZOA Integration Engineer $2,200 Specialty: AZOA connector authors
Project Manager $1,800
Trainer / Change Management $1,400

Day rates are floors. Premium up to +50% for: sub-30-day rush, hostile deadlines, on-site travel >50%, regulated-industry compliance scope (FedRAMP High, HIPAA, NERC CIP, etc.), and weekend cutover windows.

Discovery sprint: 2-week paid engagement, $40–80K depending on scope. Output: a fixed-fee SOW the buyer can sign. Discovery fee credits 100% against the resulting engagement if signed within 90 days.

2.2 Integration

What it covers: Net-new connectors and bridges between PrimusNeo and the buyer’s existing stack. The most common categories:

Connector class Typical examples Default fixed fee (USD)
ERP SAP S/4HANA, Oracle Fusion, NetSuite, Microsoft D365 $60K – $180K
MES / OT Rockwell FactoryTalk, Siemens MindSphere, Aveva, GE Proficy, OPC UA $80K – $250K
EHR / Clinical Epic, Cerner/Oracle Health, Meditech, Athenahealth $120K – $400K (HITRUST overhead)
TMS / WMS Manhattan, Blue Yonder, Oracle OTM, SAP TM $60K – $200K
CRM Salesforce, HubSpot, MS Dynamics CE $30K – $80K
Identity / IdP Okta, Azure AD, Auth0, Ping, SSI Verified Credentials $25K – $90K
Chain / RPC EVM, Solana, Cosmos, Hedera, Polkadot, custom L2 $20K – $75K per chain
Storage / data engine SurrealDB (AZOA’s sole data engine) provisioning + tuning; optional S3-compatible bucket offload $15K – $40K
BIM / CAD Autodesk ACC, Bentley iTwin, Trimble Connect $80K – $250K
Custom REST / GraphQL bespoke buyer system $20K – $120K

Mechanic: Fixed-fee per connector with a 90-day warranty period. Discount tiers for connector bundles (3 connectors → 10% off, 5+ → 15% off).

Connector reuse: PrimusNeo retains the right to harden, generalize, and redistribute any non-bespoke connector through the AZOA connector library. Bespoke customer logic is held back per the SOW.

2.3 Deployment

What it covers: Reference-architecture rollout, environment provisioning, data migration, cutover, training, change management.

SKUs:

SKU Scope Fixed fee (USD) Duration
Atom Single-site, single-layer pilot, ≤25 users, dev + prod environments $35K – $90K 4–8 weeks
Molecule Single-site, full-stack (all 4 layers), ≤100 users $120K – $320K 8–14 weeks
Hex Multi-site (2–5 sites), full-stack, ≤500 users, federated identity $400K – $900K 14–24 weeks
Lattice Enterprise (6+ sites or 500+ users), full-stack, global federation, change-management program $1.0M – $3.5M 24–48 weeks
Tessellation Industry-program scale (national rollout, regulated sector, multi-tenant inside one enterprise) $3.5M – $12M+ 9–18 months

Each SKU includes:

  • Environment provisioning (dev / staging / prod / DR).
  • Reference data migration (up to a stated volume).
  • Cutover plan and rehearsal.
  • Operator training (standard hours per scope).
  • 30-day hypercare post-cutover.

Excludes (priced separately): integration SOW, custom development, infra pass-through, ongoing upkeep.

2.4 Sustained Upkeep

What it covers: Tier-1/2/3 support, version upgrades, security patching, AZOA connector maintenance, Fractal model retraining cadence, NEOS charter evolution, AMP scene/world upgrades, on-call.

Pricing mechanic: Annual % of license value, 18–25% depending on SLA tier. This is the industry default for enterprise software (Oracle 22%, SAP 22%, Microsoft 20%, AspenTech ~20%, Snowflake bundled).

SLA tiers:

Tier Coverage Annual % of license Notes
Bronze Business hours (your tz), 1 business-day P1 response 18% Most CC-NC entities
Silver 24×5, 4-hour P1 response, named TAM, quarterly review 22% Default for commercial mid-market
Gold 24×7, 1-hour P1 response, named TAM + on-call rotation, monthly review 25% Default for regulated / enterprise
Platinum 24×7, 15-min P1, dedicated support pod, weekly review, custom escrow Quoted Bank, hospital system, ISO, federal

Inclusions across all tiers:

  • Security patches and CVE response per SLA.
  • Minor and patch version upgrades.
  • Up to two major version upgrades over a 3-year MSA.
  • Up to N hours/quarter of Tier-3 engineering time per the tier (4 / 12 / 24 / 48 respectively).

Outside inclusions (T&M at standard rates): schema migrations, governance redesign, major customization changes, integration to new external systems.

2.5 Infra (pass-through + management fee)

What it covers: Cloud (AWS / Azure / GCP), edge compute, storage tiers, observability, chain gas, optional Tie Town reference colocation.

Pricing mechanic: Pass-through at cost + 10–18% management fee. Lower fee for transparent direct-pay arrangements where the customer owns the cloud account; higher fee for fully-managed PrimusNeo-owned-and-billed.

Standard infra SKUs (Tie Town reference):

SKU Description Typical monthly cost (USD)
Edge node (Tie Town) 1U colocated, AMP + AZOA edge cache, 99.95% target $1,200 – $2,500
Cloud reference (small) t3-equivalent, 4 vCPU, 16GB, 500GB SSD, 2TB egress $800 – $1,500
Cloud reference (mid) 16 vCPU, 64GB, 2TB SSD, 10TB egress, multi-AZ $4,500 – $9,000
Cloud reference (large) 64+ vCPU, 256GB+, 10TB+ SSD, dedicated K8s nodes, multi-region $18K – $45K
Chain gas reserve Estimated monthly burn for governance txs + signature anchoring Variable; quote per chain
Storage tier (Fractal archive) Cold + warm Fractal historical archive (S3 + Glacier or equivalent) $0.012 – $0.025 per GB-month

3. Bundling rules

  • License + Deployment + Upkeep is the minimum viable bundle. Selling license without Deployment is allowed but quoted with a “no-cutover warranty” clause that explicitly disclaims rollout risk.
  • Customization is sold only after Discovery. Discovery is the only T&M sale we will do without a fixed-fee follow-on signed within 90 days, and even then the fee credits back.
  • Integration is bundled at a discount when 3+ connectors are signed in the same SOW (see §2.2 discount tiers).
  • Infra is never bundled into license for accounting cleanliness and customer trust. Always a separate line item.

4. Margin targets

Service class Target gross margin
Customization 55–65% (founders 70%+)
Integration (fixed-fee) 50–60%
Deployment (fixed-fee) 45–55%
Sustained Upkeep 75–85% (the gold mine)
Infra mgmt fee 85%+ (the fee is mostly margin; cost is mostly pass-through)

License gross margin runs 80–92% per layer per the comparables in research/layer-comparables.md.

5. Revisions

Version Date Author Change
0.1 2026-05-23 Founding team Initial catalog
0.2 2026-07-15 Founding team OASIS → AZOA rename; storage services re-scoped to SurrealDB + optional S3-compatible bucket

PrimusNeo Entry Pricing — Community Tiers & Paid Pilots

Status: Draft v0.1 Dependencies: 00-overview.md, 01-pricing-model.md, 02-licensing-model.md, 03-services-catalog.md

1. The gap this closes

The core model (01-pricing-model.md) prices Small/Mid/Large enterprise engagements. The cheapest commercial path through it today is roughly $75K–$170K in year one (Small-tier license + Discovery sprint + Atom deployment). That is correct for enterprise — and a wall for everyone else:

  • Community organizations (cooperatives, civic associations, DAOs) that the CC-NC track exists to serve, who need hosting and guidance, not a PFEL.
  • SMBs above the de-minimis thresholds (02 §2.4) but nowhere near a six-figure Y1.
  • Enterprise champions who need a sub-procurement-threshold paid pilot to manufacture the internal evidence before a committee will convene.

This document defines the on-ramp: published, flat, value-anchored entry tiers that ladder into the standard anchors — and a paid-pilot motion designed to kill pilot purgatory rather than feed it.

2. The on-ramp at a glance

Rung Name Who it’s for Price Duration Converts to
0 Community Edition (self-host) Anyone under de-minimis (02 §2.4) $0 — Community Cloud
1 Community Cloud (hosted) Co-ops, civic orgs, DAOs under de-minimis $149–$399/mo Monthly, cancel anytime Small-tier PCL on graduation
2 Community Pilot Community orgs wanting a guided launch $4K–$9K fixed 6 weeks Community Cloud (100% credit)
3 Proof Pilot Commercial SMB / enterprise champion $18K–$45K fixed 6–10 weeks Small-tier engagement (100% credit)
4 Production Pilot Regulated / complex verticals $60K–$120K fixed 10–14 weeks Small/Mid engagement (100% credit)

Above Rung 4, the standard model applies: Discovery sprint → PFEL bundle per 01 §4 and 03.

3. The six rules

  1. Every pilot is paid. Payment is the commitment signal; unpaid POCs are how the McKinsey 70% die. The free experience is Community Edition self-hosting — never a free pilot.
  2. Entry prices are published and flat. No negotiation, no discount ladder (01 §7 does not apply below Small tier). Flat list price is what makes the entry cheap to sell and easy to buy.
  3. Still value-based. The flat tiers are pre-clamped outputs of the same formula: a pilot fee targets 1–2% of the vertical’s annualized friction-cost anchor at the buyer’s scale, clamped to the rung band. Community Cloud is priced at cost floor + thin margin on pooled infra.
  4. The pilot manufactures the evidence. Every commercial pilot includes Fractal Engine baseline instrumentation. The pilot’s output — the Value Evidence Report — becomes the FRICTION_COST input to the full quote. The pilot doesn’t precede the value-based sale; it is the measurement phase of it.
  5. Credit-forward, services-first. 100% of the pilot fee credits against the Y1 engagement if signed within 90 days of pilot end. Credit applies to the Y1 services line first, then license — protecting the steward royalty base (02 §4).
  6. COST_FLOOR still binds. Thin entry margins come from the standardized Pilot Kit (the Tie Town reference architecture in a box: pre-built vertical templates, library connectors only, pooled reference infra) — never from waiving the floor.

4. Rung detail

4.0 Community Edition (self-host) — $0

What already exists under the licensing model, named as a product tier: NEOS + Fractal Engine community builds under CC-NC (within the de-minimis thresholds of 02 §2.4), plus the open AMP community runtime and the full AZOA engine (Apache-2.0). Community forum support only. This is the top of the funnel and the health of the ecosystem — it is never crippled to force upgrades.

4.1 Community Cloud — $149–$399/mo

Hosted NEOS + Fractal community stack on pooled multi-tenant reference infrastructure (which is what lets the price clear COST_FLOOR — a dedicated Cloud-reference-small runs $800–$1,500/mo per 03 §2.5).

Plan Members Price Includes
Seed ≤ 50 $149/mo 1 council, community dashboard, 30-day Fractal retention
Grove 51–250 $349/mo Multi-council, 12-month retention, AMP community space
+Add-on any +$50/mo Custom domain + data-export automation
  • Eligibility = the de-minimis thresholds. Crossing them triggers graduation (§7), not a cutoff.
  • Support: community forum + best-effort email (below Bronze; no SLA).
  • Consideration: opt-in public reference listing earns 2 free months/yr.
  • Annual prepay = 2 months free (matches the 5%+ prepay norm, simpler math).

4.2 Community Pilot — $4K–$9K fixed, 6 weeks

A guided community launch for co-ops, civic orgs, and DAOs that want hands-on help: NEOS charter workshop (ETHOS-lite), one council live with real members, Fractal community dashboard, three facilitated sessions, launch checklist.

  • Delivered from the Pilot Kit in 3–5 trainer/governance-designer days.
  • 100% of the fee credits to 12 months of Community Cloud or to any commercial engagement signed within 90 days.
  • Capped at 4 per quarter delivered internally until Community Partners (§7) are certified to deliver at wholesale.

4.3 Proof Pilot — $18K–$45K fixed, 6–10 weeks

The commercial entry rung. Single site, 1–2 layers, ≤25 users, at most one library connector (no bespoke integration), pooled reference infra or the buyer’s cloud.

Includes, always:

  1. Success-criteria contract — 3–5 metrics fixed in week 1, signed by a named executive sponsor.
  2. Baseline instrumentation — Fractal Engine measures the friction being displaced from day one. This satisfies the 60-day instrumentation phase required for the outcome-based addendum (01 §8), so a converted engagement is gainshare-ready immediately.
  3. Value Evidence Report — the measured friction cost, sourced and presented in the buyer’s numbers, delivered whether or not they convert.
  4. Pre-priced conversion quote — built with the 01 §1 formula from the measured baseline, delivered by ~week 6 so the buyer knows the full price before the pilot ends.

Fee heuristic: clamp(1.5% × measured-or-estimated annual friction, $18K, $45K). The band deliberately sits under single-budget-holder signature authority in most mid-market orgs.

4.4 Production Pilot — $60K–$120K fixed, 10–14 weeks

For verticals where compliance scope makes a Proof Pilot impossible (healthcare, energy, finance, public governance). Everything in the Proof Pilot, plus: one full catalog connector (03 §2.2), a compliance documentation pack (HIPAA/HITRUST, NERC CIP, SOC 2 evidence as applicable), Gold-grade support during the pilot term, and limited-production scope under the Pilot License Rider (§5).

Positioning: a Production Pilot is the Discovery sprint + Atom deployment fused into one fixed-fee, value-instrumented package ($60–$120K vs. $75–$170K bought separately) — with the credit-forward mechanic on top.

Per-vertical entry defaults

Vertical Default entry rung Typical pilot fee
Manufacturing Proof Pilot $25K–$45K
Logistics Proof Pilot $25K–$45K
Robotics Proof Pilot $30K–$45K
Construction Proof Pilot $25K–$40K
Agriculture Proof Pilot (co-ops: Community Pilot first) $18K–$28K
Technology Proof Pilot $18K–$35K
Healthcare Production Pilot $90K–$120K
Energy Production Pilot $80K–$120K
Finance Production Pilot $70K–$110K
Governance Public sector: Production $60K–$100K · DAO: Community/Proof $6K–$30K (DAO)

5. Pilot mechanics

SOW skeleton (Proof Pilot, 8 weeks):

Week Milestone
0 Kickoff; success criteria signed; conversion decision meeting calendar-blocked for week 9
1–2 Pilot Kit deployment; baseline instrumentation live
3–5 Layer(s) in daily use by pilot cohort; weekly metric readout
6 Value Evidence Report (interim) + pre-priced conversion quote delivered
7–8 Hardening, final measurement window
9 Conversion decision meeting: convert, stop, or extend

Anti-purgatory guardrails:

  • No pilot without a named executive sponsor and a stated conversion budget range. No sponsor, no pilot.
  • One extension maximum: +4 weeks at 25% of the pilot fee. There is no second extension — the pilot converts or ends. Data exports in full either way (the no-lock-in promise applies at every rung).
  • Payment: 50% at signature, 50% at week-4 milestone. Card/ACH accepted at Rungs 1–3; no net-30 negotiation below Rung 4.

Pilot License Rider (PLR): pilots ship under a time-boxed rider — named scope, pilot term + 30 days, auto-expiring, non-renewable (Production Pilots get limited-production scope). For CC-NC layers this simply papers the hosted service; for AMP it grants the closed-core components within the “non-commercial pilot scope” already contemplated by 02 §1; for AZOA (Apache-2.0 engine) it papers only the managed hosting and support. Conversion replaces the PLR with the standard PCL/PEL/PFEL.

6. Conversion math — worked example

Mid-size precision-machining plant (~$60M revenue, 2 lines). Proof Pilot on Fractal Engine + AZOA (MES library connector), fee clamp(1.5% × est. $1.8M friction, 18K, 45K) = $27K, 8 weeks.

Pilot baseline measures $1.8M/yr of displaceable friction (unplanned downtime $1.1M, scrap/rework traceability $0.4M, coordination rework $0.3M). Conversion quote per the 01 §1 formula:

FRICTION_COST:  $1.8M/yr   (measured, not estimated — pilot output)
VALUE_SHARE:    10% → $180K/yr    (clean attribution via pilot baseline)
Quote band:     [max(COST_FLOOR, $90K), $180K]

License:   Fractal Small $75K + AZOA Small $48K = $123K − 10% Duet = ~$111K  ✓ in band
Services:  deployment hardening + 2nd connector $110K − $27K pilot credit = $83K
Upkeep:    Silver 22% × $111K ≈ $24K
Infra:     buyer cloud, pass-through + 12% ≈ $12K
                                    ─────
Y1 TOTAL:  ~$230K   (vs. ~$75K–$170K just to *start* without the on-ramp,
                     and vs. $1.8M/yr measured friction)

Even at a conservative 40% friction removal ($720K/yr), the buyer nets ~$490K in year one — a quote the sponsor defends with their own plant’s numbers, because the pilot produced them.

7. The community flywheel

The community tiers are not a loss leader; they are the reference network. Target: 2–3 community lighthouses per vertical, each playing the Tie Town role for its niche.

  • Graduation, not eviction. A community entity crossing the de-minimis thresholds gets its first PCL year at 50% of the Small anchor (one-time), then standard rates. Growth is celebrated, never punished — this is what keeps the CC-NC ecosystem loyal at commercial scale.
  • Contribution credits. Accepted contributions (NEOS council templates, AZOA rail adapters — ChainProvider / DexAdapter, Fractal schemas) earn $500–$2,500 in Community Cloud credit per acceptance, subject to steward review. Contributors fund their own hosting by making the stack better.
  • Community Partners. Certified co-op developers and civic-tech shops deliver Community Pilots at 70% of list (wholesale), scaling the motion beyond internal capacity and seeding a delivery ecosystem.
  • Reference rights. Opt-in only, rewarded (§4.1) — never coerced.

8. Positioning against Discovery and Atom

Situation Right motion
Mid/Large enterprise, formal procurement, budget exists Discovery sprint (03 §2.1) — do not sell a Proof Pilot; the band under-prices it
Regulated vertical, compliance gates entry Production Pilot (Discovery + Atom fused)
SMB or enterprise champion pre-committee Proof Pilot
Community org under de-minimis Community Pilot / Community Cloud

The rungs are qualification tiers, not a menu — selling down-rung to a qualified enterprise buyer destroys value; selling up-rung to an unqualified one destroys the funnel.

9. Internal economics & guardrails

Rung Target gross margin Note
Community Cloud ≥ 20% over pooled infra Self-sustaining, not profit center
Community Pilot ≥ 25% Ecosystem seeding; capacity-capped
Proof Pilot ≥ 40% Pilot Kit standardization is the margin
Production Pilot ≥ 45% Aligns with Deployment margins (03 §4)
  • Royalties: pilot fees are services revenue — no steward royalty per 02 §4. Community Cloud’s license component carries the standard schedule on its NEOS/Fractal split. The services-first credit rule (§3, rule 5) exists to protect the royalty base on conversion.
  • Capacity: per delivery pod per quarter — 6 Proof + 2 Production pilots; 4 Community Pilots internally (unlimited via Community Partners).
  • Funnel health: Proof Pilot → paid conversion target ≥ 40% within 90 days. Below 25% for two consecutive quarters means the Kit or the qualification gate is broken — fix before scaling pilot volume.
  • No discounting: entry prices are list. The 01 §7 discount authority does not extend below Small tier; a request for a pilot discount is a qualification failure, not a negotiation.

10. Revisions

Version Date Author Change
0.1 2026-07-15 Founding team Initial entry-pricing on-ramp: community tiers + paid-pilot ladder

AMP — Spatial OS + Operational Access Management Engagement Model

Layer: 01 — Operational Runtime / Spatial OS / Operational Access Management Steward: PLAN Systems License: Closed-core / partial-OSS (community runtime open Apache-2.0; enterprise spatial + multi-tenant federation + OAM kernel closed) Maturity: The most mature layer in the stack — multi-year hardening through PLAN Systems plus the live Tie Town [TX] deployment Reference deployment: Tie Town [TX] Anchors source: ../research/layer-comparables.md

1. What AMP actually is (in the buyer’s vocabulary)

AMP is the operational ground layer — and it does two jobs in one runtime, which is why it is the highest-priced layer in the PrimusNeo stack:

  1. Spatial OS. The privacy-first spatial collaboration runtime that ties together physical sites, devices, people, and the contextualized data layer that makes everything above it (NEOS governance decisions, AZOA interop events, Fractal Engine signals) attach to a place and a moment.
  2. Operational Access Management (OAM). The runtime that decides — for every action, every device, every spatial zone, every shift — who is allowed to do what, here, now. AMP issues, scopes, revokes, and audits operational access against the same spatial scene the work actually happens in. It is the layer that replaces the patchwork of physical badges + OT VPNs + plant-floor permission spreadsheets + drone-clearance forms + facility access lists most operations run today.

This dual role — spatial OS and OAM in one runtime — is what makes AMP the load-bearing layer in any operational engagement, and it is why AMP commands the highest license and services anchors in the stack.

In the language enterprises already know:

  • A plant floor to a manufacturing CIO: AMP is the IT/OT-bridging spatial model that combines CAD, PLC telemetry, MES events, and worker location into one navigable scene — and the lockout/tagout + MOC + shift-handoff access system that decides who can touch which line at which moment.
  • A hospital to a healthcare CMIO: AMP is the OR/ED command center spatial layer (a GE Command Center analog without forcing Epic rip-and-replace) and the role-and-zone-based access system gating who scrubs into which suite, who reads which chart from which workstation, and who can override which alarm.
  • A port to a CSCO: AMP is the terminal-and-yard spatial twin (Navis N4-adjacent but vendor-neutral) and the operational access system that decides which driver, which crane operator, which inspector, which carrier rep is authorized in which apron zone for which time window.
  • An energy utility to a CGO: AMP is the substation + plant spatial twin and the NERC CIP-013-equivalent operational access kernel that gates physical and logical access to BES Cyber Assets with cryptographic audit trail.
  • A DAO or cooperative to a community steward: AMP is the offline-capable shared space where members coordinate and the contribution-based access system that determines who can sign for the treasury, who can deploy code, who can propose a charter change.

Crucially, AMP runs offline-first in both roles. The OAM kernel issues and validates access locally even when disconnected; access decisions reconcile to the federated audit log when the link comes back. This is what distinguishes AMP from every Web-native spatial collaboration tool (Spatial.io, Microsoft Mesh, Meta Workplace) and from every cloud-only IAM/PAM product (Okta, CyberArk, BeyondTrust), and it is the requirement set for regulated, low-connectivity, or sovereignty-conscious environments.

2. License posture

Closed-core / partial-OSS — the runtime, file standard, and client SDKs are open; the enterprise spatial server, multi-tenant federation layer, and the OAM kernel are closed.

Component License Notes
PLAN core runtime Apache-2.0 Community-maintained; forkable
Hexon (v1.0.0) file standard Apache-2.0 The portable spatial file format
Client SDKs (web, mobile, desktop) Apache-2.0 Free for any use
WASM extension interface Apache-2.0 Free; extensions can be commercial or OSS at extension author’s choice
Reference 3D viewer scaffold Apache-2.0 Free baseline UI
Community OAM policy DSL Apache-2.0 The open policy language; closed runtime evaluates it
Enterprise spatial server Closed (PEL) Multi-tenant; production SLA; required for >5 sites or >50 concurrent
Federation engine Closed (PEL) Cross-org spatial federation; required for multi-entity deployments
OAM kernel Closed (PEL) The operational access management kernel — issuance, scoping, revocation, cryptographic audit trail, offline policy evaluation. The second pillar of AMP’s value
Premium PLAN 3D interface components Closed (PEL) Industrial-grade UI; CAD-class viewport; OEM-licensed assets
Privacy-preserving mesh routing Closed (PEL) The privacy-first networking that makes “offline-capable spatial collaboration” actually work without leaking position data

See ../02-licensing-model.md §3 for the closed-core / PEL mechanics.

3. Pricing tiers (annual license, before services)

AMP carries the highest license anchors in the stack because (a) it is the most mature layer (years of PLAN Systems development plus a live Tie Town deployment), and (b) it is the only layer that delivers two enterprise-grade capabilities — Spatial OS and Operational Access Management — in one runtime. From the layer anchors in ../01-pricing-model.md §3.1:

Scale Definition Annual License Services Multiplier (Y1) Typical 3-yr TCO
Small 1–3 sites, <50 concurrent spatial users, <200 OAM identities $30K–$75K/yr 1.5–2.5x $150K–$370K
Mid 4–15 sites, 50–500 concurrent, 200–2,500 OAM identities $150K–$450K/yr 1.5–2.5x $850K–$2.5M
Large 15+ sites or multi-org federation, 500+ concurrent, 2,500+ OAM identities $600K–$2.5M/yr 1.5–2.5x $3.0M–$12M

Pricing mechanic options (choose one per engagement; default in bold):

  • Per-site + per-OAM-identity (default for industrial / multi-facility): $6K–$45K/site/yr plus $36–$180/OAM-identity/yr (volume tiered)
  • Per-concurrent-user license: $200–$1,000/concurrent-user/yr (for office/collab-heavy deployments)
  • Per-spatial-asset license (Hexon files under management): $0.75–$4/asset/yr at scale
  • Enterprise unlimited (Large tier only): flat annual commit

OAM-only entry SKU: Buyers who want the OAM kernel without the full spatial OS surface area can adopt AMP as a pure operational-access-management product at 70% of the Spatial OS line-item. This is the recommended motion for buyers (e.g., regulated FS, defense-adjacent, NERC CIP-driven utilities) where the access-management value lands before the spatial twin value.

4. Services mix

AMP is service-intensive in Y1. The typical Mid-tier full-deployment SOW:

Service Scope Fixed-fee range
Discovery sprint Site walks, scene scoping, integration inventory, success metrics $40K–$80K (credits to engagement)
Spatial environment buildout CAD ingest, scene authoring, scale calibration, asset library setup $80K–$300K per site
Sensor / OT integration Live telemetry binding (PLCs via OPC UA, sensors via MQTT, location tracking) $40K–$180K per site
Premium 3D component installation Industrial-grade viewers, CAD-class viewport, premium UI library $25K–$60K bundled
Operator training + change management Hands-on for plant ops, dispatchers, facility managers $30K–$120K per site
Federation enablement (Large only) Cross-org spatial federation, identity bridging to NEOS + AZOA $150K–$500K one-time
30-day hypercare Post-cutover stabilization Included in deployment SKU

Services delivered by a mix of PrimusNeo, PLAN Systems contractors, and certified spatial-integration partners.

5. Sustained upkeep + infra

Annual sustained upkeep = 18–25% of license, per ../03-services-catalog.md §2.4. Tier choice (Bronze/Silver/Gold/Platinum) drives the percentage and the SLA.

Infra typical components (pass-through + 12% mgmt fee):

  • Spatial server hosting (per-site edge or regional cloud): $300–$2,500/mo/site
  • Hexon archive storage (S3-equivalent + Glacier cold tier): $0.012–$0.025/GB-month
  • Sensor/IoT ingest bandwidth: variable; typically $200–$1,500/mo/site
  • Optional Tie Town reference colocation: $1,200–$2,500/mo for 1U edge node

6. Bundle interactions

AMP is the anchor layer for any operational engagement. It composes most powerfully with:

Other layer What you unlock together
+ Fractal Engine Spatial events become first-class signals; predictive maintenance, OEE, capacity forecasting attach to scene positions. Standard duo for manufacturing, energy, healthcare ops, logistics.
+ AZOA Connects the spatial scene to ERP/MES/EHR/TMS/etc. without forcing rip-and-replace of any incumbent. Required duo for buyers with substantial legacy IT/OT stacks.
+ NEOS Spatial actions become governed actions — “this approval was issued from this location on this asset by this signed identity.” Required duo for regulated industries (healthcare HITRUST, energy NERC CIP, finance MiCA).
Full Stack The federated coordination fabric. Triad → Full Stack upsell is the dominant Y2 expansion motion.

7. Two worked engagement examples

7.1 Small AMP-only: 60-acre regenerative ag co-op (Tier-1 Small)

  • Buyer: Co-op board of a 12-member regenerative cooperative running 60 acres in Vermont
  • Scope: Single farm site, 8 concurrent spatial users (board + members during planning windows), ~120 OAM identities (members + seasonal workers + visiting agronomists), offline-capable, BYO connectivity
  • Quote: $42K/yr AMP license (Small tier midpoint — spatial OS + OAM kernel) · $60K Atom deployment SKU · $8K/yr Bronze SLA · ~$400/mo edge node hosting
  • Y1 total: ~$115K · 3-yr TCO: ~$235K
  • Friction-cost anchor: Cooperative spending $40K/yr on ad-hoc tools (Google Drive, Loomio, paper records, satellite imagery) plus an estimated $25K/yr in informal access management (member onboarding, key handoffs, contractor approvals, shift handoffs) — combined $65K/yr friction
  • Why they sign: Member sovereignty (offline, self-hosted), Hexon file portability (no lock-in), unified OAM removing the patchwork of paper sign-in sheets + Slack channels, Tie Town reference proves the stack works

7.2 Large AMP+Fractal+AZOA: 12-plant discrete manufacturer (Tier-3 Large)

  • Buyer: CIO + COO of a $3B discrete manufacturer with 12 plants across NA + EU
  • Scope: All 12 plants on AMP spatial twin + AMP OAM (~4,800 OAM identities: operators + maintenance + contractors + EHS + management), federated; AZOA connectors to SAP S/4HANA, Rockwell FactoryTalk MES, AVEVA PI System; Fractal Engine OEE + downtime prediction
  • Quote:
  • AMP Large: $1.5M/yr (12 sites × ~$95K/site for combined spatial-OS + OAM; well below the $2.5M ceiling but above the floor given identity count and federation scope)
  • AZOA Large: $550K/yr (50+ integrations, ERP + MES + historian)
  • Fractal Mid (high end): $300K/yr
  • Triad bundle discount: -18% → $1.93M/yr license
  • Y1 services (~2.2x license blend): $4.25M
  • Y1 upkeep (Gold SLA, 25%): $483K (starts Y2)
  • Infra pass-through + 12% mgmt: $400K–$700K/yr
  • Y1 total: ~$6.6M–$7.0M · 3-yr TCO: ~$13M–$15M
  • Friction-cost anchor: McKinsey pilot-purgatory 70% on 8 stalled IIoT POCs = $12M sunk + $50M/yr unplanned downtime (Siemens benchmark) + ~$3–6M/yr across legacy IAM/PAM stack and lockout-tagout/MOC paperwork that AMP OAM displaces → capture window of ~$25M/yr in friction
  • Capture rate: ~$1.93M ÷ $25M = 7.7% value share — defensible inside the 5–15% manufacturing capture band, with the OAM displacement value (Okta + CyberArk equivalents at ~$200–$400K/yr for a workforce this size) underwriting the AMP premium

8. Steward economics

Per ../02-licensing-model.md §4:

  • PLAN Systems royalty: 15% of AMP line-item license revenue, paid quarterly
  • Services revenue carries no royalty unless PLAN delivers the work on subcontract
  • AMP is the highest-services-multiplier layer; PLAN benefits indirectly when they subcontract services delivery
  • Tie Town deployment serves as the reference architecture both stewards benefit from

9. Risks specific to AMP

Risk Mitigation
Vendor lock-in fear Hexon v1.0.0 is open; client SDKs are open; data export is non-negotiable contract term; OAM policy DSL is Apache-2.0
Spatial competitors (Omniverse, Unity Industrial) priced lower per-seat Position on operational + offline + federated + OAM-bundled characteristics they don’t deliver; per-seat math is irrelevant when AMP replaces two product categories at once
“Why is AMP more expensive than Fractal/AZOA?” Because it ships two production-grade capabilities — Spatial OS and Operational Access Management — in one runtime, and it is the most mature layer with multi-year Tie Town hardening. Compare against Spatial vendor + IAM/PAM vendor combined cost (typically $4,500/GPU/yr Omniverse + $5–$15/user/mo Okta + $50–$150/user/yr CyberArk-class PAM); AMP undercuts the sum
IAM/PAM incumbent displacement (Okta, CyberArk, BeyondTrust) AMP OAM is positioned as operational access management — bound to spatial zones, time windows, OT assets, and physical-world context. It complements rather than fully displaces workforce IdP/IAM for office IT; the AZOA layer handles bridging if both are needed
Talent shortage (spatial-OT integration) PLAN partner channel + certified integrator network; managed deployment service
Performance at scale (very large plants, complex CAD) Edge-first architecture; Hexon designed for streaming partial loads; reference deployments demonstrate the envelope
“We already have a digital twin” Most “digital twins” are PowerBI dashboards over historian data — not actual spatial-event-graph systems. Discovery sprint shows the gap, and the OAM kernel is a second-vector value-add the dashboard tools cannot match

NEOS — Governance Engagement Model

Layer: 02 — Agreements & Permissions / Governance Steward: OmniOne / Green Earth Vision License: Creative Commons BY-NC-SA 4.0 (non-commercial use free) + PrimusNeo Commercial License (PCL) for any commercial use Reference deployment: Tie Town [TX] governance councils + Aave/Lido benchmark adjacencies Anchors source: ../research/layer-comparables.md

1. What NEOS actually is (in the buyer’s vocabulary)

NEOS is the durable coordination + decision-rights layer. It is where councils form, charters are written, contributions are accounted, holonic organizations nest inside larger federations, and approvals become signed, auditable, replayable events.

In the language enterprises already know:

  • A bank or asset manager to a CRO: NEOS is the programmable approval-and-attestation fabric that produces the immutable governance logs MiCA, OCC Letter 1184, and SAB 122 now make a procurement requirement.
  • A hospital system to a CCO: NEOS is the consent + IRB + change-control governance layer that satisfies HIPAA, HITRUST i1/r2, and increasingly EHDS.
  • A state agency to a CIO: NEOS is the inter-departmental decision-rights system that bridges the gap between Tyler/Granicus civic platforms and the participatory democracy world (Decidim, Pol.is).
  • A DAO to a Steward Council: NEOS is the durable governance layer above Snapshot signal + Tally execution — the part that codifies the intent and the accountability, not just the vote.
  • A cooperative to a member board: NEOS is the contribution accounting + holonic structure system that lets a 50-person cooperative federate with 50 others without losing local sovereignty.

NEOS is governance at the protocol layer, not at the SaaS-board-portal layer. That difference is what justifies pricing above Loomio (which is a discussion tool) and at parity with Diligent (which is a board portal but with no on-chain auditability or contribution accounting).

2. License posture — Creative Commons with Commercial Restrictions

This is the layer that most directly tests the CC-NC + commercial-license model. Per ../02-licensing-model.md §2:

Use License Cost
Personal / academic / research CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0 Free
Nonprofit community deployment (co-op, neighborhood association, civic group) with no revenue from the deployment CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0 Free
DAO / protocol with treasury ≤ $5M USD-equiv CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0 Free
Small commercial entity meeting all de-minimis thresholds (≤$1M revenue, ≤10 FTE, ≤250 connected members, no resale) CC-BY-NC-SA 4.0 Free
Above any de-minimis threshold PrimusNeo Commercial License (PCL) See tiered pricing below
Hosting NEOS as a paid service / managed service / SaaS PCL required Tiered
Embedding in any commercial product or platform PCL required Tiered
Government / public-sector entity procuring through paid procurement vehicle PCL required Tiered

Forks are permitted; forks may not market themselves as “PrimusNeo NEOS” (trademark restriction); commercial use of a fork still requires a PCL with OmniOne / PrimusNeo.

3. Pricing tiers (annual commercial license, before services)

Scale Definition Annual License Services Multiplier (Y1) Typical 3-yr TCO
Small Single org, 1 council, <50 members $6K–$18K/yr 0.5–1x $25K–$80K
Mid Multi-council, 50–500 members, charter management $30K–$90K/yr 0.5–1x $130K–$420K
Large Federated governance, 500+ members, multiple orgs $120K–$400K/yr 0.5–1x $530K–$1.7M

Pricing mechanic options (default in bold):

  • Per-council license (default for orgs adopting governance progressively): $1.5K–$8K/council/yr
  • Per-member license (for participatory democracy / DAO contexts): $20–$120/member/yr (scales down at volume)
  • Per-covered-life (healthcare consent, civic resident contexts): $0.10–$1/covered-life/yr
  • % of treasury (DAO context, optional addendum): 0.10–0.30% of treasury AUM/yr, capped at the Large-tier license ceiling

The % of treasury option is materially below the Llama/Karpatkey 0.25–0.75% range, on purpose — NEOS is the governance fabric, not the treasury management service. The fabric should be cheaper than the service it enables.

4. Services mix

NEOS services are the lightest of the four layers. Most of the work is design (governance design, charter authoring, council structuring) and training rather than integration. Typical Mid-tier SOW:

Service Scope Fixed-fee range
Governance design workshop OmniOne ETHOS methodology, 2-day on-site or 4-half-day virtual $35K–$60K
Charter authoring 3–8 charters per engagement; legal review handoff included $25K–$80K
Council instantiation Council templates, signing key setup, contribution-accounting wiring $20K–$60K
Identity bridging To AZOA identity fabric, IdP integration (Okta/Entra/Auth0/SSI) $15K–$50K
Operator training Council moderators, secretaries, members $20K–$60K
30-day hypercare Post-launch stabilization Included

For DAO contexts, add treasury-policy authoring ($30K–$120K) and on-chain governance setup ($20K–$80K) as separately scoped items.

5. Sustained upkeep + infra

Annual sustained upkeep = 18–22% of license (lighter than the other three layers because configuration change rate is lower).

Infra (pass-through + 12% mgmt fee):

  • NEOS coordination server (per-org hosted): $200–$1,500/mo
  • Chain anchoring gas reserve (for any deployment that anchors governance events on-chain): variable per chain, typically $50–$500/mo for L2
  • Signature key management (KMS / HSM options): $300–$2,000/mo depending on tier
  • Optional treasury custody integration overhead (when bridging to Fireblocks / BitGo / Anchorage): pass-through

6. Bundle interactions

NEOS composes naturally with every other layer. The two most common bundles:

Other layer What you unlock together
+ AZOA Identity federation + signed cross-org events. Required duo for any multi-entity governance deployment (federations, cross-DAO coordination, multi-state agency cooperation, supply-chain consortia).
+ Fractal Engine Governance events become first-class signals. Contribution accounting → workforce intelligence; council decisions → policy-effectiveness analytics. High-value duo for finance (CRO dashboards), healthcare (CCO dashboards), and large DAOs (treasury policy backtesting).
+ AMP Governance attached to place: “this approval was issued from this location, on this asset, by this signed identity at this moment.” Required duo for regulated operational contexts (manufacturing change control, hospital lockout/tagout, energy NERC CIP-013 attestations).

7. Two worked engagement examples

7.1 Mid NEOS+AZOA: 200-person worker cooperative federation (Tier-2 Mid, CC-NC eligible? No — commercial)

  • Buyer: Federation of 5 worker cooperatives, ~200 total members, $40M combined revenue, planning member equity tokenization and cross-coop work-sharing
  • Scope: 5 nested councils (one per co-op) + 1 federation council; contribution accounting across coops; identity federation via AZOA
  • De-minimis check: Combined revenue $40M → above $1M threshold → PCL required
  • Quote:
  • NEOS Mid: $55K/yr (6 councils × ~$9K/council/yr)
  • AZOA Small: $40K/yr (5–10 integrations, identity-heavy)
  • Duet bundle: -10% → $85K/yr license

  • Y1 upkeep (Silver SLA, 22%): starts Y2 at $19K
  • Infra: $1.2K/mo
  • Y1 total: ~$170K · 3-yr TCO: ~$400K
  • Friction-cost anchor: $200K/yr currently spent across Loomio, Diligent, custom contribution-tracking spreadsheets, lawyer hours formalizing inter-coop agreements, and one painful prior governance failure that cost $300K in dissolved trust
  • Capture rate: ~$170K ÷ $200K = 85% capture of direct friction — but the bigger value is the federation enablement that wasn’t possible at all before, justifying the price above the direct-friction floor

7.2 Large NEOS+AZOA+Fractal: G-SIB tokenization program governance (Tier-3 Large)

  • Buyer: CTO + Head of Digital Assets + Chief Risk Officer at a $1.5T AUM global asset manager launching a tokenized fund product
  • Scope: NEOS governance fabric for the tokenization program (issuance approval councils, KYC policy councils, transfer-agent attestation flows, regulatory reporting attestations), AZOA for chain + custody integration, Fractal for risk + compliance reporting
  • Quote:
  • NEOS Large: $280K/yr (multi-entity, regulated FS premium)
  • AZOA Large: $620K/yr (10+ chains + Fireblocks + Anchorage + Snowflake + legacy core integration)
  • Fractal Large: $750K/yr (regulatory + risk analytics + audit trail intelligence)
  • Triad bundle: -18% → $1.35M/yr license
  • Y1 services (~1.4x blend): $1.9M
  • Y1 upkeep (Platinum SLA, custom %): $400K (starts Y2)
  • Infra: $300K–$800K/yr
  • Y1 total: ~$3.5M–$4.0M · 3-yr TCO: ~$8M–$11M
  • Friction-cost anchor: MiCA non-compliance = €15M or 3% of revenue (~$45M for this firm). OCC Letter 1184 attestation premium: per the finance research brief, banks will pay $200K–$500K+/yr per platform that produces attestations on-demand. Custody compliance overhead reduced by SAB 122 but legal + ops cost still $1–2M/yr at this scale. Estimated capture window $5–10M/yr in defensible friction
  • Capture rate: $1.35M ÷ $7M = ~19% value share — defensible inside the 10–25% finance capture band

8. Steward economics

Per ../02-licensing-model.md §4:

  • OmniOne royalty: 18% of NEOS line-item license revenue, paid quarterly
  • OmniOne is also the primary delivery partner for governance design + ETHOS methodology engagements; services revenue accrues to OmniOne directly when they deliver
  • The CC-NC + PCL structure is specifically what protects OmniOne’s stewardship from being undercut by cloud-hyperscaler hosting (the MongoDB-vs-AWS-DocumentDB pattern)

9. Risks specific to NEOS

Risk Mitigation
CC-NC ambiguity over “commercial use” De-minimis thresholds (§2) define commercial use objectively by revenue + headcount + members
DAO governance attack vectors NEOS ships with sane defaults (min 3/5 multisig <$10M treasury, 4/7 above; replay protection; flash-loan-resistant voting). Compliance posture documented per engagement
Cloud hyperscaler fork-and-host SSPL/BSL-style precedent (HashiCorp, MongoDB, Elastic) shows CC-NC + PCL holds up commercially. Trademark also restricts fork branding
“We already have Diligent / Aragon / Loomio” NEOS is a governance protocol, not a portal. Discovery shows the gap: durable contribution accounting, holonic federation, signed event log — none of the incumbents do all three
Government / public sector procurement NASPO / Sourcewell / GSA enrollment plus CC-NC visibility for civic deployments creates a hybrid pipeline that GovTech incumbents (Tyler, Granicus) can’t easily match
