4. Industry-specific procurement signals

Buyer personas and decision authority:

Buyer Role Authority Primary concern
Chief Grid Officer / VP Grid Operations Grid modernization sponsor Operational approval; PUC testimony Reliability, NERC CIP, OT security
CIO / VP Technology Platform + vendor governance Architecture review, vendor risk IT/OT convergence, integration complexity, vendor concentration
COO Overall business case Budget authority TCO, risk, regulatory deadlines
Head of Trading / VP Commercial ETRM, carbon, VPP Revenue-side technology Settlement accuracy, ISO market connectivity
Chief Sustainability Officer Carbon MRV, ESG disclosure Board-level mandate CSRD/CBAM accuracy, auditability, Article 6.4
Regulatory Affairs / General Counsel PUC filings Rate-case approval Regulatory defensibility, vendor attestation records

Procurement pathway: Contracts at $5M+ require PUC pre-approval or inclusion in an Integrated Resource Plan (IRP) filing before procurement can proceed. RFPs are issued via regulated competitive bidding with 3–6 month technical evaluation phases. Short-listed vendors submit detailed compliance architecture documentation before any commercial discussion proceeds.

Sales cycle: IOUs and ISO-RTOs: 18–36 months. Municipal utilities and mid-size IPPs: 6–18 months. Supermajors (O&G, large renewables): 12–24 months.

Budget cycle alignment: IOU capital plans are set annually through IRP and budget processes. Missing the capital plan submission window by one quarter delays a deal by twelve months. This is the single most preventable deal-loss in energy; sales motion must align to IRP calendar, not calendar year-end.

5. Rejection-risk mitigations

NERC CIP / OT cyber risk: Software that touches BES Cyber Systems must be classified, change-managed, and assessed under CIP-007 (systems security management), CIP-010 (configuration management), and CIP-013 (supply-chain risk management). Mitigation package: ship a documented CIP compliance architecture before any technical evaluation; CIP-005 jump-server and MFA topology for all vendor remote access; CIP-013 supply-chain assessments pre-populated for AZOA (AZOA) and PLAN Systems (AMP) as the OT-touching components; published security attestation pack available day one of evaluation. No documented CIP compliance architecture equals automatic rejection.

Vendor concentration risk: IOUs and ISOs enforce multi-vendor policies post-SolarWinds and post-Volt Typhoon disclosures. A platform simultaneously touching SCADA, ADMS, DERMS, and carbon accounting triggers vendor concentration review automatically. Mitigation: PrimusNeo enters above incumbent stacks, not as displacement. ADMS stays with GE Vernova, Schneider, or Siemens. AVEVA PI System stays as the historian. PrimusNeo is the interoperability and governance fabric above them. The federated steward structure reinforces this: AZOA (AZOA), AMP (PLAN Systems), NEOS (OmniOne), Fractal (Escherbridge) are distinct stewards — not a single vendor.

OT integration complexity: Legacy distribution SCADA and EMS run proprietary protocols: DNP3, IEC 61968/70 (CIM), ICCP, Modbus. Governance or interop platforms claiming “open APIs” without certified OT protocol connectors fail at utility architecture review boards. Mitigation: AZOA authors certified DNP3, IEC 61968/70, and ICCP connectors for AZOA as first-class connector-library entries. Reference architecture documentation available with every technical evaluation package.

Regulatory approval delays: Rate-recovery technology spend faces 18–36 month PUC evidentiary cycles. Deals stall in hearings if cost-benefit testimony is thin. Mitigation: third-party ROI validation commissioned before the rate case opens, not after. McKinsey Energy Solutions or Deloitte E&R as the validation firm adds regulatory credibility. PrimusNeo’s friction-cost anchor methodology (sourced to DOE, NERC, Federal Register) provides the evidentiary backbone.

Budget cycle mismatch: Missing the IRP capital plan window delays deals by a full year. Mitigation: annual IRP planning calendar awareness is a sales-team requirement for IOU accounts. Enterprise deal entry must be sequenced to the preceding IRP cycle, not to the buyer’s expressed urgency.

6. Regulatory + compliance considerations

FERC Order 2222 — DER Market Participation: Requires all ISOs and RTOs to allow aggregated DER participation in wholesale markets down to 100 kW minimum aggregated capacity. Mandatory metering, telemetry, and settlement-data interoperability is the direct tech-spend trigger. AZOA is architecturally positioned for this integration layer; NEOS governs the multi-party attestation council for the DER aggregation.

NERC CIP — Critical Infrastructure Protection: Standards CIP-002 through CIP-015-1 govern BES Cyber System security. CIP-015-1 (Internal Network Security Monitoring): $13.2M/yr industry-wide compliance burden across ~1,636 registered entities. CIP-013 supply-chain risk management: structured vendor assessment and attestation for BES Cyber System vendors — NEOS supply-chain council template applies directly. CIP-003-9 (effective April 2026): extends supply-chain controls to low-impact BES assets, expanding the addressable compliance population and the NEOS WTP signal.

EU CSRD / CSDDD — Sustainability Disclosure: Large EU-listed energy firms and their non-EU subsidiaries face mandatory double-materiality ESG disclosures from FY2024 reporting. The EU Omnibus simplification package (September 2025) reduced scope to ~900 companies at EU level but preserved core emissions disclosure requirements. NEOS provides the auditable governance event log underpinning these disclosures; Fractal provides the MRV intelligence layer feeding the disclosure data.

EU CBAM — Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism: Definitive phase begins January 2026. Energy exporters with embedded-carbon products entering the EU must submit certified embedded-carbon data per shipment, backed by third-party verified MRV. Financial penalties apply for non-compliant or unverifiable data. Fractal MRV + NEOS attestation together produce the certified embedded-carbon data trail CBAM requires.

Article 6.4 — Paris Agreement ITMOs: COP29 (November 2024) finalized Article 6.4 rules for Internationally Transferred Mitigation Outcomes. Carbon market participants need cryptographically auditable provenance chains to prevent double-counting across NDCs. NEOS governance events + Fractal lineage attestation map directly to this requirement. Near-term land opportunity: carbon market participants seeking Article 6.4-compliant provenance infrastructure before 2027 compliance deadlines.

State PUC Rate Recovery: IOU technology investments in ADMS, DERMS, cybersecurity, and compliance tooling are recoverable through rate cases in most jurisdictions. California CPUC has effectively mandated ADMS deployment for large IOUs; New York REV and Illinois CEJA contain analogous provisions. Rate-payer funding de-risks vendor revenue through the procurement cycle but introduces the 18–36 month PUC approval timeline.

7. Steward + service partner notes

PLAN Systems (AMP steward): Leads spatial scene authoring for power plant and substation deployments. PLAN’s industrial-grade 3D interface components and Hexon file standard (Apache-2.0) are the foundation for energy site twins. PLAN Systems subcontracts for spatial environment buildout and sensor/OT integration on AMP-containing engagements; royalty rate 15% of AMP license line-item.

Escherbridge (Fractal Engine steward): Their analytics depth is the strategic differentiator for energy APM and carbon MRV. Escherbridge delivers Fractal ML engineering, custom predictive maintenance models (turbine degradation, transformer health), and carbon MRV intelligence pipelines. They are the primary delivery partner for any Fractal-containing energy engagement with significant telemetry volume or regulatory MRV requirements. OmniOne leads governance design workshops and charter authoring on NEOS-containing engagements. Royalty rate 18% of NEOS license line-item.

Finance — Engagement Model

Vertical: Finance (banks, asset managers, insurers, exchanges, tokenization platforms, RWA) Research brief: ../research/finance.md Anchors: ../01-pricing-model.md, ../research/layer-comparables.md

1. The friction-cost anchor

Financial institutions face four compounding friction windows that make the PrimusNeo value case quantifiable before the first meeting.

Information-layer reference price. Bloomberg Terminal at $31,980/seat/year (multi-seat negotiated $28,320; enterprise 50+ at $18K–$20K/seat) sets the market expectation for what FIs will pay annually to access structured, auditable, institutional-grade data. This is the anchor for any conversation about what a governance + interop + intelligence fabric is worth per-seat at an asset manager or exchange. Refinitiv Eikon ($22K/seat), FactSet ($12K/seat), and Snowflake/Databricks enterprise ($25K–$40K/yr for 200TB, up to $10M/yr at 500-analyst scale) extend the band.

Tokenization friction. Securitize and Tokeny (T-REX) charge $50K–$150K setup plus 0.5%–2.5% of issuance value for smart-contract deployment and compliance wrapping. A $30M issuance runs $200K–$1M all-in. White-label platform build costs range $50K–$150K upfront with custom development from $150K. Ongoing compliance and transfer-agent retainers layer on top. For a mid-tier asset manager running $500M of tokenized AUM, issuance friction alone is $2.5M–$12.5M over the tokenization program lifecycle.

Custody cost. BitGo charges approximately 60 bps/year on AUC (5 bps/month on AUC >$100K). Fireblocks and Anchorage are bilateral-negotiated at $1M+ AUC minimum. Across a $500M–$2B notional portfolio, annual custody cost runs $3M–$12M — costs that AZOA multi-custodian integration directly reduces by eliminating bespoke custodian-by-custodian API build and maintenance.

Regulatory premium. MiCA non-compliance fines: €15M or 3% of annual revenue, whichever is higher. For a mid-sized EU-registered CASP with $100M revenue, 3% = €3M at risk annually. OCC Interpretive Letter 1184 (May 2025) means national banks providing crypto custody must demonstrate controls on-demand; banks will pay $200K–$500K+/year per platform that produces those attestations. SAB 122 (January 2025) rescinds SAB 121’s on-balance-sheet liability treatment, removing the primary accounting disincentive for bank custody — expanding the addressable market for compliant custody infrastructure significantly. FCA implicit compliance tax runs £100K–£500K/year in incremental monitoring and reporting under crypto-asset registration and stablecoin frameworks.

Per-customer friction windows (worked):

Customer tier Annual friction estimate Source composition
Boutique manager / family office <$1B AUM $200K–$500K TaaS SaaS + legal/structuring + custody at 60 bps/yr on $100M AUC + KYC/AML ($30K–$90K) + governance software
Regional bank / asset mgr $1–50B AUM $1.5M–$4M White-label tokenization + issuance legal + Fireblocks/BitGo custody on $500M–$2B notional + Chainalysis ($150K–$300K) + GRC/IRM ($300K–$600K) + data fabric ($150K–$400K)
G-SIB / top-20 asset mgr / major exchange $8M–$25M+ Custom tokenization + interop fabric ($2M–$5M build) + multi-custodian + Snowflake/Databricks at scale ($2M–$10M/yr) + full Chainalysis + TRM ($400K–$1M/yr) + ServiceNow IRM ($600K–$1.5M/yr) + Big-4 transformation

The 10–25% capture band on these friction windows yields the PrimusNeo defensible quote range per tier.

2. PrimusNeo positioning per layer in finance

AMP — lighter use; deferred or scoped to specific facilities

AMP’s primary value proposition in finance is operational and place-anchored: trading floor coordination, secure vault facilities, data center walk-through governance. These are real use cases — particularly for regulated operational control rooms where “this approval was issued from this location by this signed identity at this moment” is a compliance requirement — but they are not the lead in a finance engagement. Most finance buyers do not have a spatial-twin problem as their primary friction. AMP is typically deferred to Year 2 or scoped as an add-on for specific facilities within a larger NEOS+AZOA+Fractal deployment. Finance is the one vertical where the Full Stack upsell to include AMP commonly happens in Y2–Y3, not at contract signature.

NEOS — the primary killer layer for finance

NEOS is the layer that directly solves the compliance, governance, and attestation requirements that have become binary procurement requirements following MiCA, OCC Letter 1184, and SAB 122. In the buyer’s vocabulary:

  • To a CRO: NEOS is the programmable approval-and-attestation fabric that produces the immutable governance logs MiCA, OCC Letter 1184, and SAB 122 now make non-optional.
  • To a Head of Digital Assets: NEOS is the issuance approval council, KYC policy council, and transfer-agent attestation flow layer that replaces the current patchwork of email chains, legal memos, and manually-assembled audit trails.
  • To a tokenization program lead: NEOS is the RWA governance layer that gives every tokenized asset a durable, replayable, chain-anchored provenance record — the compliance artifact that Securitize and Tokeny charge 0.5–2.5% of issuance value to produce without a durable governance trail.

NEOS positions above Diligent ($12K–$60K+/yr board portal) and Loomio ($1.2K–$7.2K/yr discussion tool) because it delivers on-chain auditability, contribution accounting, and programmable attestation — none of the governance portals do all three. In the regulated FS context, the on-chain auditability alone is worth the premium; Diligent produces PDFs, NEOS produces cryptographically verifiable event logs.

AZOA — the other primary killer layer for finance

Finance is the vertical where AZOA’s pitch as “what MuleSoft is for Web2 enterprise integration, AZOA is for the Web2+Web3+identity+custody reality” lands with the most force. No incumbent iPaaS covers multi-custodian (Fireblocks, BitGo, Anchorage) + multi-chain (EVM, Solana, Cosmos, Hedera) + legacy core-banking (Temenos, Finastra, Murex, Calypso) + regulatory feed integration in a single certified connector library. MuleSoft starts at $80K–$250K+/year for the enterprise license without finance-specific chain connectors; real large-enterprise MuleSoft runs $500K–$2M+/year before implementation. AZOA covers the Web3+custody+legacy-core surface area MuleSoft doesn’t, at parity or below for the Web2 integrations MuleSoft does cover.

For G-SIBs and top-tier asset managers, AZOA’s multi-custodian-native federation engine is the explicit answer to the vendor-concentration rejection risk: the federated 4-steward structure IS the multi-vendor answer, and AZOA is the layer that operationalizes it.

Fractal Engine — regulatory, risk, and AML signal intelligence

Fractal positions against Palantir Foundry (entry $250K–$500K/year commercial, $1M–$10M+/year for large deployments) and Snowflake/Databricks (strong for raw data warehousing but no native governance-event signal types). In finance, the Fractal differentiator is that NEOS governance events and AZOA integration events are first-class signal types — meaning the CRO dashboard, the regulatory audit log, and the AML signal model all run on the same fabric that produced the governance events, not on a separately assembled data pipeline.

Chainalysis Reactor runs $40K/year single-seat; enterprise KYT $100K–$500K+/year. Fractal is not a direct AML-chain-intelligence replacement for Chainalysis — it is the layer that aggregates Chainalysis signals, on-chain governance events, and off-chain transaction context into a unified risk model. The combination is more defensible than either alone, and Fractal’s pricing is scoped per the data-source and intelligence-model tiers below, not as a per-chain AML overlay.

The canonical finance bundle: NEOS + AZOA + Fractal (Triad)

The default finance bundle is the Triad: NEOS + AZOA + Fractal Engine. AMP is deferred to Y2. The Triad bundle discount is 18% off single-layer sum per the pricing model. This is the configuration that:

  1. Produces the immutable governance logs (NEOS) required by MiCA, OCC 1184, and SAB 122
  2. Integrates across custodians, chains, and legacy core-banking (AZOA) without requiring rip-and-replace of Temenos/Finastra/Murex/Calypso
  3. Converts governance and integration events into regulatory reporting, risk signals, and AML intelligence (Fractal)

3. Engagement tiers — Small / Mid / Large

Tier 1 — Small: Boutique manager / family office (<$1B AUM)

Profile: Boutique hedge fund, family office, or early tokenization platform. Primary drivers: basic KYC-council governance for investor onboarding, single-chain or dual-chain tokenization, limited legacy-core integration.

Bundle recommendation: NEOS Small + AZOA Small Duet. Fractal deferred to Y2 unless the CRO is driving AML analytics requirements from day one.

Friction anchor: $200K–$500K/year in TaaS SaaS ($72K–$180K), legal/structuring ($100K–$250K), custody at 60 bps/year on $100M AUC (~$60K), and KYC/AML ($30K–$90K). OCC Letter 1184 attestation premium: $200K/yr for a compliant platform producing on-demand attestations.

Worked quote:

NEOS Small:    $12K/yr   (midpoint $6K–$18K; single org, 1–2 councils)
AZOA Small:     $42K/yr   (midpoint $24K–$60K; <10 integrations:
                          2 chain connectors + custody API + KYC/IdP + core banking)
Duet bundle:   -10%  →   $48K/yr license

Y1 services:   ~1.0x NEOS + ~2.5x AZOA blend → $113K
  (NEOS governance design + charter authoring: $60K;
   AZOA connector buildout 4 connectors at avg $18K ea: $72K,
   connector bundle 10% off → $65K; identity bridging: $20K)
Y1 upkeep:     Silver SLA, 22% of license → $11K (starts Y2)
Infra:         $1.5K/mo → $18K/yr

Y1 total: ~$179K 3-yr TCO: ~$370K (Y2–Y3: license $48K + upkeep $11K + infra $18K/yr, services taper to ~$20K/yr)

Friction anchor check: $179K Y1 ÷ $300K friction midpoint = 60% capture in Y1, justified because NEOS displaces legal memo + email-chain governance directly and AZOA displaces manual custodian API maintenance. At Y1=60% capture the buyer is still clearly positive. 3-yr TCO of $370K sits well inside the research brief’s $350K–$900K small-tier band.

Capture rate: 10–20% of the OCC attestation premium ($200K–$500K/yr) alone = $20K–$100K/yr recurring justification, separate from the integration cost displacement.

Tier 2 — Mid: Regional bank or asset manager ($1–50B AUM)

Profile: Regional bank entering digital assets, mid-cap asset manager launching tokenized fund, or specialized exchange. Primary drivers: MiCA compliance, multi-custodian custody integration, KYC policy governance, transfer-agent attestation, regulatory reporting.

Bundle recommendation: NEOS Mid + AZOA Mid + Fractal Mid Triad. This is the default finance engagement configuration.

Friction anchor: $1.5M–$4M/year — white-label tokenization ($150K–$500K), issuance legal ($200K–$600K), Fireblocks/BitGo custody on $500M–$2B notional, Chainalysis ($150K–$300K), GRC/IRM ($300K–$600K), data fabric ($150K–$400K), Big-4 SI ($500K–$1.5M). MiCA non-compliance at 3% of revenue = $3M/yr at $100M revenue.

Combined friction window: $5M–$10M/yr defensible capture surface.

4. Industry-specific procurement signals

Decision makers by tier:

Tier Primary Champion Tech Sign-off Compliance Veto Entry Price for Procurement
Small CIO / COO CTO — >$250K (informal at small)
Mid Head of Digital Assets CDO / CTO CRO >$500K (formal procurement)
Large Head of Digital Assets + C-suite sponsor (typically COO or CFO) CTO + CDO committee CRO + Legal + Compliance >$500K; full RFP process

At the G-SIB tier, expect a full C-suite committee process with regulatory affairs as a non-optional voice. The Head of Digital Assets is typically the internal champion, but the CRO has explicit veto authority over any platform that cannot produce attestations on the timetable regulators require.

Sales cycle guidance: - Small (<$1B AUM): 2–4 months, informal process - Mid ($1–50B AUM): 6–12 months; formal RFP likely above $500K - Large (G-SIB, top-20 AM, major exchange): 12–24 months; multiple RFP stages, security review, legal review, and often a paid pilot (discovery sprint) before substantive RFP

Gating requirements before any substantive RFP at mid or large:

  1. SOC 2 Type II (US default; mandatory for mid/large)
  2. ISO 27001 (EU/APAC; mandatory for MiCA-scope deployments)
  3. Annual third-party penetration test (results must be shareable under NDA)
  4. BCP/DR documentation
  5. GDPR / data residency mapping (for any EU data subject exposure)
  6. Reference clients in regulated FS (at least one publicly nameable reference at comparable scale)

Missing SOC 2 Type II or ISO 27001 is an automatic disqualification at mid and large. Both are funded as central PrimusNeo investments, not per-engagement costs.

Procurement enters at >$500K. Below this threshold, the Head of Digital Assets or CIO can typically approve without a formal procurement process. Above it, procurement introduces benchmarking requirements — typically against MuleSoft, ServiceNow IRM, and Securitize/Tokeny, which is where the friction-cost anchoring in section 1 becomes the sales tool.

5. Rejection-risk mitigations

Rejection reason Mitigation
Regulatory ambiguity — Legal/compliance veto until local regulator gives written guidance Publish multi-jurisdictional compliance posture per layer: MiCA (NEOS attestation logs map to CASP requirements; AZOA cross-border identity portability); OCC Letter 1184 (NEOS + Fractal on-demand attestation architecture); SAB 122 (AZOA multi-custodian integration removes the custody-accounting friction); FCA crypto-asset registration (NEOS governance log architecture).
Custody model mismatch — Must map to self-custody MPC/HSM, qualified custodian, or bank-grade sub-custodian AZOA is multi-custodian-native: certified connectors for Fireblocks, BitGo, and Anchorage are first-party; sub-custodian relationships with bank-grade MPC/HSM providers are supported through the AZOA connector library.
Legacy core integration gap — Temenos / Finastra / Murex / Calypso require certified FIX/API connectors Publish certified connectors for Temenos T24 / Transact, Finastra Fusion, Murex MX.3, and Calypso as first-class AZOA connector library entries; include FIX 4.2/4.4/5.0 gateway support for exchange and trading-desk integrations. These are AZOA-authored connectors, which is where their connector-engineering depth shines.
Open-source / IP ownership risk — Unaudited smart contracts, no formal vuln disclosure, no IP indemnity IP indemnity capped at 2× annual fees for the affected layer per the commercial terms in 01-pricing-model.md §6. Smart-contract audit is bundled as a deliverable for any issuance-adjacent NEOS deployment. Formal CVE disclosure program.
Vendor concentration / lock-in risk — Multi-custodian, multi-chain required at G-SIB The federated 4-steward structure (OmniOne + Escherbridge + PLAN + AZOA) IS the multi-vendor answer. No single entity controls the stack. Data export is non-negotiable per the commercial terms. Source escrow available for AMP/AZOA components above $500K ARR via Iron Mountain.
Missing security certs — SOC 2 + ISO 27001 non-negotiable at mid/large SOC 2 Type II + ISO 27001 funded as central PrimusNeo investments. Not a per-engagement cost. The Tie Town [TX] reference deployment provides the operational proving ground for the security posture before any formal audit engagement.

6. Regulatory + compliance considerations

MiCA (EU, fully applicable December 2024). NEOS produces the auditable issuance records and governance event logs that CASP licensing requires. AZOA provides the cross-border identity portability architecture that MiCA’s cross-member-state CASP passporting requires. Non-compliance fines (€15M or 3% of revenue) make both layers non-optional for any EU-registered CASP. MiCA CASP licensing build-out itself runs €50K–€100K for small operators; capital requirements add €50K–€150K depending on activity class. PrimusNeo’s MiCA compliance architecture should be documented per-layer and available as a regulatory briefing before any mid or large engagement progresses to RFP.

OCC Interpretive Letter 1184 (May 2025). National banks may now provide crypto custody (fiduciary and non-fiduciary) and use sub-custodians, but must demonstrate controls on-demand. NEOS + Fractal attestation logs are designed to produce these on-demand demonstrations: governance events are cryptographically signed, chain-anchored, and replayable; Fractal aggregates them into the audit-trail intelligence package the OCC examiner reviews. Banks will pay $200K–$500K+/year for platforms that make this attestation production routine rather than a manual legal exercise.

SEC SAB 122 (January 2025). Rescinds SAB 121’s on-balance-sheet liability treatment for crypto custody. This removes the primary accounting disincentive for bank custody, expanding the addressable market for compliant custody infrastructure. AZOA’s multi-custodian integration layer is directly positioned to capture this expanded TAM: as more banks enter crypto custody, the integration complexity between custodians, core banking, and regulatory reporting increases — which is exactly the surface area AZOA addresses.

FCA + GDPR cross-border. Any deployment touching UK data subjects requires FCA crypto-asset registration posture and GDPR/UK GDPR data residency mapping. AZOA’s regional gateway architecture and NEOS’s data-residency-aware signing model support data-residency compliance without architectural rework.

SOC 2 + ISO 27001 as gating. Both funded centrally. No mid or large finance engagement can proceed to substantive RFP without these in hand. The Tie Town [TX] reference deployment is the proving ground; certification scope covers the full PrimusNeo stack including all four layers.

CRA (EU Cyber Resilience Act) / CSRD overlap. For exchange operators and large asset managers with EU reporting obligations, CSRD (sustainability reporting) and CRA (cybersecurity for connected products) create additional compliance overhead that Fractal’s regulatory model layer can address: NEOS governance events become CSRD audit artifacts; AZOA integration events support CSRD data collection pipelines.

7. Steward + service partner notes

OmniOne (NEOS steward) is the primary delivery partner for governance design in regulated financial services. NEOS’s ETHOS methodology engagements — issuance approval council design, KYC policy council structuring, transfer-agent attestation flow architecture — are OmniOne’s domain. OmniOne earns 18% of NEOS line-item license revenue as steward royalty, and services revenue accrues directly when they deliver governance design work. In finance, expect OmniOne’s involvement to be heaviest in the mid/large tier where regulatory attestation design is non-trivial.

Escherbridge (Fractal steward) is the primary delivery partner for Fractal regulatory and risk models. In finance, this means: CRO dashboard architecture, regulatory reporting model design, AML signal aggregation ontology, and the audit-trail attestation buildout that OCC Letter 1184 requires. Escherbridge earns 18% of Fractal line-item license revenue. For regulated FS engagements, Escherbridge also contributes to the regulatory model validation scope ($60K–$250K per engagement) that is a separately scoped deliverable for any high-risk Fractal deployment.

AZOA (AZOA steward) leads multi-chain and custody connector development — this is where their connector-engineering depth is most directly applied. The Fireblocks, BitGo, Anchorage, Temenos, Finastra, Murex, Calypso, and FIX gateway connectors are AZOA-authored first-party connectors. For finance, AZOA’s custody and core-banking connector library is the primary competitive differentiator against MuleSoft and Boomi, neither of which has a certified multi-custodian + multi-chain connector set. AZOA earns 15% of AZOA line-item license revenue and leads connector SOW delivery.

SI channel — Big-4 engagement sizes and rates. For mid and large finance engagements, SI partners are typically involved in implementation, change management, and regulatory advisory. Rates from the research brief: Deloitte FSI (GSA) $2K–$3K/day senior consultant, $3K–$5K/day principal/director, $5K–$10K/day partner (digital-asset specialty). EY Blockchain, PwC Digital Assets, KPMG Capital Markets, and Accenture Capital Markets operate in similar bands. Engagement sizes: tokenization readiness assessment $150K–$400K (8–16 weeks); full tokenization program delivery $1M–$5M (6–18 months); MiCA/regulatory compliance implementation $500K–$2M; enterprise data fabric + governance transformation $3M–$12M. At mid tier, SI channel engagement adds $500K–$1.5M to the total program cost alongside PrimusNeo fees. At large tier (G-SIB), $3M–$12M SI engagement is typical — this is a revenue opportunity for PrimusNeo through SI referral and co-sell arrangements, not a competitive threat.

Tie Town [TX] reference. The Tie Town deployment provides the operational proof-of-concept for the full stack — governance councils, interop fabric, and intelligence layer — running in a real federated environment. In regulated FS sales cycles, having a live reference deployment that can be demonstrated under NDA is a prerequisite for advancing past the initial security and compliance review. Tie Town is that reference.

8. Revisions

Version Date Author Change
0.1 2026-05-23 Founding team + research squad Initial Finance engagement model

Governance — Engagement Model

Verticals: Public-sector / Civic Government AND DAO / Web3 Governance / Cooperatives / Federations Research brief: ../research/governance.md Anchors: ../01-pricing-model.md, ../research/layer-comparables.md

1. The friction-cost anchor

Governance spans two structurally different buyer archetypes but both pay enormous friction costs for inadequate coordination infrastructure.

Civic anchor. GovTech incumbents extract rents far beyond their delivered value. Tyler Technologies’ EnerGov permitting platform ranges $50K–$2.7M in implementation fees alone, with annual maintenance of 18–22% on top. The Illinois statewide Tyler contract ballooned to $250M with documented delays — a cautionary tale for every large-city CIO. Granicus’ Citizen Experience Cloud runs $25K–$500K/yr at state level. Esri statewide enterprise agreements hit $2M–$10M+/yr. The FedRAMP Moderate authorization gate alone costs $400K–$900K and 12–24 months of staff time before a single user can be provisioned on a federal contract. None of these incumbents offer durable contribution accounting, holonic federation, or auditable signed-event logs. They are licensing access to convenience, not solving coordination. The combined annual friction exposure for a mid-size city managing GovTech contracts, participatory democracy tools, board portals, and identity systems runs $500K–$2M/yr; for a state-level agency, $5M–$20M+/yr.

DAO anchor. Economic value in DAO governance concentrates almost entirely in treasury management services rather than the tooling itself — Snapshot, Tally, and Aragon are free at the protocol layer, which creates a dangerous impression that governance is “solved.” It isn’t. Llama charges ~$2.5M/yr for Aave treasury management. Karpatkey and Steakhouse Financial provide comparable services at $200K–$500K/yr base plus performance upside, with Steakhouse’s Lido engagement on public record at ~$322K/yr. The generic treasury advisory range is 0.25–0.75% of AUM annually — a $500M treasury implies $1.25M–$3.75M in governance-adjacent services spend. Meanwhile, governance attacks have destroyed $4B+ across DAO treasuries since 2016. The friction cost is twofold: the services overhead and the existential risk of attack. A top-50 DAO has a defensible friction window of $2M–$8M/yr combining services + security risk.

Pricing model capture band: 15–30% of governance services spend per the 01-pricing-model.md summary table. This is the highest capture band across all verticals, reflecting the clarity of the value case (governance fabric is the prerequisite for everything else) and the direct line-item displacement NEOS provides against incumbent services spend.

2. PrimusNeo positioning per layer in governance

Governance is the vertical where NEOS is the center of gravity. Every other layer is additive; NEOS is non-negotiable.

NEOS is the durable governance fabric above Snapshot signal and Tally execution for DAOs, and the inter-departmental decision-rights system that bridges Tyler/Granicus civic platforms and participatory democracy tools (Decidim, Pol.is) for public-sector buyers. The framing differs by buyer archetype but the product is the same: programmable approval-and-attestation fabric, contribution accounting, holonic federation, signed auditable event logs. The federated four-steward structure is not just an org chart — it is the explicit answer to GovTech vendor lock-in (Tyler, Granicus, Esri) because no single entity owns the stack. That structural credibility matters to both city councils nervous about another $250M Tyler overrun and DAO stewards nervous about protocol-level centralization.

AZOA handles the connective tissue in both contexts. Civic: cross-agency interoperability, FHIR-adjacent identity data flows, SSI Verified Credentials for constituent identity, multi-jurisdiction data exchange. DAO: cross-chain bridges (EVM, Cosmos, Solana, Hedera), off-chain identity federation, decentralized storage backends. AZOA is the mandatory co-purchase with NEOS for any multi-entity governance deployment.

AMP (Spatial OS) has lighter but real application in governance. Civic: emergency management coordination (spatial scene plus governance approvals in a single view), urban planning participation (spatial twin of a development project attached to participatory consultation records), physical-asset permitting (approved asset location signed to a governance event). DAO/cooperative: less relevant at the spatial layer; the cooperative’s “site” is more metaphorical. AMP enters governance engagements at Mid and Large tiers where there is a physical-infrastructure component.

Fractal Engine converts the governance stack into operating intelligence. Civic: policy effectiveness analytics (which council decisions produced what budget outcomes), grant allocation optimization, constituent service telemetry, cross-agency spend intelligence. DAO: treasury policy backtesting, delegate effectiveness scoring, protocol KPI intelligence, on-chain signal analytics — “the Llama analytics layer without the Llama retainer” is the exact positioning.

3. Engagement tiers — Small / Mid / Large

Small: 1 town / 1 cooperative / 1 small DAO (<$10M treasury)

Bundle: NEOS Small + AZOA Small (Duet). AMP and Fractal rare at this tier.

A small municipality replacing a patchwork of Google Forms, email chains, and Loomio for council decision-making and resident engagement.

Capture rate: $91K ÷ $90K = ~1x — justified because the compliance audit trail NEOS produces eliminates the town clerk’s manual reconciliation labor and creates a defensible record the incumbents don’t provide.

Mid: City 100K–500K / state agency / mid DAO ($10M–$500M treasury)

4. Industry-specific procurement signals

Civic buyer personas. CIO / CITO triggers on modernization mandates, legacy EOL, and federal efficiency pressure — they control the budget line and sign the contract. Chief Innovation Officer often sponsors pilot engagements outside the standard IT procurement lane; shorter cycle (90–120 days for a pilot SOW vs 6–18 months for full RFP), but must convert to CIO sponsorship for production scale. City Manager / County Administrator triggers on constituent service pressure and council mandates — the political buyer who creates the urgency a CIO needs to move. State agency: Deputy Secretary / Assistant Commissioner level with CIO co-approval.

DAO buyer personas. Steward Council or Foundation controls the treasury and signs the governance proposal. The procurement motion is a formal governance proposal (Research Forum post → Snapshot temperature check → Tally on-chain vote → execution) with a 2–8 week cycle including discussion period. This is faster than any civic procurement and does not require cooperative vehicle enrollment. The risk is governance attacks during the proposal period — PrimusNeo’s own governance design (ETHOS methodology, multisig defaults) should be demonstrable.

Cooperative / federation personas. Board vote, usually 30–60 days for an expedited resolution; longer if bylaws require member ratification. Federation steward boards are similar. These buyers often qualify for CC-NC free tier (nonprofit, small revenue) — be explicit about the de-minimis thresholds early to avoid wasted discovery.

Cooperative purchasing vehicles for civic. Sourcewell is the first enrollment target — 50,000+ member agencies, pre-competed vehicle, eliminates the 6–18 month full RFP cycle for a substantial share of local government buys. NASPO ValuePoint (all 50 states + territories, strong for cloud/SaaS) is the second. GSA MAS (SIN 518210C for IT; OASIS+ for professional services) unlocks federal and state/local cooperative purchasing authority. State-specific schedules (TX DIR, CA DGS, NY OGS) are non-negotiated pre-priced access within those states and are worth pursuing once any anchor reference exists in that state.

Budget cycle alignment. Federal FY ends September 30; decision pressure builds June–August. State and local vary but most compress into Q3–Q4. Missing a budget cycle is a 12-month pipeline delay regardless of product fit. Calendar-aware outreach (initial engagement 6–9 months before the agency’s fiscal year close) is required.

5. Rejection-risk mitigations

Procurement red tape. New vendor not on an approved cooperative vehicle = automatic elimination in most civic RFPs. Sourcewell enrollment must happen before any serious civic pipeline engagement. Tyler/Granicus/Esri switching costs run $500K–$5M+; PrimusNeo cannot win a pure displacement argument — win by expanding scope (participatory democracy fabric, cross-agency federation, DAO governance capability) that incumbents don’t cover at all.

FedRAMP gap. No ATO = disqualification for US federal and most state agencies. The FedRAMP 20x initiative (launched 2025) provides an agency-path acceleration route targeting ~90 days using pre-authorized infrastructure boundaries — pursue this path rather than the full JAB queue. Target FedRAMP Moderate authorization as the primary investment; High and DoD IL4/IL5 are phased based on pipeline revenue thresholds.

DoD security classification. IL2 is FedRAMP Moderate-equivalent ($400K–$800K). IL4 (CUI, contingency ops) and IL5 (NSS data) require DISA-authorized cloud hosting and controls well beyond FedRAMP Moderate. Phase: IL2 in Y1 → IL4 in Y2 when a defense-adjacent anchor client is signed. IL5 requires dedicated program investment.

DAO treasury security. Governance attacks are existential. NEOS ships with sane multisig defaults out of the box: 3-of-5 for treasuries below $10M, 4-of-7 for larger. Flash-loan-resistant voting (time-lock on proposal execution, snapshot voting power at proposal creation not vote time) and replay protection (chain-specific signature binding) are non-negotiable defaults. Governance security posture documentation is produced per engagement for any DAO above $10M treasury.

Data sovereignty / GDPR. EU public sector requires data residency in the EEA. Multi-region AZOA deployment with EU-hosted Fractal compute must be demonstrable to any EU civic buyer. This is not a future roadmap item — it must be available before any EU public tender engagement begins.

Proof of scale. Governments require 3–5 comparable references in evaluation. A lighthouse cooperative, a lighthouse mid-size city, and a lighthouse mid-DAO are the three reference deployment types required before pursuing large-tier civic or DAO engagements. Tie Town serves as the early reference but is not a civic government reference in the formal sense.

Budget cycle mismatch. Track municipal budget calendars actively. A contact made in November will not produce a procurement signature until after the next fiscal year starts. Engagement pacing must match.

6. Regulatory + compliance considerations

Civic regulatory stack.

Framework Scope Cost to PrimusNeo
FedRAMP Moderate US federal SaaS $400K–$900K authorization; $100K–$300K/yr ongoing
FedRAMP High Sensitive federal data $2M–$5M+ authorization
StateRAMP / GovRAMP US state + local (rebranded 2025) $150K–$500K
DoD IL2 Non-sensitive DoD public data $400K–$800K
DoD IL4 CUI + contingency ops $800K–$2M+
DoD IL5 NSS data $2M–$5M+
GDPR / EU NIS2 EU public sector EEA data residency; DPA; DPIA; up to €20M or 4% global turnover in fines
CMMC Level 2–3 Defense-adjacent contractors Level 3 requires C3PAO assessment: $500K–$3M+

DAO / Web3 regulatory stack.

MiCA CASP licensing (EU): required for EU-facing DAOs and crypto-asset service providers. Grandfathering period ends mid-2026; legal + licensing costs €200K–€1M+ depending on jurisdiction. PrimusNeo’s NEOS governance audit trail and AZOA cross-chain bridge architecture both support MiCA compliance posture documentation. SAB 122 (issued January 2025, replacing SAB 121) removes the requirement for custodians to hold crypto assets on their own balance sheet — meaningfully reducing custody compliance overhead for US DAO clients and their institutional counterparties.

Cross-cutting. EU public sector and EU-regulated DAOs share the data sovereignty requirement. Multi-region AZOA deployment with EEA-resident data processing is the single architectural commitment that unlocks both markets simultaneously.

7. Steward + service partner notes

OmniOne is the primary delivery partner for NEOS in both civic and DAO contexts. Their ETHOS methodology — governance design, charter authoring, council structuring, contribution accounting — is the central differentiable asset. NEOS deployments without OmniOne ETHOS facilitation are technically possible but miss the highest-value part of the engagement. OmniOne earns 18% royalty on NEOS license revenue plus direct services revenue when they deliver the governance design work.

AZOA leads AZOA implementation and is the author of the closed-source provider connectors. For civic engagements: SSI Verified Credentials for constituent identity, cross-agency interoperability fabric, GovCloud-compatible API gateway. For DAO engagements: cross-chain bridges (EVM to Cosmos to Solana and back), multi-custodian integration (Fireblocks, Anchorage, BitGo), off-chain identity federation. AZOA earns 15% royalty on AZOA license revenue.

Escherbridge delivers Fractal Engine ML engineering, custom model training, and treasury/policy analytics. For civic: policy effectiveness analytics, grant allocation intelligence, constituent service telemetry. For DAO: treasury policy backtesting, delegate-effectiveness scoring, protocol risk metrics. Escherbridge earns 18% royalty on Fractal line-item license revenue.

Systems integrator channel for civic. Deloitte GPS ($200–$450/hr GSA schedule; senior $500–$700/hr), Accenture Federal Services ($190–$420/hr GSA MAS), Booz Allen Hamilton ($187–$376/hr validated GSA 2024). These firms carry the delivery capacity, pre-existing agency relationships, and FedRAMP program management experience required for state and federal engagements. PrimusNeo positions as the product the SI wraps — not competitive with the SI, which brings program management, organizational change, and agency credibility that PrimusNeo does not have at early stage.

DAO co-sell partners. Llama, Karpatkey, and Steakhouse Financial define the treasury services market. PrimusNeo does not compete with their treasury management service — NEOS is the governance fabric beneath their work, making it more auditable, more attack-resistant, and more institutionally credible. The co-sell motion: PrimusNeo + Llama (or Karpatkey) as a combined offering where the treasury services firm handles financial strategy and PrimusNeo handles governance infrastructure. Both parties benefit: the services firm gets a defensible audit trail; PrimusNeo gets the treasury services firm’s client relationships.

8. Revisions

Version Date Author Change
0.1 2026-05-23 Founding team + research squad Initial Governance engagement model

Healthcare — Engagement Model

Vertical: Healthcare (hospitals, health systems, payers, pharma, biotech, public health, digital health) Research brief: ../research/healthcare.md Anchors: ../01-pricing-model.md, ../research/layer-comparables.md

1. The friction-cost anchor

Healthcare is PrimusNeo’s highest-friction, highest-value vertical. The integration tax alone is staggering at scale.

EHR implementation and integration spend:

  • Epic full IDN deployment: $50M–$1.2B all-in (Trinity Health ~$800M, Northwell ~$1.2B, AdventHealth ~$660M)
  • EHR implementation services at large IDNs: $50M–$500M over 3–7 years via Deloitte/Accenture/Huron/Chartis
  • Redox interop fabric: floor of $35K/month (~$420K/yr), plus $0.50–$2.00/message — a $1.3M+ 3-year floor before volume
  • HL7/FHIR custom integration at regional health systems: $2M–$15M per major initiative
  • Clinical data platforms (Innovaccer, Health Catalyst, Arcadia): $200K–$3M+/yr for mid-to-large systems

Compliance gate costs:

  • HITRUST i1 certification (minimum viable for enterprise RFPs): ~$70K
  • HITRUST r2 (expected for core platforms): $100K–$500K upfront; $400K–$800K per 3-year cycle
  • HIPAA Security Rule overhaul (effective May 2026): MFA, universal PHI encryption, 24-hr breach, annual pen testing — material uplift to every vendor’s compliance COGS

Analytics and population health:

  • PMPM analytics: $0.50–$3 PMPM basic, $5–$20 PMPM population health, $150–$200 PMPM intensive care management
  • Veeva Vault CRM (pharma): $1,500–$1,800/user/yr list; top-20 pharma $5M–$10M+/yr
  • IQVIA data licenses: multi-million-dollar annual contracts for pharma analytics

The aggregate friction window for the vertical: An 800-bed regional system typically runs $5M–$15M over 3 years on interoperability alone (not counting the EHR license), plus $600K–$3M/yr on analytics platforms, plus $150K–$500K/yr on governance and consent. A national IDN running Epic adds $20M–$80M over 3 years on integration and data services above and beyond the EHR license itself. PrimusNeo’s capture target: 8–20% of the integration-and-analytics friction spend (per 01-pricing-model.md §9).

2. PrimusNeo positioning per layer in healthcare

The central positioning principle for this vertical: “We wrap Epic, we don’t replace it.” No hospital that spent $300M–$1.2B on Epic will entertain a rip-and-replace conversation. The winning motion wraps the EHR and compresses the integration tax.

AMP — Spatial OS: hospital operations and OR/ED command center

AMP is the OR/ED command center spatial layer — the GE HealthCare Command Center Digital Twin analog without forcing rip-and-replace of Epic or Cerner. GE Command Center covers ~500 hospitals and 55K+ beds globally; AMP’s differentiation is offline-first operation, Hexon portability, and native composition with NEOS governance events and Fractal clinical-intelligence signals.

Healthcare-specific AMP use cases:

  • OR command center: Surgical scheduling, room readiness, equipment asset tracking — spatial events become first-class Fractal signals (block utilization, turnaround forecasting)
  • ED throughput management: Patient flow visibility attached to spatial positions (bed, bay, zone); alert surfaces to charge nurse dashboard
  • Facility/campus management: HVAC, equipment maintenance, lockout/tagout — AMP + NEOS as the governed spatial operations layer

Pricing mechanic in healthcare: per-bed or per-facility ELA. For a 4-hospital regional system (800 beds total), AMP Mid at $80K–$250K/yr is positioned against GE Command Center’s custom-enterprise pricing with a clear “same outcome, TEFCA-native, no EHR displacement” message.

NEOS — Governance: consent, IRB, data governance, EHDS-ready

NEOS is the consent + IRB + change-control governance layer that satisfies HIPAA, HITRUST i1/r2, and EU EHDS (Reg EU 2025/327) simultaneously. The CCO and Privacy Officer are the primary buyers here.

Healthcare-specific NEOS use cases:

  • Patient consent management: Signed, auditable consent events per HIPAA and GDPR special-category health rules; DPIA workflow native to NEOS governance design
  • IRB governance: Research protocol approvals, amendment tracking, multi-site coordination (hospital + university + sponsor) as nested councils
  • Data-sharing governance: Multi-party data use agreements for TEFCA QHINs, state HIEs, payer-provider contracts — signed event log satisfies audit requirements
  • EHDS compliance: EU EHR interoperability and security certification requirements (Jan 2026 EHR cert deadline; March 2029 priority data exchange mandate) are addressable via NEOS consent and governance schema — a direct new revenue opportunity for EU-operating health systems and pharma

NEOS pricing in healthcare: per-covered-life ($0.10–$1.00/covered-life/yr) for payer/population contexts; per-council ($1.5K–$8K/council/yr) for hospital governance; enterprise governance ELA for national IDNs. OmniOne is the steward and primary delivery partner for NEOS governance design using their ETHOS methodology.

AZOA — Interoperability Fabric: TEFCA-native FHIR, the direct Redox differentiator

AZOA is the TEFCA-native FHIR fabric that compresses the Epic/Cerner integration tax. This is PrimusNeo’s most direct displacement vector in healthcare — Redox charges a $35K/month floor before any volume; AZOA at Mid tier ($80K–$250K/yr) undercuts Redox by 35–60% at equivalent integration scope while adding QHIN-native federation and Web3 identity anchoring that Redox does not offer.

The AZOA healthcare pitch to CIOs in one sentence: “Epic FHIR R4 certified, App Orchard listed, TEFCA QHIN-native — and we cost less than Redox at scale.”

Healthcare-specific AZOA use cases:

  • Epic FHIR R4 + Cerner FHIR connectors: Fixed-fee $120K–$400K per EHR connector (includes HITRUST overhead per 03-services-catalog.md §2.2); connector library reuse amortizes for subsequent customers
  • TEFCA QHIN participation: AZOA as the Common Agreement v2.1-compliant exchange fabric; 21,086 orgs live on TEFCA as of Jan 2025 — a massive TAM for interop-as-a-service
  • Claims and SDoH data ingest: Payer claims feeds, social determinants data pipelines — AZOA handles transport; Fractal handles population analytics
  • Pharmacy, lab, imaging integration: Outbound/inbound HL7 v2 → FHIR transformation via AZOA connector; replaces brittle in-house ETL

Critical path milestone: AZOA must achieve Epic FHIR R4 certification + App Orchard listing before any large IDN sale is closeable. The 6–18 month Epic technical review is a known friction point — begin immediately.

AZOA is the steward for AZOA and leads connector development; they are the delivery partner for Epic/Cerner/Meditech/Athenahealth connector engineering.

Fractal Engine — Intelligence: population health, RWE, payer analytics, pharma

Fractal is the population health + RWE + operational analytics layer that doesn’t require ripping out Innovaccer, Health Catalyst, or Arcadia. The canonical worked example is the Mid Fractal+AZOA regional health system engagement documented in layers/fractal-engine.md §7.1 — reproduced and extended in §3 below.

Healthcare-specific Fractal use cases:

  • Population health / value-based care: Risk stratification, gap-in-care identification, utilization analytics — PMPM-priced for payer and VBC contexts
  • Real-world evidence (RWE) for pharma/biotech: Retrospective cohort analysis, comparative effectiveness, safety signal detection using Escherbridge’s public corpus + customer EHR data. Fractal’s per-research-query mechanic ($5–$500/query) is appropriate for pharma buyers who need episodic deep analytics rather than always-on population monitoring
  • OR/ED operational intelligence: Fractal attached to AMP spatial events — block utilization forecasting, ED throughput prediction, workforce demand signals
  • Payer analytics: Fraud, waste, and abuse detection; member risk scoring; network adequacy analytics at $0.50–$8 PMPM (well below the $15–$200 PMPM industry range)

EU MDR consideration: If Fractal outputs constitute clinical decision support (Class IIa/IIb), EU MDR conformity assessment applies — $20K–$80K per high-risk system, scoped into the engagement deliverable. Position Fractal outputs as decision-support, not autonomous clinical action, to maintain Class I/II SaMD pathway where possible.

Escherbridge is the steward and primary delivery partner for Fractal ML engineering and RWE model development; their specialty in population health and operational telemetry makes healthcare one of their highest-density vertical use cases.

3. Engagement tiers — Small / Mid / Large

Small: Community Hospital (50–200 beds)

Profile: Critical access hospital or community hospital on Meditech Expanse or Cerner; limited IT staff; TEFCA adoption early but driven by state HIE mandates; analytics budget $200K–$600K/yr.

Recommended bundle: AZOA Small + Fractal Small + NEOS Small (Triad)

Worked quote:

AZOA Small:      $40K/yr   (TEFCA + state HIE + 2 external feeds, 5 integrations)
Fractal Small:  $45K/yr   (departmental utilization analytics, 3 data sources, <1TB)
NEOS Small:     $10K/yr   (consent management, 1 council, <50 clinical governance members)
                ──────
Subtotal:       $95K/yr
Triad -18%:     $78K/yr
Y1 services     (~1.8x blend, integration-heavy): $140K
                  (AZOA: Meditech FHIR $120K + state HIE $60K — discounted as 2-connector bundle)
                  (Fractal: ontology + dashboard $50K; NEOS: governance design $30K)
Y1 upkeep       (Gold SLA, healthcare default, 25%): $20K (starts Y2)
Infra           (HITRUST-compliant cloud surcharge applies): $18K–$30K/yr
                ──────
Y1 TOTAL:       ~$240K–$255K
3-yr TCO:       ~$550K–$650K

Friction-cost anchor: Redox floor alone is $420K/yr; community hospital state HIE integration historically $500K–$2M one-time. Meditech/Cerner analytics supplemented with a standalone tool runs $200K–$600K/yr. Combined 3-yr friction: $1.5M–$3M.

Capture rate: $650K ÷ $2.25M midpoint = ~29% — high but defensible because AZOA is genuinely displacing Redox and the standalone analytics spend, not merely layering on.

Mid: Regional System (200–2,000 beds) — Canonical Example

Profile: 4-hospital regional system, ~800 beds, 200K-life Medicare Advantage payer arm. On Epic at lead hospital, Cerner at acquired hospital. CDO has a population health mandate; CIO wants to reduce the Redox bill and close TEFCA gaps; CCO is watching the May 2026 HIPAA Security Rule overhaul.

This is the canonical example from layers/fractal-engine.md §7.1, extended to the full-stack quote:

Recommended bundle: Fractal Mid + AZOA Mid + NEOS Mid (Triad, with AMP Mid as Y2 upsell for OR command center)

Worked quote:

Fractal Mid:    $264K/yr  (PMPM mechanic: $1.10 PMPM × 200K MA lives; 12 data sources, 8TB)
AZOA Mid:        $140K/yr  (10 integrations: Epic FHIR + Cerner FHIR + 3 claims + 2 SDoH + 3 internal)
NEOS Mid:       $55K/yr   (consent management + IRB councils + data-sharing governance; 6 councils)
                ──────
Subtotal:       $459K/yr
Triad -18%:     $376K/yr
Y1 services     (~1.6x blend): $600K
                  (Epic FHIR connector $200K, Cerner FHIR $180K,
                   ontology + population health models $100K,
                   consent governance design OmniOne $70K, dashboards $50K)
Y1 upkeep       (Gold SLA, 25%): $94K (starts Y2)
Infra           (HITRUST-compliant cloud, multi-AZ): $80K–$180K/yr
                ──────
Y1 TOTAL:       ~$1.1M–$1.2M
3-yr TCO:       ~$2.5M–$2.9M

Friction-cost anchor: Per the research brief, interop fabric at this scale runs $5M–$15M over 3 years (Redox at $35K/mo alone is $1.26M over 3 years, before volume). Innovaccer/Arcadia population health platform $600K–$3M/yr. Combined Y1 displacement opportunity: $1.5M–$3M/yr. 3-yr friction: $4.5M–$9M.

Capture rate: $376K ÷ $2.2M midpoint (Y1 friction) = ~17% value share — defensible inside the 8–20% healthcare capture band per 01-pricing-model.md §9.

Y2 expansion path: Add AMP Mid ($150K/yr) for OR command center — the spatial event signal enhances Fractal’s block utilization forecasting. Full-stack 3-yr TCO rises to ~$3.8M–$4.5M, still well inside the $8M–$25M Mid-tier band.

Large: National IDN, AMC, or Top-20 Pharma

Profile: 40-hospital IDN on Epic (full Cosmos data-sharing participant) OR top-20 pharma with enterprise RWE program. IT capital committee approval required. Decision cycle 12–18 months. SI partners (Deloitte Health, Accenture Health, Optum Advisory) actively involved.

Recommended bundle: Full Stack (AMP Large + NEOS Large + AZOA Large + Fractal Large)

Worked quote (IDN scenario):

AMP Large:      $600K/yr  (25 hospitals, OR command center + ED spatial, ~$24K/facility)
NEOS Large:     $280K/yr  (enterprise consent + IRB + data governance, EHDS-ready)
