Head of Safety is the non-negotiable stakeholder in regulated robotics. Any engagement touching industrial cobots (ISO 10218 HRC cells), surgical arms (FDA 510(k) SaMD exposure), or drone fleets (FAA Part 107 waiver contexts) that does not have the compliance posture doc in hand by end of discovery is a stalled deal.

Sales Motions

RaaS opex motion. The fastest path for warehouse AMR and security patrol contexts. Position PrimusNeo as an add-on line item to the existing RaaS operator invoice. “You’re already paying $3,000/robot/month to Locus. We add $300 for cross-site spatial coordination, safety attestation traceability, and predictive maintenance — and we target cutting your unplanned downtime rate by 15%, which on a 200-robot fleet at $15K/incident is $540K/yr.” Requires a partnership or referral relationship with the RaaS operator or a direct connection to the COO who signs the RaaS contract.

Pilot-to-fleet funnel. Three-to-six month fixed-fee pilot at $50K–$150K (Atom or single-site Molecule SKU) covering AZOA connector buildout for 1–2 robot vendors + Fractal telemetry baseline for 1 facility. Pilot outputs: (a) connector library reusable across customer fleet, (b) Fractal downtime baseline that becomes the gainshare measurement period, (c) reference site for multi-site expansion pitch. McKinsey cites 70% pilot-to-fleet failure — Tie Town reference architecture is the counter-narrative that shortens the sales cycle from 9–18 months to 4–8 months.

OEM embedded licensing. Target ABB, FANUC, KUKA, Universal Robots, Boston Dynamics — embed AZOA connector library + Fractal as licensed modules in their fleet management software or cloud portal. Revenue share or per-robot-per-year royalty at scale. Entry: technical partnership agreement + co-sell on OEM’s existing deals. This is the highest-leverage distribution channel for reaching 10,000+ robot deployments without proportional services overhead.

5. Rejection-Risk Mitigations

“You’re not ISO 10218 / EU AI Act certified.” Mitigation: Compliance posture document delivered as an engagement artifact, not a post-sale promise. Fractal audit logs are architected as cert-ready evidence from day one — the telemetry schema maps to the traceability requirements in ISO 10218-1:2025 Section 5.4 and EU AI Act Article 12 logging requirements. NEOS safety attestation councils produce signed governance events that TUV/Intertek reviewers can audit. The posture doc is a deal gate, not a nice-to-have.

“We’re already on NVIDIA Isaac + ROS 2. We don’t want to fork the ROS graph.” Mitigation: AZOA is additive. It runs alongside Isaac without touching the ROS 2 DDS layer. The AZOA connector subscribes to ROS topics as a standard subscriber; it does not require modifying the robot’s navigation stack or mission planner. AMP sits above the Isaac fleet manager — it consumes VDA5050 fleet state as a data source. The positioning is “coordination intelligence above Isaac, not instead of Isaac.”

“Every robot vendor we run needs a custom integration. That’s expensive and risky.” Mitigation: Explicit per-connector fixed-fee scoping ($25K–$80K per connector) before any engagement begins. Connector scope, timeline, and acceptance criteria are defined in the SOW. PrimusNeo retains rights to harden non-bespoke connectors and redistribute through the AZOA connector library — which means connector cost amortizes across multiple customers and future connector upgrades are covered under sustained upkeep. For a fleet with 3 vendors, the connector line is $75K–$180K one-time, not an open-ended integration tax.

“We can’t calculate ROI per robot.” Mitigation: Fractal’s 60-day instrumentation phase (pre-contract measurement period, optionally part of the pilot) establishes the downtime baseline. The gainshare addendum structure (base license at 80% of standard anchor + 15–30% of documented annualized savings above baseline) converts the ROI question from a sales conversation into a contractual obligation. One avoided incident in a logistics AMR fleet at $15K–$50K converts the baseline math on its own.

“We don’t have robotics engineers to manage another platform.” Mitigation: Managed deployment service (all four services classes — customization, integration, deployment, sustained upkeep — delivered by PrimusNeo + steward partners). NVIDIA partner channel (Isaac/Omniverse certified, $200–$350/hr; projects $250K–$2M) and AWS partner network (post-RoboMaker migration projects, $100K–$500K) provide certified SI capacity that extends the engagement delivery capacity without requiring customer headcount.

“Humanoids are the future but we don’t know when they’ll be commercial.” Mitigation: Do not over-index pricing or engagement design on humanoid robotics as of 2026. Figure, 1X, Agility Robotics, and Boston Dynamics Atlas are pre-commercial at enterprise fleet scale. Humanoid positioning is appropriate for research and pilot agreements only; commercial fleet pricing models are premature. Track the space; activate humanoid-specific AZOA connector development when a reference customer has a commercial fleet above 20 units.

6. Regulatory and Compliance Considerations

Robotics sits under more regulatory overlay than any other PrimusNeo vertical except healthcare. Every engagement touching industrial cobots, surgical robotics, drones, or service robots in the EU must have a compliance posture document as a named engagement deliverable.

ISO 10218-1:2025 / -2:2025 (Industrial Robots and Robot Cells) Updated 2025 revision strengthens functional safety, HRC (Human-Robot Collaboration), and force/power limiting requirements for cobot cells. Certification via TUV Rheinland, Intertek, or UL; 7–12 month timeline; $50K–$200K+ per certified cell including engineering hours.

PrimusNeo contribution: AMP provides safety-zone geofencing that maps directly to ISO 10218-2:2025 collaborative workspace definitions. Fractal audit logs provide the traceability evidence that notified body reviewers require. NEOS safety attestation governance events provide the change-control record for cell modifications.

ISO 13482:2014 (Personal Care and Service Robots, including Humanoids) Applies to robots operating in proximity to humans in non-industrial settings: service robots, hospital logistics robots, and humanoids. Requires notified body engagement for EU market access.

PrimusNeo contribution: NEOS hazard accountability traceability — every human-proximity event, safety intervention, and operational exception becomes a signed governance event with replay capability for post-incident analysis.

EU AI Act (2024, enforcement 2026) Fractal Engine, when processing operational decision data for robot navigation, fleet dispatch, or safety intervention recommendations, is classified as a high-risk AI system under Annex III (safety component of products covered by Union harmonisation legislation, which includes robots under the Machinery Regulation).

Requirements: conformity assessment, Article 12 logging (full audit trail of all inputs and outputs), Article 14 human oversight mechanisms, technical documentation per Annex IV.

PrimusNeo contribution: Fractal audit log architecture satisfies Article 12. NEOS human oversight councils satisfy Article 14. Conformity assessment documentation: $20K–$80K per high-risk system, bundled into the engagement deliverable as a named SOW item, not post-sale.

FAA Part 107 (Commercial Drones <25kg, US) Knowledge test $175 + $5/drone registration. 90–120 day waiver for operations beyond visual line of sight (BVLOS). UTM (Unmanned Traffic Management) API integration is required for BVLOS commercial fleet operations.

PrimusNeo contribution: AZOA UTM API integration as a drone-vertical connector class. Priced per-connector ($30K–$60K fixed-fee) within the standard AZOA connector framework.

FDA 510(k) (Surgical Robotics, Class II Medical Devices) FDA submission fee: $6,084–$24,335 (2025 fee schedule) + $20K–$30K consulting. 142-day median review period. Fractal Engine, if processing clinical decision data (e.g., surgical performance analytics, real-time guidance signals), may qualify as Software as a Medical Device (SaMD) under FDA guidance and require 510(k) clearance or De Novo classification.

PrimusNeo contribution: Fractal’s telemetry architecture for surgical robotics is scoped to operational performance analytics (robot arm telemetry, procedure time, maintenance prediction) rather than clinical decision support, to stay below the SaMD trigger. Any surgical customer requiring clinical decision support integration must have a regulatory counsel review prior to engagement scoping. This is a deal gate.

RIA TR R15.806 (US Human-Robot Collaboration Technical Report) Not a certification standard — an internal compliance reference. Fractal risk signals and AMP spatial zone mapping support HRC safety documentation under this technical report.

7. Steward and Service Partner Notes

Layer Stewards in Robotics

PLAN Systems (AMP) leads AMP fleet spatial coordination buildout. PLAN’s Hexon file standard is the portable spatial format for robot cell layouts, AMR path maps, and multi-site fleet spatial models. PLAN subcontracts site-walk, spatial environment buildout, and sensor/OT binding services within robotics engagements.

Escherbridge (Fractal Engine) leads Fractal predictive maintenance and custom robot model development. Escherbridge’s analytics depth is the differentiator for the robotics vertical — their team has domain context in time-series anomaly detection on robot actuator telemetry that makes the custom model training materially faster than a generalist Fractal deployment. Escherbridge royalty: 18% of Fractal line-item license revenue.

OmniOne (NEOS) leads fleet governance and safety attestation council design. OmniOne’s ETHOS methodology is adapted for robotics safety governance: ISO 10218 cell approval councils, EU AI Act human oversight councils, and RaaS SLA governance councils are all deliverable templates OmniOne can instantiate within a standard Governance Design Workshop ($35K–$60K).

AZOA (AZOA) leads robot vendor connector buildouts — the critical work in every robotics engagement. Every robot vendor added to an AZOA deployment requires a custom connector authored and maintained by AZOA. The connector library (FANUC KAREL, ABB RAPID, KUKA KRL, UR URScript, Boston Dynamics API, Symbotic API, DJI drone API, Intuitive Surgical telemetry) is the strategic moat that makes AZOA the right choice for heterogeneous fleets. AZOA royalty: 15% of AZOA line-item license revenue.

Service Partner Channel

Robotics SI firms (US boutique): $150–$275/hr specialist; $100–$175/hr mid-level. These firms (Bastian Solutions, Honeywell Intelligrated, Dematic, and boutique AMR integrators) are the warm channel into mid-size fleet operators who do not have internal robotics engineering capacity. SI partnership = co-sell arrangement; PrimusNeo provides the coordination layer, SI provides the robot hardware + OEM relationship.

NVIDIA partner network (Isaac/Omniverse certified): $200–$350/hr; fixed-scope projects $250K–$2M. NVIDIA’s certified partner channel is the fastest path into large OEM and enterprise fleet operator deals where Isaac Sim and Omniverse are already in the stack. PrimusNeo’s positioning as “coordination intelligence above Isaac” makes this a complementary co-sell, not a competitive displacement.

AWS partner network (post-RoboMaker migration): $150–$250/hr; projects $100K–$500K. The AWS RoboMaker deprecation (September 2025) created a migration cohort of robotics teams that need a new cloud robotics runtime strategy. AZOA as the vendor-neutral integration layer for AWS Batch + Isaac Sim on EC2 G6e is a specific positioning play for this migration cohort.

0.1 2026-05-23 Founding team + research squad Initial Robotics engagement model

Technology — Engagement Model

Vertical: Technology (software vendors, platforms, AI/ML companies, infra providers, telcos, gaming/metaverse, devtool ecosystems) Research brief: ../research/technology.md Anchors: ../01-pricing-model.md, ../research/layer-comparables.md

1. The friction-cost anchor

Technology buyers are the sharpest cost-aware purchasers in any PrimusNeo vertical — they build the tools that other industries buy. But their internal coordination stack carries real, quantifiable friction:

IDP / developer platform spend. Managed IDP pricing has converged at $22–$78/developer/month (Roadie $22, Port Enterprise $60–$78, Cortex $65–$69, OpsLevel $39). These look cheap per seat until headcount scales. A 300-engineer scale-up pays $79K–$281K/yr for portal seats alone — before platform engineering salaries. The counter-anchor is self-hosted Backstage, which appears free but carries 3–5 senior platform engineers at $250K fully loaded = $750K–$1.25M/yr in carrying cost plus $12K–$24K/yr infra. Three-year Backstage TCO regularly exceeds $2M at scale, a figure Roadie, Port, and Humanitec all cite in competitive decks.

AI agent infrastructure. LLMOps tooling (LangSmith Plus at $39/seat/mo, Weights & Biases at $50/seat/mo) adds $30K–$80K/yr for a 50-engineer org running active AI programs, before compute. GPU inference via managed serverless (Modal A10G $1.10/hr, H100 $3.95/hr; Replicate H100 $5.49/hr) introduces high per-experiment cost and zero commitment visibility. NVIDIA Omniverse Enterprise benchmarks GPU-level infrastructure at $4,500/GPU/year — the spatial and metaverse/gaming sub-segment pays this directly.

Compliance as a selection criterion. Per BCG 2026: 73% of enterprise AI initiatives cite compliance posture as a top-three vendor selection criterion, up from 41% in 2024. CAIO roles now exist in 61% of large enterprises, making compliance a named buyer with budget. SOC 2 Type II runs $35K–$150K in year one; ISO 27001 initial certification $25K–$80K; FedRAMP Moderate/High $500K–$2M+. EU AI Act full application August 2026 (high-risk Annex III potentially deferred to December 2027 under the AI Omnibus) adds conformity-assessment cost ($20K–$80K per high-risk system) for any platform with AI decision-support outputs.

Compute-per-customer friction. Multi-tenant SaaS platforms and AI infra companies face a per-customer coordination problem: governance of compute allocation, model versioning, multi-tenant observability, and cross-org federation that neither Backstage nor MuleSoft nor LangSmith was designed to solve in combination. That coordination gap is the technology vertical’s primary PrimusNeo friction window.

Friction-cost anchor summary (3-yr): $130K–$340K for a 50-engineer startup; $550K–$1.5M for a 50–500-engineer scale-up; $2.3M–$7M+ for 500+ engineer enterprises, telcos, and hyperscalers. Large-tier buyers frequently embed these costs inside cloud EDP/CUD committed-use agreements at 20–40% effective discount off list — which means the cash friction is lower than the TCO number, but the procurement path is easier (existing cloud commitment absorbs new line items).

PrimusNeo capture band: 8–18% of platform engineering spend, per the cross-vertical pricing model.

2. PrimusNeo positioning per layer in technology

The technology vertical is the sharpest build-vs-buy context PrimusNeo will face. Buyers are themselves platform vendors, infrastructure builders, and developer-tooling companies — they have high tolerance for self-hosting and high skepticism of vendor lock-in. The engagement motion leads with PLG + OSS inbound, not with an enterprise RFP response.

AMP — Spatial OS. Lighter use in pure-software companies; heavier in metaverse/gaming studios, spatial-product companies, AR/VR platform vendors, telcos running physical network operations, and infra providers with data center and edge site coordination problems. For a pure-software SaaS vendor, AMP is not the primary entry layer. For a gaming company building a metaverse product, a telco managing 50K cell towers, or an AR SDK vendor running multiplayer spatial sessions, AMP is the differentiated operational layer — positioned against NVIDIA Omniverse Enterprise ($4,500/GPU/yr) and Unity Industry ($4,950/seat/yr) on price and against both on offline-first + federated governance characteristics.

NEOS — Governance. Highly relevant for telcos with subsidiary structures and post-merger integration challenges, multi-org devtool platforms (plugin ecosystems, marketplace governance), and AI/ML companies managing federated multi-org model governance (regulatory audit trails, cross-team contribution accounting). The CC-NC dual-license makes NEOS free for non-commercial OSS projects and open-source ecosystems — which is also the inbound funnel. Commercial deployment triggers the PCL. Position as the governance protocol layer above Snapshot/Tally (which handle signaling and execution but not durable contribution accounting or holonic federation).

AZOA — Interoperability Fabric. The primary entry layer for pure-software technology buyers. AZOA covers what MuleSoft covers for Web2 enterprise integration plus what Chainlink CCIP, LayerZero, and Okta cover individually — unified under one contract, with durable workflow settlement on top. For a fintech or AI platform vendor managing integrations across a partner ecosystem, AZOA is the Web2+Web3+identity+reputation fabric that no incumbent iPaaS covers. For a devtool company managing GitHub Enterprise, Jira, PagerDuty, Datadog, and Slack integrations plus emerging chain-based attestations, AZOA is the integration layer that replaces brittle custom glue code and cleans up the engineering debt before it compounds.

Fractal Engine — Intelligence. The LLMOps and agent observability layer — Palantir Foundry for federated orgs, without the services-heaviness. Positions below Palantir commercial entry ($250K–$500K/yr) and above commodity Databricks/Snowflake in the operational intelligence tier. For AI/ML companies, Fractal provides the multi-org agent observability, model performance tracking, and governance audit-trail intelligence that neither LangSmith nor W&B provides at the organizational/federated layer. The CC-NC dual-license makes it free for research and academic use, seeding adoption inside the ML engineering community that eventually becomes the enterprise champion.

Layer interaction for technology. The dominant bundle for technology buyers is AZOA + Fractal (integration fabric + intelligence), escalating to AZOA + NEOS + Fractal for multi-org governance programs and telco/hyperscaler deals. AMP enters primarily for gaming/metaverse/telco/spatial-product sub-segments. Full-stack is the right shape for a telco managing physical network operations + subsidiary governance + partner ecosystem integration + AI operations.

3. Engagement tiers — Small / Mid / Large

Small: <50-engineer single-product startup ($130K–$340K comparable 3-yr market TCO)

Profile: Early-stage SaaS or devtool company with a growing API/integration surface, early AI features, and no dedicated platform team. Spending $22–$39/seat/mo on IDP tooling and ad-hoc on AI observability. May have one Backstage installation abandoned after 60 days.

Bundle: AZOA Small + Fractal Small (Duet).

Worked quote:

AZOA Small:      $40K/yr  (8 integrations: GitHub, Jira, PagerDuty, Datadog, Slack, Okta, 2 custom REST)
Fractal Small:  $45K/yr  (4 data sources: event stream, logs, model traces, deployment telemetry)
Duet bundle:    -10% → $76.5K/yr license
Y1 services     (~2x blend for AZOA-heavy: $153K — connector buildout $80K, ontology mapping $50K, deployment $23K)
Y1 upkeep       (Silver SLA, 22%, starts Y2): $17K/yr
Infra (pass-through + 12%): $18K–$30K/yr
─────────────────────────────────────────
Y1 total:       ~$247K–$259K
3-yr TCO:       ~$390K–$430K

Friction anchor: $22–$39/dev/mo IDP seats ($13K–$23K/yr) + LangSmith Plus for 5 active AI engineers ($2.3K/yr) + ad-hoc integration debt (estimated 0.5 FTE at $200K loaded = $100K/yr in maintenance engineering time). Direct friction ~$115K–$125K/yr; PrimusNeo 3-yr cost lands inside comparable market band with meaningfully lower integration debt ongoing.

Capture rate: $76.5K/yr ÷ $120K/yr friction midpoint = ~64% of direct friction displaced — justified because AZOA eliminates a category of ongoing maintenance engineering work, not just a seat license.

Cloud EDP note: At this tier, buyers rarely have cloud EDPs; pricing is straightforward.

Mid: 50–500-engineer scale-up ($550K–$1.5M comparable 3-yr market TCO)

Profile: Growth-stage SaaS platform, AI infrastructure company, or devtool scale-up. Has a platform engineering team (2–4 engineers), is running Port or Cortex at $40–$65/seat/mo, and is actively evaluating LangSmith Enterprise or W&B for LLMOps. May have a nascent CAIO function.

Bundle: AZOA Mid + Fractal Mid + NEOS Mid (Triad) for multi-org coordination; or AZOA Mid + Fractal Mid (Duet) for single-org.

Worked quote (Triad, 200-engineer scale-up):

AZOA Mid:        $165K/yr  (25 integrations: GitHub Enterprise, Jira, PagerDuty, Datadog, Slack, Okta, 
                           3 chain connectors for on-chain attestations, 10 partner API connectors, 8 internal)
NEOS Mid:       $55K/yr   (3 councils: engineering guild, partner ecosystem governance, AI model governance)
Fractal Mid:    $200K/yr  (10 data sources: deployment pipeline, model telemetry, SLO/SLA signals, 
                           partner API usage, governance events)
Triad bundle:   -18% → $344K/yr license
Y1 services     (~2.0x blend: $688K — AZOA connectors $320K, Fractal ontology+pipeline $250K, NEOS governance design $118K)
Y1 upkeep       (Silver SLA, 22%, starts Y2): $76K/yr
Infra (pass-through + 12%): $60K–$120K/yr
─────────────────────────────────────────
Y1 total:       ~$1.09M–$1.15M
3-yr TCO:       ~$2.0M–$2.3M

Friction anchor: Port Enterprise at $65/seat/mo × 200 engineers = $156K/yr + 2 platform engineers at $250K loaded = $500K/yr + W&B at $50/seat/mo × 20 AI engineers = $12K/yr + LangSmith Enterprise custom ~$30K/yr. Estimated direct stack: $698K/yr before custom integration debt. Comparable market 3-yr TCO $550K–$1.5M — PrimusNeo Y1 lands at the high end of comparable 3-yr range, which is defensible only with a clear friction-displacement demonstration in the discovery sprint. The AZOA connector audit should identify at least 2 FTE-equivalent of integration maintenance engineering being displaced (at $200K loaded each = $400K/yr), which repositions the capture to ~49% value share — inside the 8–18% band for pure platform spend but reasonable when engineering time is included.

Cloud EDP note: Mid-tier scale-ups often have AWS or GCP EDPs with 20–30% discounts. Frame PrimusNeo as eligible for cloud marketplace procurement to absorb into existing EDP at effective ~20–25% net discount off the above quote.

Large: 500+ engineer FAANG/MAANG/telco/hyperscaler ($2.3M–$7M+ comparable 3-yr market TCO)

Profile: MAANG or near-MAANG software company, Tier-1 telco (AT&T, Verizon, Deutsche Telekom, NTT), hyperscaler partner/platform, or major gaming/metaverse company (EA, Activision Blizzard, Roblox-adjacent). Has a full internal platform engineering organization (10–20 engineers), existing Humanitec or custom-built IDP, enterprise W&B + LangSmith or internal tooling, and a growing CAIO function.

Bundle: Full-stack (AMP + NEOS + AZOA + Fractal) for telco and metaverse/gaming sub-segments; AZOA + NEOS + Fractal (Triad) for pure-software large enterprise.

Worked quote (Full-Stack, Tier-1 telco, 2,000-engineer, 800 cell-tower sites):

AMP Large:      $900K/yr  (800 tower sites; physical network ops spatial coordination, per-site ~$1,125/yr)
NEOS Large:     $280K/yr  (federated subsidiary governance: 12 regional councils + partner ecosystem governance)
AZOA Large:      $750K/yr  (60+ integrations: OSS/BSS systems, partner APIs, chain-of-custody attestations,
                           multi-cloud, regulatory reporting endpoints, identity federation across subsidiaries)
Fractal Large:  $800K/yr  (25+ data sources: network telemetry, customer experience signals, governance events,
                           spatial events, LLMOps telemetry, regulatory audit trails)
Full Stack:     -25% → $2.03M/yr license
Y1 services     (~1.8x blend: $3.65M — AZOA connectors $1.2M, AMP site buildout $1.1M,
                 Fractal ontology+models $900K, NEOS governance design $450K)
Y1 upkeep       (Gold SLA, 25%, starts Y2): $508K/yr
Infra (pass-through + 12%): $600K–$1.2M/yr
─────────────────────────────────────────
Y1 total:       ~$6.3M–$6.9M
3-yr TCO:       ~$11M–$14M

Friction anchor: A Tier-1 telco running a comparable market stack pays $2.3M–$7M+ per year across Humanitec (~$2M/yr IDP for 2,000 engineers at scale), enterprise W&B + LangSmith ($200K+/yr), Omniverse Enterprise for network digital twin ($450K/yr, 100 GPUs), ISO 27001 + SOC 2 compliance overhead ($200K+/yr), and integration engineering maintenance (estimated 8 FTE at $220K loaded = $1.76M/yr). Combined friction window $4.8M–$9.5M/yr; PrimusNeo $2.03M capture = 21–42% value share — at the high end of defensible, appropriate given full-stack displacement depth.

Cloud EDP note: Tier-1 telcos and hyperscalers negotiate cloud EDPs at 30–40% discounts. PrimusNeo on cloud marketplace reduces effective cost to $1.2M–$1.5M/yr equivalent, which repositions the 3-yr TCO to $7M–$9M — firmly inside the $2.3M–$7M comparable band at the high end.

4. Industry-specific procurement signals

CTO (build-vs-buy for IDP + agent infra; entry via conference talks + OSS presence). The CTO or VP Engineering is the primary build-vs-buy authority in technology companies. They respond to architecture credibility, standards alignment (OpenTelemetry, CNCF, OpenUSD, W3C DID/VC), and federated multi-org deployment proof. They will not respond to a traditional enterprise deck — they want a GitHub repo, a runnable demo, and a peer reference from a respected engineering org. Entry via PlatformCon, KubeCon, QCon, or an AI engineering conference; not via analyst relations at this stage. The Humanitec 2023 DevOps Benchmarking finding is the right counter to the NIH objection: top-performing engineering orgs adopt hybrid buy+extend models, not full custom builds.

VP Platform Engineering / Head of DevEx (IDP budget + DORA metrics; entry via community + peer referral). This is the operational buyer with the IDP line item. They own DORA metrics: deploy frequency, change failure rate, mean time to restore. They model ROI in engineering time saved, typically at $150–$250K/engineer-year fully loaded. A 5% reduction in integration-related incidents across a 200-engineer org at $200K/engineer = $2M/yr in recovered engineering productivity — a number that makes a $344K/yr PrimusNeo license defensible without further math. Entry is through community (PlatformCon, Internal Developer Platform Slack, platform.sh/DORA report influence), not cold outreach.

Chief AI Officer / Head of AI (agent infra + LLMOps governance + data residency; entry via analyst relations + AI governance working groups). CAIO roles exist in 61% of large enterprises. This buyer owns the LLMOps stack (LangSmith, W&B), the governance audit trail (EU AI Act compliance), and the data residency posture (EU telcos, GDPR-adjacent scale-ups). Fractal + NEOS is the relevant product pair for this buyer — Fractal for agent observability and model intelligence, NEOS for the governance fabric that produces the signed audit trails the EU AI Act rewards. Entry via AI governance working groups (Partnership on AI, EU AI Office stakeholder engagement), analyst briefings (Gartner CAIO summit), and Escherbridge thought leadership in the LLMOps community.

Head of Developer Experience / PLG champion (hands-on before budget; entry via free tier + community). The PLG motion in devtools is well-established: Cursor reached $2B ARR by February 2026 on PLG inbound. OSS inbound (Backstage plugin, AZOA connector SDK, Fractal community tier under CC-NC) drives free team adoption → PQL-triggered outbound → VP Platform/CTO expansion. Baseline PLG free-to-paid conversion is ~9%; top quartile devtool companies reach 25–30% with PQL-triggered outbound (PrimusNeo target). The Head of DevEx is the internal PLG champion — they run the trial, build the proof of value, and bring the budget conversation upward.

5. Rejection-risk mitigations

Build-vs-buy / NIH syndrome. Engineering-led organizations, especially FAANG-adjacent, default to “our requirements are unique.” Counter with evidence: the Humanitec 2023 DevOps Benchmarking study found that top-performing engineering organizations use hybrid buy+extend models, not full custom builds. PrimusNeo’s open SDK architecture (AZOA connector SDK on MIT/Apache-2.0, AMP community runtime on Apache-2.0, NEOS + Fractal on CC-NC for non-commercial use) is explicitly designed for hybrid buy+extend — buyers can extend any layer without forking the commercial stack.

Vendor lock-in across four layers / four stewards. The federated architecture raises legitimate portability questions. Counter: open SDK across all layers (AZOA connector SDK open, AMP Hexon file standard open Apache-2.0, NEOS + Fractal CC-NC for non-commercial), CC-NC dual-licensing on NEOS and Fractal (so the governance schema and intelligence ontology are portable under the open license even if the commercial engine changes), non-negotiable data export guarantees in AZOA (machine-readable export at any time), and OpenUSD / CNCF / W3C DID standards alignment in AMP and AZOA respectively. Source escrow via Iron Mountain is available above $500K ARR for AMP and AZOA closed-core.

Open-source competition (Backstage, Crossplane, Flux, ArgoCD, OPA, MLflow, LangFuse). Every commercial PrimusNeo layer has credible OSS alternatives. Counter is not feature competition but TCO and time-to-value. Backstage self-hosted runs $2M+ over 3 years for large teams (3–5 senior platform engineers at $250K loaded). OPA is free but implementation engagements run $150–$300/hr. MLflow and LangFuse lack the federated multi-org governance and operational ontology layer that makes Fractal’s agent observability distinct from pure logging. Enterprise support SLA (Gold 24×7, 1-hour P1) is the final differentiator that OSS cannot match at scale.

Integration with existing devtools. GitHub Enterprise, Jira, PagerDuty, Datadog, Slack, Okta, and LaunchDarkly are non-negotiable for any technology buyer. The first 90 days of any engagement must demonstrate connector coverage for the buyer’s core toolchain. AZOA connector library includes these as standard connectors (CRM at $30K–$80K, Identity/IdP at $25K–$90K, Custom REST/GraphQL at $20K–$120K per the services catalog). Discovery sprint output is a connector coverage matrix showing which integrations are available immediately and which require buildout. Gap commitments go into the SOW with 90-day warranty.

Governance / data residency (EU telcos, post-GDPR scale-ups, AI Act compliance). EU-based technology companies — telcos, fintech platforms, AI companies subject to GPAI provisions — require in-region data processing and demonstrable EU AI Act readiness. AZOA supports multi-region deployment with data-residency routing baked into the federation engine. Fractal’s model governance and Escherbridge’s audit-trail architecture are designed to produce the conformity-assessment documentation that Annex III high-risk systems require. NEOS governance events are immutably signed — the audit trail is the product. Frame these not as compliance checkboxes but as the architecture choices that make EU AI Act conformity a 3-week exercise rather than a 6-month legal program.

Procurement cycle length. MAANG and Tier-1 telco enterprise procurement averages 6–18 months from first meeting to signed contract. PLG inbound shortens this: product usage data from the AZOA community SDK or Fractal CC-NC deployment substitutes for an RFP at the team level, escalating to procurement only after internal champions have demonstrated adoption. Target is VP Platform / Head of DevEx as the internal champion who drives usage above the PQL threshold before procurement is formally engaged.

6. Regulatory + compliance considerations

SOC 2 Type II is mandatory for any sale to US enterprise, govtech-adjacent, or financial-services technology company. Cost: $35K–$150K in year one with automation; $20K–$50K/yr ongoing. PrimusNeo SOC 2 Type II is funded as a central investment shared across verticals, not charged per-engagement.

ISO 27001 is expected by EU enterprise technology buyers and all telco customers. Initial certification $25K–$80K; $15K–$30K/yr surveillance audits. Required for any Capgemini- or Deutsche Telekom-adjacent deal in the EU market. Funded centrally.

FedRAMP Moderate/High is relevant for govtech-adjacent technology companies (defense-tech platforms, civic-tech SaaS, government-facing AI infrastructure). Cost: $500K–$2M+ to achieve; typically 12–18 months process. The post-2025 “20x acceleration” brings package completion to under 5 weeks once the authorization package is complete, but the package itself still requires the full investment. Recommended: pursue FedRAMP Moderate by 2027 for the govtech TAM.

EU AI Act enters full application August 2026. High-risk systems under Annex III (AI in critical infrastructure, employment, education, essential services) face conformity-assessment requirements potentially deferred to December 2027 under the AI Omnibus proposal. GPAI models above compute thresholds (initially ~10^25 FLOPs) face transparency and systemic-risk documentation requirements effective February 2025. Technology companies building on PrimusNeo’s Fractal Engine should document which AI outputs constitute high-risk decision support (Annex III) early — conformity assessment $20K–$80K per high-risk system is scoped as part of the engagement deliverable for any buyer where it applies.

EU Data Act / DSA / DMA apply to AZOA-class interoperability fabrics, platform gateways, and data portability mechanisms. Any PrimusNeo AZOA deployment for a platform company with EU operations above DSA/DMA thresholds should include legal review of data portability obligations and interoperability mandates. Initial posture advisory $50K–$200K per jurisdiction.

US AI EOs and dual-use foundation model disclosure apply to technology companies building foundation models above compute thresholds. Internal governance program cost $100K–$500K+. Fractal Engine’s audit-trail and governance-event architecture is positioned as the technical substrate that makes AI governance reporting tractable.

7. Steward + service partner notes

PLAN Systems (AMP steward, 15% royalty) leads AMP delivery for metaverse/gaming/telco/spatial-product company deployments. PLAN’s contribution is lightest in pure-software SaaS deals and heaviest in any engagement involving physical infrastructure (cell towers, data centers, studio sites) or spatial-product development (AR SDK vendors, game studios building shared spatial environments).

OmniOne (NEOS steward, 18% royalty) leads NEOS governance design using the ETHOS methodology. Particularly relevant for technology companies managing multi-org governance: telco subsidiaries, plugin/marketplace ecosystems, OSS foundation governance, and AI model governance councils. OmniOne’s ETHOS workshops ($35K–$60K for governance design, $25K–$80K for charter authoring) are typically the first named service that NEOS-entry buyers sign.

AZOA (AZOA steward, 15% royalty) leads connector development and federation engine deployment. For technology buyers, AZOA is the primary delivery partner for any devtool integration program — GitHub Enterprise, Jira, PagerDuty, Datadog, Okta connectors are built and maintained by the AZOA connector author community. The AZOA connector library reuse model means each new connector built for one technology buyer reduces the marginal cost of the next.

Escherbridge (Fractal steward, 18% royalty) leads Fractal ML engineering, custom model development, and agent observability architecture. For AI/ML company buyers, Escherbridge is the primary delivery partner for LLMOps instrumentation, model performance tracking, and multi-org agent observability. Escherbridge’s public corpus is the pretrained foundation that reduces custom model training cost by 40–60% relative to training from scratch — a material time-to-value advantage the CAIO buyer will quantify.

SI channel for technology large deals. Large technology accounts (telcos, hyperscaler-adjacent) enter through SI partners who frame PrimusNeo inside a broader transformation program:

PLG conversion targets: 9% free-to-paid baseline; 25–30% top quartile with PQL-triggered outbound once AZOA community SDK or Fractal CC-NC tier installs exceed internal adoption thresholds.

Layer Comparables — Cross-Cutting Market Research

Purpose: Pricing benchmarks for the four PrimusNeo product layers (AMP, NEOS, AZOA, Fractal Engine) to anchor the value-based commercial pricing model. Date: 2026-05-23

Layer 1 (AMP) — Spatial OS / 3D Collaboration

Pricing Benchmarks

NVIDIA Omniverse Enterprise - $4,500 per GPU per year (unit is the GPU running the simulation server, not named seat) - Small studio: 2–4 GPUs = $9K–$18K/yr for compute licensing alone, before add-ons

Unity Industry / Industrial Collection - $4,950/seat/yr (post-2025 price revision; was ~$4,500) - Includes Pixyz, Unity Mars, Unity Cloud, DevOps tooling - Unity Enterprise (non-industry): ~$2,200/seat/yr after 8% increase effective Jan 2025

Unreal Engine Enterprise - $1,850/seat/yr for enterprise seat license — notably lower than Unity, positioned aggressively for industrial/simulation deals

Spatial.io - Business tier (up to 50 users/server, 20 servers, analytics): public listing but $ behind sales conversation - Enterprise tier (1,000+ concurrent, custom games/XR, dedicated support, advanced security): custom - Mozilla Hubs shut down May 31, 2024 — no longer a comparable

Improbable / Hadean - Both pivot to defense simulation + game multiplayer infra - Public pricing not disclosed; enterprise engagements are six-figure minimums

Service Multiplier

  • Typical 1.5–3x license (heavy SI deployment)

Layer 2 (NEOS) — Governance / Councils / Holonic Orgs

Pricing Benchmarks

Aragon / DAOhaus / Snapshot / Tally — All free or near-free at baseline: - Aragon: no subscription; L2 deployment gas $50–$200 one-time; custom smart contract buildouts $20K–$100K+ if bespoke - DAOhaus: entirely free, OSS, forkable - Snapshot: free for all off-chain voting; powers 96% of DAOs + 35,000+ communities; gas-free by design - Tally: free self-serve Governor deployer; enterprise/white-glove DAO launch via biz@tally.xyz (no public pricing)

Diligent Boards / Nasdaq Boardvantage - Diligent: $12K–$60K+/yr depending on board size, features, org type. Per Vendr, 20–30% discounts routinely negotiated on multi-year - Nasdaq Boardvantage: starting ~$12K–$15K/yr for small boards - OnBoard: affordable alt; similarly quote-based, often 20–40% below Diligent list

Microsoft Viva - Employee Communications & Communities: $2.00/user/mo - Workplace Analytics & Employee Feedback: $6.00/user/mo - Viva Suite (full): $12.00/user/mo ($144/seat/yr)

Loomio - Per-group subscription model (not per-seat) - Nonprofit/cooperative discount; enterprise/custom domain custom-quoted - Indicative: $99–$599/month per organization depending on member count

Service Multiplier

  • Typical 0.5–1x license (lighter services, mostly config/training)

Layer 3 (AZOA) — Interoperability Fabric

Pricing Benchmarks

MuleSoft Anypoint Platform - $1,250/vCore/month on-demand - Min production deployment ~1 vCore = $15K/yr entry, but real enterprise starts $80K–$250K+/yr - Large enterprise: commonly $500K–$2M+/yr under multi-year ELA - Services-to-license: 2–3x Year 1 (impl, DataWeave dev, architecture) - Total Y1 TCO mid-market: $350K–$600K+

Boomi - Professional ~$550/month ($6,600/yr) for lower volumes - Mid-market: $30K–$150K/yr - Enterprise: six figures custom - Services multiple: 1–2x vs MuleSoft’s 2–3x

Workato - Entry: ~$10K/yr (SMB automation) - Enterprise: 20–30% lower than MuleSoft for equivalent volumes ($60K–$1.5M+ depending on recipe volume) - Services: 1–2x license

Blockchain interoperability (Chainlink CCIP, LayerZero, Axelar, Wormhole) - None publish per-tx list prices; costs denominated in gas + relayer fees (network-condition-dependent) - Axelar processed $8.66B of transfers + 1.85M transactions by May 2024 across 64 chains - Enterprise SLA (e.g., Chainlink BUILD) negotiated directly - Cost advantage flows to LayerZero + Wormhole for high-frequency use cases; CCIP holds mid-cost position with stronger security guarantees

Decentralized storage (Pinata / Web3.Storage) - Pinata: ~$0.15/GB/month on paid plans; enterprise custom - Web3.Storage: free tier wound down; now enterprise-focused custom SLAs - Ceramic (identity/data streams): no public pricing; developer-tier free on hosted node; self-hosted OSS

Service Multiplier

  • 2–3x Year 1 (connector buildout, architecture); 0.5–1x maintenance from Y2

Layer 4 (Fractal Engine) — Intelligence / Analytics / Operational Telemetry

Pricing Benchmarks

Palantir Foundry - No public per-seat price. Enterprise ACV: $1M–$10M+/yr for gov; commercial mid-market entry $250K–$500K/yr - Dimensions: compute-seconds + storage (GB-months) + Ontology (GB-months) + named-user software licenses bundled into ELA - Gross margin: 80.25% (FY2024) on combined platform - Services-to-license: bundles O&M services as single performance obligation with software. Pre-2022 service-heavy model ran close to 1:1 software-to-services; early Palantir gov contracts ran 3–5x services vs license in total deal value — a model they are deliberately shrinking

Snowflake - Compute: $2–$4/credit on-demand; $1.50–$2.50/credit under committed-use - Storage: $23/TB/month (AWS US regions) - Enterprise commits: $250K–$1M+ to unlock meaningful credit discounts - Gross margin: ~75–78% software

Databricks - DBUs: $0.15–$0.40/DBU (Enterprise tier includes Unity Catalog + advanced governance) - Cloud compute billed separately (dual-bill model) - Storage: ~$0.02/GB/month on underlying object storage - 700+ customers spending $1M+/yr; net dollar retention >140%; annualized revenue $5.4B (Jan 2026) - Gross margin: ~74–80% (declining slightly as AI workloads increase compute intensity) - Services largely delivered through SI partners; Databricks direct services multiple: 0.3–0.5x

Splunk - On-premises: ~$150/GB/day/year list; enterprise security (ES) $250–$400/GB/day - Cloud premium: 1.2–1.5x on-premises rates - Multi-year discounts: 10–20% - Mid-size (200 hosts, 500 GB/day): $30K–$60K/month under enterprise agreement

Datadog - Logs ingest: $0.10/GB ingest + $1.70/M indexed - 200 GB/day at list exceeds $1.3M/yr for log mgmt alone - Hosts (infra monitoring): ~$15–$23/host/month - Mid-size (200 hosts + 500 GB logs + APM): $15K–$25K/month

C3.ai / Cognite Data Fusion / AspenTech - All enterprise custom-quote, no disclosed per-seat list - C3.ai reported $108.7M in FY2025 Q4 revenue with consumption-based model - Cognite Data Fusion on Azure Marketplace with custom quotes; heavy-industry OT/IT convergence target - AspenTech remains industrial simulation/optimization benchmark, similarly opaque

Service Multiplier

  • 1–2x Year 1 (data pipeline buildout, model training, ontology mapping); 0.3–0.5x from Y2

OSS-to-Paid Conversion Playbook

Relevant precedents for NEOS (CC-NC) + Fractal (CC-NC) + AZOA SDK:

  • MongoDB SSPL (2018): Switched from AGPL to SSPL after AWS launched DocumentDB. SSPL forces SaaS providers offering MongoDB as a service to open their entire stack — effectively a commercial license requirement for cloud deployments. MongoDB gross margin ~73–75% on software.
  • Elastic (ELv2 + SSPL then AGPL): Moved from Apache 2.0 to dual SSPL+ELv2 in 2021 after AWS forked OpenSearch; returned to AGPL Sep 2024. Lesson: CC-NC/SSPL-style restrictions stop cloud hyperscaler free-riding but must have a clean commercial escape valve.
  • HashiCorp BSL (Aug 2023, now IBM/HashiCorp): Switched Terraform, Vault, Consul from MPL 2.0 to BSL 1.1. BSL allows production use except by direct competitors; converts to OSI-recognized license after a defined “Change Date.” Closest precedent to PrimusNeo’s CC-NC: non-competing entities get near-open access; commercial SaaS competitors must buy a license.
  • Conversion rate reality: OSS-to-paid in infra software runs 1–5% of active community users. At 10,000 active NEOS deployments → realistic paid conversion 100–500 orgs. Revenue-per-converted-customer must be high enough to sustain dev — typically $10K–$100K+/yr for enterprise governance tooling.

Outcome / Value-Based Pricing Precedents

  • Zendesk AI agents (2024): Switched to outcomes-based — charges only for successful AI resolutions, not seat licenses or consumption
  • Intercom Resolution Bot: Pay per successful resolution, zero cost for failed interactions
  • Gartner forecast: 30%+ of enterprise SaaS will incorporate outcome-based components by 2025 (up from ~15%)
  • McKinsey: Value-based pricing companies achieve profit margins 15% above peers
  • Palantir: Early gov contracts included value-share clauses (% fraud recovered, % logistics savings). Materially harder to audit and collect on; Palantir moved away post-2018
  • ServiceNow pilots: Outcome pricing with guaranteed efficiency improvement SLAs — full rate only when targets met

Service Multiplier Summary by Layer

Layer Comparable Services : License Ratio
AMP NVIDIA Omniverse, Unity Industrial 1.5–3x (heavy SI deployment)
NEOS Diligent, Microsoft Viva 0.5–1x (lighter services, mostly config/training)
AZOA MuleSoft, Boomi 2–3x Year 1 (connector buildout, architecture)
Fractal Palantir (historical), Snowflake/Databricks (current) 0.5–1.5x (shifting software-led; SI partners absorb much of services multiple)

Fractal Engine (Intelligence / Analytics)

Scale Definition Annual License Anchor Notes
Small <5 data sources, departmental analytics, <1TB managed $30K–$75K/yr Above Databricks/Snowflake raw compute for equivalent vol but includes PrimusNeo operational ontology + signal layer — comparable to Looker Standard entry
Mid 5–20 data sources, operational telemetry + historical archive, 1–20TB $100K–$350K/yr Below Palantir commercial entry ($250K–$500K/yr), above commodity Snowflake/Databricks. “Palantir for federated orgs” without the services-heaviness
Large 20+ sources, cross-org intelligence, real-time + archive, >20TB $500K–$2M+/yr Palantir Foundry territory; justified by the operational ontology layer (NEOS governance events as first-class signals) that neither Palantir, Databricks, nor Snowflake provides natively

Services multiplier target: 1–2x license Y1; 0.3–0.5x from Y2. CC-NC dual-license: research/nonprofit use free; commercial production requires license at these rates.

Gross Margin Targets

Comparable software gross margins: Palantir 80%, Snowflake 75–78%, Databricks 74–80%, MuleSoft (within Salesforce) ~73–78%. PrimusNeo target blended gross margin on license/subscription revenue: 72–80% — achievable given the federated steward payout structure (stewards receive a share of license revenue, which is the primary cost-of-revenue). Services gross margin target: 35–50% (consistent with mid-tier SIs). Blended target at scale: 65–72%.

Agriculture Vertical — Market Intelligence Brief

PrimusNeo Engagement Model Research Prepared: May 2026 | Coverage: Row Crop, Specialty, Livestock, Cooperatives, Ag Inputs, Regenerative Ag, MRV

1. Comparable Vendor Pricing

Farm Management / FMIS

Vendor Model Price Range
Climate FieldView (Bayer) Per-acre/yr $4–$10/acre/yr
John Deere Operations Center Bundled w/ JD equipment; analytics tiers $0 base (JD hw required) → $3–$6/acre add-on analytics
Trimble Ag Mixed-fleet guidance + SaaS $5–$12/acre/yr software; hw additional
Granular / Traction Ag (Corteva divestiture) Enterprise per-acre SaaS $3–$25/acre/yr by module depth
CropX Soil sensor hw + SaaS Sensors $500–$1,000/unit; software $15–$25/acre/yr
AGCO Fuse Equipment-bundled; TerraStar correction Correction ~$800–$1,500/seat/yr; analytics dealer-quoted

FieldView’s $4–$10 reflects mature mass-market product with vol discounts. CropX high end reflects hw + analytics. AGCO opaque outside dealer relationships.

Ag-Fintech / Supply Chain Traceability

  • Indigo Ag (Market+ / Scope 3): Service fee to grain buyers + CPGs 2–5% of contracted grain value. Farmers receive up to 10% premiums on sustainably-attributed deliveries. Carbon layer guaranteed farmers $15/tCO2e at launch; peak prices reached $60–$80/tCO2e before market contraction.
  • Cargill / Provenance-style traceability: Grain premiums carry $0.10–$0.30/bu traceability overhead. Enterprise food-co API integrations: $50K–$250K/yr.

MRV / Soil Carbon

Platform Revenue Model Effective Price
Indigo Carbon Credit issuance $60–$80/tCO2e peak; market contracted 57% in 2024
Nori Marketplace fee Shut down September 2024
Boomitra AI/satellite MRV; smallholder focus Credits <$40/tCO2e; MRV costs <$10/acre at continental scale
Regrow Ag B2B SaaS for supply chain owners ~$5–$15/acre/yr for Scope 3 monitoring
CIBO Technologies Platform license + program admin ~$3–$8/acre for scenario modeling + reporting
Truterra (Land O’Lakes) Revenue share program ~$19/tCO2e effective farmer payout ($9M paid for 462K tCO2e over 2 yrs)
Bayer Carbon Bundled w/ FieldView enterprise Subsidized via seed/chemical margin; not separately priced

Voluntary ag carbon credit market contracted from $84.9M to $36.1M in 2024 — 57% decline driven by quality concerns + methodology disputes. Boomitra claims 90%+ MRV cost reduction vs traditional soil sampling at $50–$100/acre.

Cooperative ERP / Digital Platforms

  • Conservis / Traction Ag: $5–$15/acre/yr for full FM suite; 50K+ acre operators quoted $200K–$500K/yr
  • AgVend: Retail-facing omnichannel platform for co-ops; 0.3–0.8% of digital orders processed (GMV-based)
  • CHS / GROWMARK in-house: Both orgs formed Cooperative Ventures ($50M joint VC fund, 2022) to invest in ag-tech rather than build proprietary. Traction Ag $10M Series A (May 2024) Cooperative Ventures-led — signals which FMIS platforms are on co-op shortlist

Remote Sensing

  • Planet Labs: Enterprise ag contracts $30K–$500K/yr for field-level daily imagery. Insights-as-a-Service emerging at $few thousand/mo for regional monitoring
  • Descartes Labs (Earth Daily): AI-processed crop intelligence $50K–$300K/yr
  • EOS Crop Monitoring: Lower-cost entry $1–$3/acre/yr for NDVI + crop health indices; field-level reporting tiers from $500/mo

2. Engagement Scale Tiers — 3-Year TCO

Tier 1 — Small: Family Farm / Small Co-op (<5,000 acres, <$10M revenue)

  • Typical annual: $4–$10/acre/yr → $20K–$50K/yr for 5K-acre operation
  • 3-yr TCO: $60K–$150K (incl 2–3 guidance/precision units @ $5K–$15K each + connectivity + light impl + annual SaaS)
  • PrimusNeo: AMP for field boundary + agronomic data; Fractal for yield prediction + input optimization. NEOS/AZOA lightweight unless co-op-connected.

Tier 2 — Mid: Regional Co-op / Mid Corporate Farm (5K–100K acres, $10–200M revenue)

  • Typical annual: $8–$20/acre blended → $400K–$2M/yr for 100K-acre entity
  • 3-yr TCO: $1.2M–$6M incl impl, ERP integration (AgVend, SSI, Agris), training, data governance setup, MRV enrollment
  • PrimusNeo: Full AMP + AZOA for ERP interop across member systems; NEOS for member-data governance + sovereignty assurance; Fractal for SC carbon attribution + Scope 3 reporting

Tier 3 — Large: Ag Input Giant / Global Trader / National Co-op Federation ($200M+ revenue)

  • Typical annual: $5M–$25M/yr across technology, data licensing, consulting, compliance
  • 3-yr TCO: $15M–$75M incl multi-region rollout, custom integrations, regulatory compliance infra (EUDR, FSMA, Article 6), ongoing PM
  • PrimusNeo: All four pillars — AMP authoritative spatial layer, AZOA multi-partner interop (grain traders, CPGs, regulators, MRV verifiers), NEOS cross-jurisdictional data sovereignty, Fractal cross-commodity SC intelligence

3. Services Rates

  • McKinsey Agriculture Practice (ACRE): Engagements $350K–$2M+ for digital ag strategy; senior partner ~$10K–$15K/day. ACRE licensed separately at undisclosed enterprise rates
  • Bayer Crop Science / Digital Farming: Impl + agronomy advisory bundled into FieldView enterprise; dedicated agronomist $500–$1,500/day for custom
  • AGCO Digital Solutions: Dealer-delivered Fuse impl $150–$300/hr; national account fixed-fee fleet-wide rollout
  • ADM Outcomes: Multi-year regenerative ag transition + Scope 3 advisory; $500K–$3M per engagement depending on grower network size
  • Co-op tech installations (precision planting, VR, soil health networks): $15K–$80K per site for hw + config; annual maintenance 15–20% of license

4. Pricing Mechanics

Per-acre dominant for crop software ($2–$15/acre/yr). Low ($2–$4): basic field mapping + record-keeping. High ($12–$15): soil health indexing + satellite NDVI + weather integration + financial reporting. Most enterprise co-op deals negotiated as total-AUM contracts, not per-acre rack rates.

Per-head livestock standard. Entry: $1–$3.65/head/yr (~$0.003–$0.01/head/day). Full precision livestock farming (wearables, AI health, feed optimization): $5–$20/head/yr.

$/tCO2e (MRV): Farmer revenue $15–$80/tCO2e by registry + buyer. MRV service costs $3–$15/acre/yr or $2–$10/tCO2e verified. Verra revised Dec 2024 fee schedule charges $5,000 upfront per verification review + per-credit issuance fees — compresses developer margins. Gold Standard imposes higher co-benefit verification overhead.

% of harvest value: Indigo-style programs offer 2–10% premiums on sustainably attributed grain. SC traceability platforms charge buyers 0.3–2% of sourced commodity value.

Per-farm site: Flat-rate farm-site licensing ($500–$3,000/site/yr) attractive for co-ops serving many small members — avoids per-acre metering complexity across variable field sizes.

5. Regulatory Overlay

EU Deforestation Regulation (EUDR): Covers soy, cattle, cocoa, coffee, palm oil, rubber, wood. Large operators must comply by December 30, 2026 (delayed twice); micro/small operators by June 30, 2027. Requires plot-level geolocation for all commodities entering EU market. EC estimates simplification measures reduce annual compliance costs by 75%, but upstream costs (physical segregation, traceability, geolocation infra) remain substantial and can exceed thin ag margins in high-risk origins.

CBAM: Nitrogen fertilizer production in scope → indirect input-cost pressure. Direct commodity crops not yet in CBAM but under ongoing review; inclusion would fundamentally reshape ag SC economics.

USDA NIFA / FSMA: NIFA Food Safety Outreach Program (FSOP) provides technical assistance funding to specialty crop producers. FSMA Produce Safety Rule creates record-keeping, traceability, food safety plan requirements driving FMIS + SC software adoption. USDA estimated farms with $25K–$250K annual produce sales spend up to 60% of profits on FSMA compliance — substantial SaaS buying trigger for documentation/audit-trail tools.

Article 6 (Paris Agreement): COP29 (Nov 2024) finalized remaining technical guidance. As of March 2025, 97 bilateral agreements across 59 countries active; Article 6.4 UN carbon market expected to launch 2025–2026. UN-endorsed credits expected premium pricing vs voluntary — could re-energize soil carbon MRV investment after 2024’s contraction.

Verra VM0042 / Gold Standard: VM0042 v2.0 (active May 2023) primary compliance pathway for row-crop carbon. Dec 2024 fee revision ($5K upfront + per-credit issuance fees) squeezes developer economics at low credit prices. Gold Standard co-benefit doc requirements add cost but serve premium buyer segments.

6. Procurement Signals

Decision-maker map: Co-op boards (member-elected, risk-averse, annual budget cycles) hold ultimate authority for large cooperative tech investments. CTO/CIO at national co-ops + large input companies (Bayer, AGCO, CNH Industrial) govern platform-level decisions. At mid-sized corporate farms, buying committees include lead agronomist, ops manager, increasingly an ESG/sustainability lead. The agronomist is the critical influencer — controls field data access + vendor trial permissions.

Sales cycle: 12–24 months for co-op enterprise software; 18–36 months for MRV/carbon program enrollment at scale. Planting-cycle awareness non-negotiable: decisions made Oct–Feb for spring deployment. Pilots must complete within one full growing season to generate board-credible ROI evidence.

Buying triggers: EUDR SC compliance deadlines; Scope 3 reporting mandates from major retail buyers (Walmart, Unilever, Nestlé SC programs); USDA EQIP/CSP conservation program eligibility; state-level carbon incentive programs; co-op member demand for input cost transparency.

CHS/GROWMARK gateway: Both channel tech investment through Cooperative Ventures fund. Vendor relationships typically begin with Cooperative Ventures portfolio teams before reaching operational buyers — fund relationship = prioritized channel entry point.

7. Rejection Reasons

Data ownership / sovereignty (primary barrier): GAO January 2024 precision agriculture report formally identified farm data sharing/ownership as top adoption challenge. Farmers fear precision data (field boundaries, yield maps, soil tests, application records) shared with large vendors becomes competitive intelligence for input cos or grain traders. Civil Eats (March 2026): farm data remains “a hidden crop for corporate tech.” Only 36% of smaller farms planned AI adoption vs 81% of large farms — trust gap is structural.

Rural connectivity: High-speed broadband unreliable across large portions of US Corn Belt, Great Plains, international growing regions. Cloud-first platforms with no offline mode fail at field edge — fundamental disqualifier in many geographies.

ROI per acre too thin: At $200–$500/acre commodity crop margins, a $10–$15/acre software stack requires demonstrable 2–5% yield lift or input cost reduction just to break even. Most platforms have over-promised + under-delivered.

Broken farm-tech promises: John Deere data lock-in controversies, Sep 2024 collapse of Nori, multiple failed carbon programs, Bayer’s years of financial losses on Climate Corporation → deep market skepticism. New entrants need verifiable 3rd-party outcome evidence — not vendor case studies.

Seasonal cash flow: Farm ops highly seasonal w/ cash concentrated post-harvest. Annual subscription billing misaligned to farm cash flow creates real friction; installment billing or harvest-contingent payment terms materially improve conversion with smaller operators.

Summary

Agriculture is a deeply fragmented but high-stakes opportunity. Per-acre pricing ($2–$15/yr) dominates crop software; MRV/carbon layers add $3–$15/acre for regenerative programs — with credit revenues under structural pressure after 2024’s 57% voluntary market contraction. Enterprise co-op + global trader deals scale to $5M–$25M/yr in blended technology spend, with 3-yr TCOs reaching $75M for largest operators. Regulatory tailwind significant: EUDR (effective Dec 2026), FSMA, Article 6 carbon market formalization all mandate traceability + data infrastructure that PrimusNeo’s AZOA interop + NEOS governance directly address. Single largest adoption barrier: farmer data sovereignty — go-to-market must lead with transparent data ownership terms, genuine interoperability, one-season pilot designs producing board-credible ROI. Co-op procurement flows through 12–24 mo board approval cycles; Cooperative Ventures (CHS + GROWMARK) = critical gateway to cooperative segment. Motions that bypass the agronomist + grower buying committee reliably fail.

PrimusNeo — Construction & AEC Market Research Brief

Vertical: Construction (AEC, GC/Sub, Real Estate Developers, Infrastructure, MEP, Smart Buildings / FM) Products mapped: AMP (Spatial OS) · NEOS (Governance) · AZOA (Interop Fabric) · Fractal Engine (Intelligence) Prepared: 2026-05-23

1. Comparable Vendor Pricing

1.1 BIM / Digital Twin

Vendor Model Indicative Range

Bentley iTwin Credits + enterprise Community free non-commercial; enterprise custom — typical large infra $200K–$1M+/yr

2. Engagement Scale Tiers — 3-Year TCO

Small: GC/Sub <$50M Revenue, 1–2 Active Projects

Procore entry ($5K/yr) + ACC Build (5 seats, ~$8K/yr) + Fieldwire Pro (5 seats, ~$3K/yr) + Matterport Starter. Impl: self-serve or 1–2 SI days; $5K–$15K. 3-yr TCO: $40K–$90K. PrimusNeo entry: AZOA as Procore/ACC connector eliminating duplicate entry; AMP for as-built spatial record without displacing incumbents.

Mid: Regional Contractor/Developer $50–500M, 5–30 Projects

Procore ($25K–$80K/yr) + ACC BIM Collaborate 20–50 seats ($14K–$35K/yr) + CDE Asite/Newforma ($20K–$80K/yr) + DroneDeploy Advanced ($8K/yr) + One Click LCA Designer ($20K/yr). Impl: SI partner 60–120 days; $50K–$150K. 3-yr TCO: $350K–$800K. PrimusNeo play: NEOS for cross-project governance/approvals; Fractal replacing PowerBI/Excel portfolio dashboards; AZOA federating CDE + PM + BIM data.

Large: ENR Top-100, Mega-Projects ($1B+ capex), National Portfolio

Procore enterprise ($200K–$500K/yr) + Bentley iTwin or ACC enterprise ($200K–$1M/yr) + Willow Twin or Honeywell Forge ($250K–$1M/yr) + One Click LCA Power Pack ($80K–$120K/yr) + AECOM/Arup digital advisory ($500K–$2M/yr). Impl: 6–18 mo, $500K–$3M. 3-yr TCO: $3M–$12M. PrimusNeo play: Full AMP+NEOS+AZOA+Fractal as federated intelligence/governance layer across portfolio.

4. Pricing Mechanics

  • Per-project: Reality capture (DroneDeploy, Matterport, Open Space), LCA scans (Tally per model), CDE project modules. $500/project (residential) to $50K+ (mega-project)
  • Per-seat/per-user: Dominant for PM + CDE. Fieldwire $54–$94/user/mo; Aconex $29–$49/user/mo; ACC $42–$135/user/mo by module. Vol discounts at 5–25 seats
  • Per-square-foot (FM): IWMS + twin licensing $0.10–$0.50/sf/yr for portfolios >500K sf. TRIRIGA, Planon, Willow Twin
  • % of Annual Construction Volume: Procore proprietary; 0.1–0.3% of ACV. Healthy owner digital program budgets run 1–2% of total capex (McKinsey)
  • % of capex (advisory): AECOM + Arup digital retainers on mega-projects 0.2–0.5% of capex. McKinsey Capital Projects 0.5–1% for strategic programs

5. Regulatory Overlay

EU EPBD (recast 2024): All new buildings must meet Zero-Emission Building criteria. Fossil-fuel boiler subsidies ended Jan 2025. Digital Building Passports (per-building records covering energy, materials, lifecycle) mandated as part of EPBD impl. Immediate demand for data fabrics ingesting heterogeneous building system data — direct AZOA + AMP use case.

California Buy Clean Act (BCCA) / AB 2446: GWP limits on structural steel, concrete rebar, flat glass, insulation on CA public works enforceable Jan 2025. AB 2446 targets 40% reduction in material carbon intensity by 2035. Drives One Click LCA + EC3; Fractal opportunity for automated EPD ingestion + real-time compliance reporting.

UK Net Zero Carbon Buildings Standard (NZCBS): v1 launches early 2026, integrating with PAS 2080. Upfront carbon (A1–A5) limits per m². From 2025, publicly funded projects must be designed without fossil-fuel heating.

ISO 19650 BIM: Global info-management benchmark; mandatory EU + UK (since 2016), increasingly GCC + India. Part 6 (H&S integration) released 2025. Requires structured CDE — AMP can serve as the ISO 19650-compliant spatial information layer.

LEED / BREEAM / Green Star: Embodied carbon docs, energy modeling, operational data submissions tied to credit thresholds → ongoing data obligations through bldg ops, supporting persistent AMP/Fractal usage post-construction.

6. Procurement Signals

Primary champions: - VDC / BIM Manager at GC or design-build: controls day-to-day platform selection for ACC, Procore, CDE. Pain: design-side BIM vs field-side PM interop gaps; duplicate model mgmt - CIO / CDO at ENR-100 contractor: owns enterprise arch + vendor consolidation. Budget authority $500K+. Receptive to consolidation reducing vendor sprawl - Project Director / Owner’s Rep on capital programs: schedule + cost + risk visibility across 10–50 active projects. Values federated data without forcing subs onto single mandated tool — AZOA value prop - Director of Sustainability / ESG Officer: owns embodied carbon reporting under EPBD/BCCA/NZCBS. Needs automated EPD ingestion + LCA workflow

RFP trigger events: - Owner-mandated ISO 19650 BIM/CDE reqs on public infra tenders (Highways England, GSA, state DOTs, Network Rail) - Post-merger portfolio consolidation - Mega-project mobilization: capital programs >$500M trigger digital delivery planning phase - ESG reporting deadline (annual sustainability report, EU SFDR for EU real estate funds, SEC climate disclosure for US-listed owners)

7. Rejection Reasons

Paper/email-based culture: Significant portion of workforce (esp subs, trades) on paper, WhatsApp, email. Adoption requires change mgmt buyers consistently underestimate. Sub resistance to yet-another-login is a top-3 Procore complaint.

Subcontractor adoption friction: ENR-100 GCs routinely report sub networks won’t adopt owner-mandated platforms. Solutions requiring zero friction on subs (read-only views, email-triggered approvals, no account creation) consistently outperform full-onboarding.

ROI attribution difficulty: Each construction project is unique → no A/B comparison. Decision-makers struggle to prove platform caused schedule improvement vs weather or skilled superintendent. Vendor-published ROI reports (Procore, Autodesk) discounted by CFOs.

Integration lock-in: Most ENR contractors have Procore + ACC + CDE locked in by multi-year contracts. Adding PrimusNeo requires clear AZOA story: bidirectional Procore webhook, IFC/RVT ingestion from ACC, write-back without breaking workflows. Integration tax = primary reason pilots stall at POC.

Price sensitivity on thin margins: GC net margins 2–4%. Premium add-on without near-term measurable ROI (rework reduction, fewer RFIs, faster financial closeout) gets deferred. Pilot pricing with clear documented-ROI path is table stakes.

Summary

Construction/AEC is a $16T global market with fragmented multi-vendor software spend and accelerating regulatory pressure on data transparency. Dominant pricing: per-seat ($29–$135/user/mo) and ACV-% (0.1–0.3%) for PM, shifting to per-sf ($0.10–$0.50/sf/yr) for FM digital twins. 3-yr TCO: $40K–$90K (small GC) → $3M–$12M (ENR-100 portfolio). EU EPBD digital building passports + CA Buy Clean Act + UK NZCBS + ISO 19650 directly validate AZOA interop + AMP spatial record propositions. Primary champions: VDC/BIM Managers + CDOs at large contractors; Owner’s Reps drive top-down mandates on capital programs. Most durable rejection risks: subcontractor adoption friction, ROI attribution opacity, Procore/Autodesk integration lock-in. PrimusNeo addresses with zero-friction AZOA connectors, documented project-level ROI instrumentation, and pilot structure that proves value in single project lifecycle before portfolio expansion.

PrimusNeo Energy Vertical — Market Research Brief

Products in scope: AMP (Spatial OS), NEOS (Governance), AZOA (Interop Fabric), Fractal Engine (Intelligence) Vertical: Energy — utilities, oil & gas, renewables, grid/ISO, DERs, microgrids, carbon markets Date: May 2026

1. Comparable Vendor Pricing

Operational Digital Twin / ADMS / DERMS

Full-scale ADMS deployments: $5M–$50M software-only depending on utility scale and module count, exclusive of hardware and integration services (DOE ADMS Advisory).

  • GE Vernova GridOS (ADMS + DERMS): Enterprise quote-only; ranked #1 Guidehouse Insights 2024 DERMS Leaderboard. IOU packages estimated $8M–$20M over 5-year term.
  • Schneider Electric EcoStruxure ADMS: ~23% ADMS mindshare. Modular SCADA + OMS + DMS; comparable IOUs report $6M–$15M.
  • Siemens Spectrum Power ADMS: ~20% mindshare; comparable. Both Schneider and Siemens price via competitive tender for IOU/ISO procurements.
  • AVEVA PI System (OSIsoft): $162K example proposal itemises tags + connectors; enterprise deployments with 1M+ tags for large refineries / grid operators: $500K–$5M/yr subscription + support.
  • Cognite Data Fusion: Closed 2024 at ~$100M SaaS ARR (+38% YoY); priced on usage/MAU tiers. Supermajor deployments: $2M–$8M ARR.
  • AspenTech APM suite / Mtell: Enterprise custom; starts $250K–$1M+/yr for mid-size process industry.
  • Bently Nevada (Baker Hughes) System 1: HW-bundled APM; per rotating machine; large plant deployments: $500K–$3M incl sensors.

DER Orchestration / VPP

  • AutoGrid Flex / Uplight DERMS: Utility-facing SaaS converging around $3–$8/kW-year enrolled capacity. 50 MW portfolio: $150K–$400K/yr.
  • Voltus / Leap: No-cost-to-customer, market-revenue-share. Voltus customers earn $100K–$470K/MW-year depending on ISO (PJM/ISO-NE highest; SPP $100K). Voltus retains ~20–30% of cleared revenue — platform value $20K–$140K/MW-year to aggregator.
  • Tesla VPP / Generac Grid Services (formerly Enbala): Retail incentive ~$2/kWh dispatch (CA, PG&E). B2B utility DERMS SaaS: $2–$6/kW-month estimated.

Carbon Accounting / MRV

  • Watershed: Packages from ~$50K/yr; large corporates with complex Scope 3 + energy portfolios pay $200K–$500K/yr.
  • Persefoni: Free “Pro” tier; “Advanced” enterprise $55K–$250K/yr.
  • Sylvera / Pachama / Toucan / Flowcarbon: Project-level rating; Sylvera $20K–$100K/yr for portfolio rating; tokenization platforms take 1–3% of credit face value.

ETRM (Energy Trading & Risk Management)

  • Allegro (ION Group) / OpenLink: Perpetual licenses $250K–$5M+; SaaS $10K–$100K+/month. Implementation: 1–2× license cost. Annual support 18–25% of license. Allegro renewables-sector CAGR 14.5% (Chartis Research).

REC / Energy Attribute Certificate Tracking

  • M-RETS / PJM-EIS GATS: Registry transaction fees low (cents/MWh); value in data quality + hourly granularity. PJM Tier I RECs hit ~$40/MWh in 2024 (long-run average $8.74/MWh). EnergyTag granular certificate pilots running in six jurisdictions. Attestation infrastructure cost is the primary WTP signal for buyers seeking 24/7 CFE compliance.

4. Pricing Mechanics — What Is Normal in Energy

Mechanism Where Used Typical Rate
Per-MW (enrolled capacity) DERMS, VPP, DR aggregation $3–$12/kW-year
Per-asset / per-machine APM (Bently Nevada, AspenTech) $500–$5,000/asset/yr
Per-tag (data historian) AVEVA PI System $0.10–$2/tag/month at scale
% of energy traded ETRM / VPP revenue share 20–30% of cleared market revenue
$/REC or $/MWh Certificate tracking, PPA settlement $0.02–$0.15/MWh registry fee; $8–$40/REC market
$/tCO2e managed Carbon MRV SaaS Implicit in $55K–$500K/yr platform fees
Enterprise annual license ADMS, EMS, ETRM $500K–$10M/yr large-utility tier
% of project CAPEX EPC / advisory 2–8% for engineering services

Dominant pattern: enterprise license + modules with 3–5% annual escalators. DERMS/VPP migrating to capacity-enrolled SaaS with performance incentives. Carbon software flat annual SaaS today, trending toward $/tCO2e-verified as MRV accuracy becomes auditable.

5. Regulatory Overlay — Willingness-to-Pay Drivers

FERC Order 2222 (DER Market Participation): Requires all ISOs/RTOs to allow aggregated DER participation in wholesale markets (down to 100 kW min). Mandatory metering + telemetry + settlement-data → direct tech spend trigger for utilities + aggregators needing interoperable dispatch. AZOA architecturally positioned for this integration layer.

NERC CIP (CIP-002 through CIP-015-1): Annual aggregate burden for CIP-015-1 (Internal Network Security Monitoring) alone: $13.2M/yr industry-wide across ~1,636 registered entities (Fed Register Sep 2024). CIP-013 supply-chain mandates → structured vendor assessment + attestation → direct WTP increase for auditable provenance (NEOS). CIP-003-9 (effective April 2026) extends supply-chain controls to low-impact BES.

EU CSRD / CSDDD: Large EU-listed energy firms + non-EU subs face mandatory double-materiality ESG disclosures from FY2024. Omnibus simplification (Sep 2025) reduced scope but preserved core emissions disclosure. Cross-border EU exporters face CBAM financial obligations from Jan 2026 — requires certified embedded-carbon data per shipment.

GHG Protocol + Article 6 (Paris Agreement): COP29 finalised Article 6.4 rules for ITMOs. Energy exporters + carbon market participants need cryptographically auditable provenance chains to prevent double-counting across NDCs. NEOS + Fractal attestation + lineage capabilities map directly.

State/PUC Rate Recovery: IOU technology investments (ADMS, DERMS, cyber) are recoverable through rate cases. CA CPUC has effectively mandated ADMS deployment for large IOUs; similar in NY (REV), IL (CEJA). PUC approval introduces 18–36 mo procurement cycle but de-risks vendor revenue through rate-payer funding.

6. Procurement Signals

Buyer Role Decision Authority Key Concern
Chief Grid Officer / VP Grid Ops Grid modernisation sponsor Op approval, PUC testimony Reliability, NERC CIP, OT security
CIO / VP Technology Platform + vendor governance Architecture, vendor risk IT/OT convergence, integration complexity
COO Overall business case Budget authority TCO, risk, regulatory deadlines
Head of Trading / VP Commercial ETRM, carbon, VPP commercial Revenue-side tech Settlement accuracy, market connectivity
Chief Sustainability Officer Carbon MRV, ESG disclosure Board-level mandate CSRD/CBAM accuracy, auditability
Regulatory Affairs / GC PUC filings Rate-case approval Regulatory defensibility, vendor attestation

Procurement pathway: Large ADMS / DERMS contracts ($5M+) require PUC pre-approval or inclusion in an Integrated Resource Plan (IRP) filing. RFPs via regulated competitive bidding; short-listed vendors pass 3–6 mo technical eval. Sales cycle: 18–36 mo for IOUs/ISOs. Municipal utilities + IPPs faster (6–18 mo).

7. Rejection Reasons

NERC CIP / OT Cyber Risk: Software touching BES Cyber Systems must be classified, change-managed, assessed under CIP-007/010/013. No documented CIP compliance architecture = rejection at technical eval. CIP-005 requires jump-server + MFA for vendor remote access; cloud-native platforms need explicit network segmentation attestation. CIP-013 supply-chain assessments — no published security attestation pack = immediate disqualification.

Vendor Concentration Risk: IOUs + ISOs enforce multi-vendor policies post-SolarWinds + Volt Typhoon disclosures. Platform simultaneously touching SCADA, ADMS, DERMS, carbon accounting triggers vendor concentration review. PrimusNeo must enter as interoperability + governance fabric above incumbents — not displacement — to reduce this.

OT Integration Complexity: Legacy distribution SCADA + EMS run proprietary protocols (DNP3, IEC 61968/70, ICCP). Governance/interop platform must demonstrate certified connectors + reference architectures. Abstract “open APIs” claims don’t survive utility architecture review boards.

Regulatory Approval Delays: Rate-recovery tech spend faces 18–36 mo PUC cycles; deals stall in evidentiary proceedings if cost-benefit testimony weak. Third-party ROI validation before the rate case is prerequisite.

Budget Cycle Mismatch: Utility capital plans set annually through IRP + budget processes. Missing capital plan window by one quarter delays deal by 12 months.

Summary

Energy presents PrimusNeo’s highest regulatory tailwind and longest sales cycles simultaneously. ADMS, DERMS, APM markets dominated by GE Vernova, Schneider, Siemens, AVEVA at $5M–$50M per IOU deployment, with 18–36 mo procurement windows gated by PUC rate-case approval. FERC Order 2222, NERC CIP-013/015, CSRD, CBAM collectively force utilities + IPPs + supermajors to pay for auditable provenance, OT supply-chain attestation, cross-border carbon data — precisely NEOS + AZOA capability set. Highest-probability entry: interop + governance fabric layered above incumbent ADMS + PI System stacks — bypasses vendor-concentration objections, reduces OT cyber risk exposure, positions PrimusNeo in ETRM + carbon MRV adjacency ($50K–$500K/yr SaaS) while building toward full Spatial OS deployments at mid + large tier ($2.5M–$80M 3yr TCO). Carbon markets + Article 6 attestation + REC hourly granularity = fastest near-term land opportunity.

Finance Vertical — Market Research Brief

PrimusNeo: AMP (Spatial OS) · NEOS (Governance) · AZOA (Interop Fabric) · Fractal Engine (Intelligence) Date: 2026-05-23

1. Comparable Vendor Pricing

Financial Data Fabrics / Middleware

Bloomberg Terminal: $31,980/seat/year (single seat); $28,320/seat/year (multi-seat); negotiated enterprise at $18,000–$20,000/seat (50+ seats). Hardware + add-ons push true Y1 cost to $34K–$35.5K/seat. Refinitiv Eikon (LSEG) ~$22,000/seat/year full feed; stripped variants from $3,600/year; exchange pass-through adds 20–50%. FactSet ~$12,000/seat/year mid-range. Snowflake / Databricks: $25K–$40K/year for 200 TB analytics; $2M–$10M/year for 500-analyst enterprise footprints.

Tokenization Platforms

Securitize: minimum engagement $50K–$100K (setup + issuance bundled); ongoing compliance/transfer-agent annual retainers. Tokeny (T-REX, EU-focused): SaaS licensing plus setup, comparable to Securitize. Polymath/Polymesh: transparent on-chain transaction fees. TaaS SMB SaaS: $2K–$5K/month (~$24K–$60K/year). White-label platforms: $50K–$150K upfront; custom development from $150K.

For live issuance: $3M offering = $75K–$200K all-in; $30M offering = $200K–$1M+. Platforms charge 0.5%–2.5% of asset-under-tokenization for smart-contract deployment + compliance wrapping; success-fee models charge 6%–10% of funds raised + 2% equity allocation. Onyx (now Kinexys) by JPM and Provenance are relationship-gated.

Custody / Wallet-as-a-Service

Provider Model Rate
BitGo AUM bps 5 bps/month (60 bps/yr) on AUC >$100K
Fireblocks API-usage + bespoke infra Negotiated; ~$1M+ AUC minimum

Compliance / KYC / AML

Chainalysis Reactor: ~$40K/year single-seat; enterprise KYT: $100K–$500K+/year. Elliptic Forensics: comparable to Chainalysis at enterprise. TRM Labs: modular chain-coverage; negotiated. ComplyAdvantage: $500–$5K/year SMB API; enterprise negotiated.

Governance / GRC

Diligent, Nasdaq Boardvantage: custom enterprise; Vendr shows Fortune 500 negotiate 30–50% off initial quotes. ServiceNow IRM/GRC: Fortune 500 negotiate 60–80% off list; mandatory 5–10% annual uplift since 2024.
