5. Regulatory Overlay

MiCA (EU, fully applicable Dec 2024): CASP licensing build-out €50K–€100K (small); capital reqs €50K (advisory), €125K (custody/exchange), €150K (trading platforms). Non-compliance fines: €15M or 3% of revenue. Creates direct demand for immutable governance logs, auditable issuance records, automated sanctions screening, cross-border identity portability.

OCC Interpretive Letter 1184 (May 2025): National banks may provide crypto custody (fiduciary + non-fiduciary), use sub-custodians, but must demonstrate controls. Banks will pay $200K–$500K+/year for platforms that produce attestations on-demand.

SEC SAB 122 (Jan 2025): Rescinds SAB 121 on-balance-sheet liability — removes primary accounting disincentive for bank custody.

FCA (UK): Implicit compliance tax £100K–£500K/year in incremental monitoring/reporting under crypto-asset registration and stablecoin frameworks.

Aggregate signal: Auditability + immutable governance logs + automated attestations are highest-value features FIs pay a premium for — they directly reduce regulatory capital risk and legal liability.

6. Procurement Signals

Tier Primary Buyer Tech Sign-off Compliance Veto Cycle
Small CIO / COO CTO — 2–4 mo
Mid Head of Digital Assets CDO / CTO CRO 6–12 mo
Large Head of Digital Assets + C-suite sponsor CTO + CDO committee CRO + Legal 12–24 mo

Mandatory mid/large requirements: SOC 2 Type II (US default), ISO 27001 (EU/APAC), annual 3rd-party pen test, BCP/DR docs, GDPR/data residency, reference clients in regulated FS. Missing SOC 2 or ISO 27001 disqualifies at mid/large.

7. Rejection Reasons

  1. Regulatory ambiguity — Legal/compliance veto until local regulator gives written guidance.
  2. Custody model mismatch — Must map to self-custody MPC/HSM, qualified custodian, or bank-grade sub-custodian.
  3. Legacy core-banking integration gap — Temenos/Finastra/Murex/Calypso require certified FIX/API connectors.
  4. Open-source / IP ownership risk — Unaudited smart contracts + no formal vuln disclosure + no IP indemnity = reject.
  5. Vendor concentration / lock-in risk — Multi-custodian, multi-chain is a requirement at G-SIB.
  6. Missing security certs — SOC 2 + ISO 27001 non-negotiable at mid/large.

Summary

FIs deploying tokenization, governance, and interop infrastructure face: Bloomberg ($32K/seat) and Refinitiv ($22K/seat) anchor information-layer reference price; tokenization platforms (Securitize, Tokeny) charge $50K–$150K setup + 0.5%–2.5% of issuance value; custody (BitGo, Fireblocks, Anchorage) at 5–60 bps/year on AUC. 3-year TCO: $350K (boutique) to $25M+ (G-SIB). Big-4 day rates: $2K–$10K. MiCA, OCC Letter 1184, SAB 122 make auditability + immutable governance logs binary procurement requirements. Primary deal-killers: regulatory ambiguity, absence of SOC 2 Type II / ISO 27001, inability to integrate with legacy core-banking. Sales cycles: 6–12 mo at mid, 12–24 mo at G-SIB.

4. Pricing Mechanics

Per-resident: Granicus, OpenGov, CitizenLab, Bang the Table use informal population-banded pricing. City of 50K residents ~$25K/yr; 500K ~$120K/yr for comparable modules. Not published; negotiated.

Per-member / per-user: Board portals (Diligent, Boardvantage) + enterprise SaaS charge per seat. SSI platforms per credential tx. Tyler SaaS courts $125–$370/user/mo.

Per-vote / per-tx: Snapshot free (off-chain). Tally + on-chain Governor: gas pass-through $0.01–$50/vote depending on chain + congestion. Entra Verified ID per-tx above 50K/mo threshold.

% of treasury: Karpatkey, Llama, comparable DAO treasury managers charge 0.25–0.75% AUM or fixed retainer. $100M treasury → $250K–$750K/yr before performance component.

Enterprise license: Esri statewide EAs, Tyler ERP, Granicus state contracts = flat annual against population counts + user volumes — typically $500K–$5M+/yr at state scale.

Grant / public funding: Decidim, Consul, Pol.is — OSS with deployment costs borne by agency. EU Horizon funds + US federal innovation grants regularly fund participatory democracy deployments.

5. Regulatory Overlay

Framework Applies To Cost Implication
FedRAMP Moderate US federal SaaS $400K–$900K to authorize; $100K–$300K/yr ongoing
FedRAMP High Highly sensitive federal data 450+ controls; JAB or agency ATO; $2M–$5M+ authorization
StateRAMP / GovRAMP US state + local (rebranded 2025); no FedRAMP reciprocity $150K–$500K
DoD IL2 Non-sensitive DoD public data ~325 controls; FedRAMP Mod equivalent; $400K–$800K
DoD IL4 CUI + contingency ops Add’l DoD-specific controls beyond IL2; $800K–$2M+
DoD IL5 NSS data + higher-sensitivity CUI 450+ controls; restricted DISA-authorized cloud only; $2M–$5M+
GDPR / EU NIS2 EU public sector Data residency in EEA; DPA; DPIA; privacy by design; up to €20M or 4% global turnover in fines
MiCA (EU) DAOs + CASPs operating in EU CASP licensing required by end-2024 (stablecoins from June 2024); 18-mo grandfathering ending mid-2026; legal + licensing €200K–€1M+
SAB 121 / SAB 122 US DAO + crypto custodians SAB 121 rescinded Jan 2025 via SAB 122; custodied crypto no longer on custodian balance sheet — reduces custody compliance overhead
CMMC / CMMI Defense-adjacent contractors Maturity Levels 1–3; Level 3 requires C3PAO assessment; $500K–$3M+

6. Procurement Signals

Buyer Personas

Persona Org Type Trigger
CIO / CITO City, county, state, federal Modernization mandate; legacy EOL; federal efficiency pressure
Chief Innovation Officer Large metro, state, federal Pilot/innovation budget; often outside traditional IT procurement
City Manager / County Admin Local gov Budget cycle; council mandate; constituent service pressure
DAO Steward Council / Foundation Protocol DAO Token holder vote; treasury diversification; ops maturity milestone
Cooperative Board / Federation Steward Worker co-op, credit union, housing co-op Board vote; governance reform; federation membership requirement

Cooperative + Expedited Procurement Vehicles

  • Sourcewell: Pre-competed cooperative; 50,000+ member agencies. Ideal first cooperative vehicle for PrimusNeo targeting US local government
  • NASPO ValuePoint: Lead-state model; all 50 states + DC + territories eligible. Strong for cloud/SaaS. Separate enrollment from Sourcewell
  • GSA MAS Schedule (SIN 518210C for IT; OASIS+ for prof services): Federal + state/local cooperative purchasing authority built into contract clauses
  • State-specific schedules: TX DIR, CA DGS, NY OGS — non-negotiated access to pre-priced agreements within those states

Process Norms

  • Government procurement cycles: 6–18 months RFP to award for mid/large. Small/micro-purchase thresholds (under $250K federal) allow sole-source + streamlined
  • DAO governance procurement: Token holder vote via Snapshot signal → Tally on-chain execution. Full proposal-to-execution cycle: 2–8 weeks incl discussion period
  • Budget cycles: Federal FY Oct 1. State/local vary; most decisions compress into Q3–Q4. Missing a budget cycle = 12-month delay

7. Rejection Reasons

Risk Driver
Procurement red tape New vendor not enrolled on approved cooperative vehicle or agency-specific approved list. Incumbent (Tyler, Granicus, Esri) lock-in with switching costs $500K–$5M+
FedRAMP gap No ATO = automatic disqualification for most US federal + many state agencies. FedRAMP 20x initiative (launched 2025) aims to accelerate via agency-path in ~90 days using pre-authorized infra boundaries
DoD security classification gap IL4/IL5 block commercially-hosted SaaS without DISA provisional. CMMC Level 2–3 required for defense-adjacent
DAO treasury security $4B+ lost to governance attacks + treasury exploits since 2016. Multi-sig thresholds commonly too low (2/3 cited as insufficient; experts recommend min 3/5 for <$10M, 4/7 for larger)
Governance attack vectors Sybil attacks on voting identity, delegation abuse, flash-loan voting power acquisition. MiCA regulators cite these as institutional adoption barriers. Governance attack = existential risk for DAO clients
Data sovereignty / GDPR EU public sector requires data residency in EEA. US-headquartered vendor without EU hosting = automatic disqualifier in most EU public tenders
Proof of scale / references Governments require 3–5 comparable-agency references. Early-stage vendors without marquee public-sector references routinely eliminated in technical evaluation
Budget cycle mismatch Missing a gov’s annual budget decision window = 12-month pipeline delay regardless of product fit

Summary

Governance spans two structurally different buyer archetypes — traditional public-sector agencies + emergent Web3 organizations — but both share core need for credible, auditable, multi-stakeholder decision infrastructure at scale. GovTech incumbents (Tyler, Granicus, OpenGov, Esri) command $20K–$5M+ annual contracts and benefit from cooperative purchasing lock-in but face criticism for cost overruns, slow delivery, poor interop. Participatory democracy tools (Decidim, CitizenLab) occupy lower-cost lane ($0–$80K/yr) but lack enterprise orchestration. DAO governance infra (Snapshot, Tally, Aragon) free at protocol layer; economic value concentrates in treasury management services ($200K–$2.5M/yr) where Llama + Karpatkey define pricing benchmark at 0.25–0.75% AUM or fixed retainer. PrimusNeo’s combo (AMP + NEOS + AZOA + Fractal) positions above point solutions, below full SI programs. Primary barriers: FedRAMP/StateRAMP compliance ($400K–$900K, 12–24 mo), cooperative purchasing vehicle enrollment, proof-of-scale references. DAO adoption barriers: governance security architecture + MiCA licensing overhead in EU. Optimal entry: Sourcewell or NASPO enrollment + a single marquee city or mid-DAO reference deployment to unlock broader pipeline.

3. Services Rates

Firm Role Day Rate (USD)

Epic impl at large IDN: $50M–$500M services spend over 3–7 years. HL7/FHIR/custom-API integration at regional health systems: $2M–$15M per major initiative. AZOA directly compresses this — clear ROI pitch to CIOs.

4. Pricing Mechanics

Per-bed standard for inpatient EHR ($500–$1,000/bed upfront). Per-provider/user for ambulatory/analytics ($25–$3,500/provider/mo). PMPM dominant for payer analytics/population health/VBC ($0.50–$200 PMPM by intensity). Per-API-call for FHIR fabric/interop. Enterprise license (ELA) for broad deployments (Veeva, Datavant). Per-encounter emerging for ambient AI (Nuance DAX, Suki, Epic ambient). PrimusNeo positioning: AMP on per-bed/per-facility ELA · AZOA on per-API/flat interop ELA · NEOS on per-covered-life/enterprise governance ELA · Fractal on PMPM/per-research-query.

5. Regulatory Overlay

HIPAA / HITECH: Legal floor. BAAs non-negotiable. HIPAA Security Rule overhaul (effective May 2026) mandates MFA, universal PHI encryption, 24-hr breach notification, annual pen testing.

HITRUST CSF: De facto procurement gate. 81% of hospitals and 83% of payers require HITRUST from tech vendors. i1 (~$70K) is now minimum for most health system RFPs; r2 ($100K–$500K, 12–15 mo) expected for core platforms. 3-yr enterprise cycles: $400K–$800K. PrimusNeo must achieve at least HITRUST i1 before substantive RFPs.

ONC TEFCA / QHIN: Common Agreement v2.1 effective Jan 2025; 21,086 orgs live. QHIN designation: ~12 months. AZOA as TEFCA-native fabric is a direct differentiator vs Redox/Health Gorilla.

EU EHDS (Reg EU 2025/327): In force March 2025; general application March 2027. EHR vendors must certify for interop/security by Jan 2026. Priority health data exchange (patient summaries, ePrescriptions) mandatory EU-wide by March 2029.

EU MDR / GDPR: MDR applies if Fractal outputs constitute clinical decision support (Class IIa/IIb). GDPR special-category health rules → consent management + DPIA — direct NEOS revenue opportunity.

6. Procurement Signals

Buyer personas: CIO (infra, vendor consolidation, integration), CMIO (workflow, patient safety, physician adoption), CDO (data strategy, AI governance), CCO (HIPAA, HITRUST, GDPR). Pharma: VP Data Science, Head of RWE, CTO.

Capital committee: IT capital >$1M requires board/capital committee. Decision: 6–18 months. EHR replacement: 5–10-yr cycles. Analytics/interop refresh: 3–5 yrs. Budget cycles Jul–Sep; Q4/Q1 peak signing.

RFP cadence: 68% of providers manage procurement manually (2024 HIMSS). RFPs: 6–12 mo issuance to award. Evaluation committees: 3–5 hrs/wk per member; technical fit weighted 30–35%; regulatory compliance gating (pass/fail).

Vendor risk mgmt: HITRUST cert attachment gating before submission. BAA + SOC 2 Type II + pen test standard. CISOs require AI governance docs for any ML/AI platform.

7. Rejection Reasons

Patient safety risk: Any clinical-workflow touch faces CMIO/patient-safety officer scrutiny. Position as decision-support, not autonomous; pursue Class I/II SaMD pathway early.

HIPAA fear: Privacy officers default-reject when PHI flows unclear. Document de-identification methodology, BAA scope, subprocessor chains BEFORE any technical demo.

“Rip and replace” resistance: Hospitals that spent $300M–$1.2B on Epic are committed. Position AMP+AZOA as layered augmentation — “We wrap Epic, we don’t replace it.”

Epic/Cerner integration lock-in: Epic proprietary APIs (MyChart, Hyperdrive, App Orchard) create real friction. 6–18 mo Epic technical review + App Orchard certification fees. AZOA must achieve Epic FHIR R4 cert + App Orchard listing.

Procurement cycle mismatch: 12–18 mo RFPs disadvantage startups. Lack of segment references = hard rejection. Lighthouse program (2–3 references per community/regional/national) prerequisite.

Summary

Healthcare is PrimusNeo’s highest-complexity, highest-value vertical: 3-yr deals from $1.5M (community hospital) to $200M+ (national IDN / top-20 pharma). Epic’s gravitational dominance + multi-billion-$ integration-services tax + regulatory stack (HIPAA, HITRUST, TEFCA, EHDS) function as moats for certified vendors. Per-bed and per-provider dominate EHR; PMPM dominates payer/population health; ELAs close largest pharma/IDN deals. HITRUST i1 non-negotiable before substantive RFP. Winning motion: wrap, don’t replace Epic/Cerner — AMP for spatial ops intelligence, AZOA as TEFCA-native FHIR fabric, NEOS for consent/data governance, Fractal for RWE + population analytics. CIOs + CMIOs co-buy; patient safety + HIPAA anxiety are primary emotional rejection levers — neutralize in discovery convo #1.

FourKites Annual platform + per-shipment $100K–$500K/yr platform; $0.50–$2/shipment

CargoWise (freight forwarder default) shifted to value-packs at $6–$10/shipment bundling forwarding + Customs + T&T. Relevant per-shipment anchor for AZOA conversations.

2. Engagement Scale Tiers — 3-Year TCO

Small: Regional 3PL / Freight Forwarder (<$50M revenue)

Current 3-yr TCO landscape: $120K–$400K (CargoWise, EDI middleware, manual compliance). PrimusNeo target: $60K–$180K/yr (AMP lite + AZOA connectors); 3-yr TCV $180K–$540K.

Mid: National 3PL ($50–500M) / Port Operator

Current 3-yr TCO landscape: $1.5M–$6M (TMS, WMS, visibility, customs, middleware). PrimusNeo target: $300K–$900K/yr; 3-yr TCV $900K–$2.7M. Displacement of 1–2 point solutions accelerates ROI.

Large: Top-20 Carrier / Top-5 3PL / Global Integrator

Exemplars: Maersk, MSC, CMA CGM; DHL Supply Chain, K+N, DB Schenker; FedEx, UPS. Current 3-yr TCO landscape: $15M–$80M+ globally. PrimusNeo target: $1.5M–$5M+/yr; 3-yr TCV $4.5M–$15M. Paid pilot ($250K–$500K) precedes full rollout.

Firm Role Indicative Day Rate (USD)

Comprehensive SC transformation programs: $250K–$2M+ for 3–6 mo. Channel partners to target: Accenture, Deloitte, GEP.

4. Pricing Mechanics — Industry Norms

  • Per-shipment: $0.50–$10 (CargoWise $6–$10 anchor); TMS, T&T, eBL
  • Per-TEU/container: Software-rare; carrier/terminal surcharges (THC). Twin/analytics experiments per-vessel-call $200–$2K
  • Per-vehicle/per-asset: Fleet telematics $10–$30/vehicle/mo SaaS GPS; $15–$50/tracker/mo for IoT
  • Per-warehouse/per-DC: WMS SaaS $3K–$10K/site/mo + per-user + per-tx tiers; multi-DC discounts standard
  • % of freight spend: Managed transport (4PL/TMSaaS): 0.5%–2%
  • Per-API-call: Emerging for carrier data + port community; $0.001–$0.05/call at vol; project44 carrier network access partly metered this way
  • Annual platform + overage: Dominant mid-market model

PrimusNeo positioning: AZOA priced per-API-call/per-integration-event (mirroring MuleSoft/Boomi at $0.01–$0.10/tx). NEOS governance priced per-entity (carrier, 3PL, shipper) — network-effect pricing rewarding ecosystem growth.

5. Regulatory Overlay

IMO CII / EEXI (in force Jan 2023): Vessels ≥5K GT must calculate/report CII annually. D-rated 3 consecutive years or E → corrective action plan. CII tightens ~2%/yr through 2026. Drives demand for voyage optimization, slow-steaming analytics — direct Fractal Engine use case.

EU ETS — Maritime (2024 onward): 100% of intra-EU + 50% of extra-EU voyages calling EU ports. At ~€60–70/tonne CO₂, compliance costs reach tens of millions €/yr for large carrier — hard financial incentive for AMP-driven route/speed optimization.

EU CBAM (definitive phase Jan 2026): Authorized declarants only for steel, aluminium, cement, fertilizers, electricity, hydrogen. CBAM certificates tied to EU ETS price. Creates new doc workflows + customs classification + supplier carbon data — addressable by NEOS + AZOA.

US 21st Century Customs Framework (21CCF): Mandatory e-record-keeping, expanded SC data sharing, ACE 2.0 (in progress). Customs brokers must report data breaches. AZOA positioned for 21CCF-compliant data flows.

Single Window Mandates: IMO FAL + EU Maritime Single Window framework (effective 2025) require pre-arrival + port clearance data via national single-window — AZOA integration play at port-operator + shipping-line tier.

6. Procurement Signals

Buyer Cycle
CSCO / CPO Large enterprise; 9–18 mo. 2024 CSCO agenda: resilience + tech ROI + sustainability
COO / Head of Network Ops Mid-tier 3PLs / regional carriers; 6–12 mo
CTO / VP Engineering Technical gatekeeper for API platforms; critical for AZOA/NEOS pilot approval
Head of Trade Compliance / VP Customs NEOS + CBAM/21CCF tooling buyer

Carrier–shipper–3PL triangle: No single party controls the network. Carriers own vessel data; 3PLs own last-mile/warehouse data; shippers own cargo data. TradeLens failed precisely because Maersk tried to own a network requiring trust from its competitors. PrimusNeo’s governance-neutral NEOS positioning is a direct answer.

RFP cycles: Top-20 carriers + top-5 3PLs run formal tech RFPs annually/biannually; typically Q1 for budget alignment. Target Q3–Q4 budget season convos.

7. Rejection Reasons

Consortium / network-effect fatigue (TradeLens precedent): TradeLens shut down Nov 2022 after 4 yrs + $1B+ combined investment. Maersk ownership perceived competitively by rivals (MSC, CMA CGM, Evergreen never joined). Asian carriers cited governance concerns. Lesson for PrimusNeo: NEOS must demonstrate governance neutrality + open standards alignment (DCSA, GS1, OpenAPI) from day one. No single carrier may access competitor data.

Legacy TMS/WMS integration complexity: Oracle OTM, SAP TM, Manhattan have deeply customized large-account implementations. Pre-built connectors required. Action: AZOA must publish connector libraries for top-5 TMS + top-5 WMS before enterprise sales scale.

Carrier data hoarding: Ocean carriers treat real-time vessel/container position as competitive. p44/FourKites spent years building carrier network agreements; gaps remain. Either partner with existing visibility networks or offer carriers a data-value-exchange model.

Perceived overlap with existing visibility spend: Shippers paying $300K+/yr to p44/FourKites won’t add another layer. Must displace OR offer superset (spatial context + governance + intelligence).

“We built it ourselves” at top carriers: Maersk (Captain), CMA CGM (CEVA), MSC (MSC One), DHL (myDHLi), UPS. Motion here is co-sell / embed (white-label Fractal or AZOA data fabric), not displacement.

Summary

Logistics tech segments: TMS ($400K–$3M/yr enterprise), WMS ($500K–$3M Y1), visibility ($100K–$1M/yr), port/terminal OS ($300K–$1.5M/yr terminal). Consulting via Accenture/Deloitte/GEP at $1.5K–$8.5K/day depending on seniority. Pricing favors per-shipment ($0.50–$10), per-site, or annual platform; API/event-metered emerging. Regulatory pressure from IMO CII, EU ETS, EU CBAM (now definitive), US 21CCF creates genuine urgency for data-layer + compliance tooling. Primary buyers: CSCOs + COOs at 3PLs/carriers; CTOs hold integration veto. Central challenge: the TradeLens shadow — any cross-party logistics network play must demonstrate ironclad governance neutrality (NEOS), pre-certified integrations (AZOA), and carrier-data value exchange (Fractal) before procurement commits.

Manufacturing & Industrial Production — Engagement Model Research Brief

Context: $16.8T global manufacturing value (UNIDO 2024), 70% of Industry 4.0 pilots never scale (McKinsey), ~$50B/yr unplanned downtime, average OEE 60–65% vs. 85% world-class target. Regulatory headwinds: EU Digital Product Passport (ESPR), CBAM, Scope 3 mandatory disclosure.

1. Comparable Vendor Pricing

Digital Twin / IIoT Platforms

PTC ThingWorx — No published list price. Per-core (server) plus named-user and device counts. Enterprise deployments: $150K–$500K+/yr for a meaningful plant-level rollout; seven-figure annual spend is common for multi-site engagements. PTC sold the ThingWorx/Kepware portfolio for up to $725M in late 2025.

Siemens Insights Hub (formerly MindSphere) — Tiered Basic / Standard / Premium, scaled by asset-attribute count and cloud bundles. $50K–$300K/yr for a mid-size plant; large OEMs in the Premium tier often exceed $1M/yr across a fleet.

GE Vernova Proficy MES / Predix Manufacturing Data Cloud — Proficy MES starts publicly at ~$5,800/month ($69.6K/yr); real deployments $150K–$400K/yr per plant. Predix-layer contracts are custom enterprise.

AVEVA System Platform / MES — Subscription; ~$350/month at entry (single-line monitoring), scaling to $100K–$500K/yr for full MES deployments.

Cognite Data Fusion — Custom enterprise. Industry benchmarks for asset-heavy industrials place contracts at $500K–$3M/yr for enterprise deployments. Independent study found $21.6M value over deployment lifecycle.

Tulip (Frontline Operations) — $1,200/interface/yr (Professional) to $2,400/interface/yr (Enterprise); minimum 10 interfaces. A 20-station plant runs $24K–$48K/yr in software alone.

Litmus Edge — $5K–$15K/edge device/yr; Edge Manager fleet licensing additive. Professional services at $200–$400/hr.

Enterprise IIoT (general) — Platform licensing typically $50K–$300K/yr at enterprise. Full single-plant digital twin builds: $500K–$2M initial + $100K–$400K/yr ongoing. Azure Digital Twins is consumption-based.

MES / MOM Platforms

SAP Digital Manufacturing (DMC, formerly ME) — Published floor ~$105/month (entry SaaS tier). Real enterprise plant deployments: $200K–$1M+/yr depending on module count (production, quality, OEE, genealogy).

Rockwell FactoryTalk MES — Per production line/asset + named users. Single-plant deployment: $200K–$600K all-in for software + integration. Annual maintenance: 15–20% of license.

MES Market General TCO (single plant, 20–30 machines, 3-year): - Cloud-native SaaS MES: $29K–$72K/yr; 3-yr TCO $87K–$216K - On-premise MES: 3-yr TCO $250K–$500K - Professional services typically exceed software license cost by 2–3×

APM / Predictive Maintenance

  • AspenTech Mtell — Custom; asset-heavy process industry contracts $300K–$2M/yr at scale.
  • C3 AI APM — Outcome-linked; large-scale heavy industry deployments $1M–$5M/yr total.
  • Uptake — Discrete mfg / fleet APM contracts generally $250K–$1M/yr.

ESG / DPP / Scope 3

  • Sphera — Enterprise mfg contracts with full EHS + Scope 3 + product stewardship: $150K–$600K/yr. Average disclosed transaction ~$16,874 (SME pilot).
  • Watershed — Most enterprise contracts $50K–$150K/yr; complex Scope 3 + CSRD: $150K–$300K/yr.
  • Circularise (DPP / traceability) — Mid-size mfr supply chain layer: $50K–$200K/yr. EU ESPR mandates DPPs for batteries (2025), textiles/electronics (2027–2029).
  • Sweep / Optera — $40K–$120K/yr based on employee count and supplier network.

2. Engagement Scale Tiers (3-Year All-In: License + Integration + Ongoing)

Tier Scope 3-Year Cost Range Notes
Small 1 plant, 1 production line, <50 machines $200K–$750K Cloud MES + basic IIoT; 2–6 month go-live
Mid 3–12 plants, mixed legacy/modern OT $1.5M–$8M Enterprise IIoT + MES; heavy integration; 12–24 months per wave
Large / Enterprise Tier-1 OEM, 25+ plants, global $10M–$40M+ Full twin + MES + APM + ESG/DPP; 30–60 month horizon

Professional services dominate — typically 2–3× software license for complex OT-connected deployments. New-site rollout on an established template: $50K–$250K per additional plant.

3. Services Rates — SI / Consulting for IT/OT Integration

Tier-1 Global SIs (Accenture Industry X, Capgemini, Deloitte Digital, IBM) - Day rate: $1,600–$3,200/day ($200–$400/hr) senior OT/mfg consultants (NA/Western Europe) - Principal/Director architects: $3,000–$5,000/day - Offshore blended: $800–$1,400/day (India/Poland/Mexico COE)

Specialist OT/MES SIs (Hitachi Digital Services, ATS, Rockwell SI partners, Inductive Automation SI partners) - $1,200–$2,400/day certified MES/SCADA engineers - Fixed-fee single-plant cloud-MES rollout (8–12 wks): $80K–$200K - OT network segmentation + IT/OT gateway: $100K–$400K per plant

Implementation rates (general) - MES implementation consultants: $150–$350/hr; 100–700+ consultant-hours typical for configuration - Annual S&M: 15–25% of license value/yr

4. Pricing Mechanics Norms

Mechanic Prevalence Notes
Per-asset / per-device Common (IIoT, APM, twin) Siemens, Litmus, AspenTech
Per-tag (data point) Common in SCADA/historian OSIsoft/AVEVA PI ($0.50–$5/tag/yr at scale)
Per-seat / named user Common (MES, ESG, analytics) SAP DM, Sphera, Watershed
Per-interface / per-station Tulip model Charges workstation, not headcount
% of CapEx Uncommon OT integrators sometimes 10–20% of plant CapEx
% of OEE / gainshare Emerging Fixed base + 10–30% share of documented savings; attribution-hard
Outcome-based / XaaS Growing in equipment sector Guaranteed uptime SLAs; $/ton, $/vehicle
Subscription (annual SaaS) Dominant trend 2023–2026 Cloud MES, ESG, IIoT moving here

Hybrid (base platform + consumption/asset overage) is now the most common enterprise structure. Pure gain-share remains rare in mfg due to attribution disputes.

5. Procurement Signals

Decision Makers by Deal Size: - Plant Manager / VP Ops — Signs <$250K. Prioritizes payback <18 months, minimal IT dependency. - CIO / VP Digital / CDO — Signs $250K–$2M. Architecture fit, cloud strategy, OT cyber, vendor roadmap. - COO / CFO — Required above $2M. Program ROI, supply chain resilience, ESG compliance. CSO sign-off on ESG/DPP. - Procurement — Enters formally above $500K; RFP/RFQ, benchmarking, MSA/SLA terms.

RFP Cadences: - Enterprise CapEx >$5M: 6–18 month cycle - Mid-market OpEx SaaS $500K–$2M: 4–9 months; often paid pilot/POC $50K–$150K - ESG/compliance deadline-driven: 2–4 months when mandated (CBAM 2024, DPP 2025–2027)

CapEx vs. OpEx: - Strong post-COVID bias toward OpEx/subscription — reduces upfront approval, preserves capital - OT infrastructure (edge HW, OPC-UA gateways, historian servers) still CapEx-classified — split: software OpEx, connectivity CapEx

6. What Buyers Will NOT Pay For

Vendor Lock-In — Single largest recurring rejection. Buyers reject proprietary data formats, non-standard protocols, platform-bundled AI that blocks open tooling, single-vendor historian/edge lock.

Integration Complexity / “Black Box” OT — 42% of manufacturers cite OT integration as primary scaling barrier. No credible connector story for legacy PLCs (Siemens S7, Allen-Bradley, Modbus) = rejected at pilot.

Unclear ROI / Unquantified Payback — 48% report buyer’s remorse, most often from integration cost overruns (38%) and implementation delays (35%). No quantified OEE/downtime scenario with payback <24 months = stalls at COO/CFO.

Pilot-to-Scale Gap — 70% of pilots never scale. Buyers now reject POC-first proposals lacking a defined productionization path and budget envelope.

Underestimated Total Cost — Hidden costs that kill deals post-signature: OT segmentation, historian per-tag overage, change management, retraining, ML model maintenance. Binding TCO disclosure increasingly required in RFP responses.

Data Sovereignty / Cloud Residency — European manufacturers reject US-hyperscaler-only deployments without EU data residency or on-premise/hybrid for production IP and quality data.

Key Pricing Anchors (One-Paragraph Summary)

IIoT/digital-twin platform licenses run $50K–$300K/yr per plant for mid-tier vendors (Siemens Insights Hub, AVEVA) and $150K–$500K+/yr for premium platforms (PTC ThingWorx, Cognite); MES software adds $100K–$500K/yr per plant for enterprise vendors (SAP, Rockwell), while cloud-native MES (Tulip, Symestic) starts under $30K/yr for small lines. Three-year all-in (license + integration + ongoing) is $200K–$750K for a single-plant small engagement, $1.5M–$8M for a mid-market 3–12 plant rollout, and $10M–$40M+ for a Tier-1 OEM global program. SI day rates for OT/MES integration run $1,200–$3,200/day onshore. ESG/Scope 3/DPP platforms (Sphera, Watershed, Circularise) run $50K–$300K/yr at enterprise scale, with EU regulatory deadlines (CBAM, ESPR/DPP by 2027) compressing procurement cycles. Dominant rejection triggers: vendor lock-in, unquantified ROI, underestimated integration cost.

2. Engagement Scale Tiers — 3-Year TCO for Coordination/Intelligence Layer

Small: <10 Robots, Single Facility, Startup/Integrator

Current fleet mgmt: $0–$5K/mo (free tier or entry InOrbit). Pain: no mission-level coordination, no governance, no cross-system telemetry. PrimusNeo 3-yr TCO: $120K–$300K · Y1 $60K–$120K (integration + deployment + first license year), Y2–3 $30K–$90K/yr. Primary layers: AZOA (interop to ROS/proprietary) + Fractal (telemetry/analytics).

Mid: 50–500 Robots, Multi-Site, Mid-Size Integrator/OEM

Current spend: InOrbit/Formant $25K–$250K/yr; custom SI $200K–$800K. Pain: heterogeneous vendors, multi-site dispatch, safety audit readiness, fleet analytics. PrimusNeo 3-yr TCO: $750K–$2.5M · AMP (spatial across sites) + NEOS (fleet governance, SLA councils) + AZOA (vendor-neutral API) + Fractal (predictive maintenance). Services: $250K–$600K integration; $150K–$400K/yr upkeep. Displaces 2–4 point solutions ($50K–$500K/yr combined).

Large: 1,000+ Robots, Global OEM / National Logistics Fleet

Reference class: ABB, FANUC, KUKA, Boston Dynamics, Symbotic. Current: proprietary OEM fleet sw + SI ($1M–$5M+/yr); custom twin $500K–$3M. Pain: cross-vendor fleet interop, global compliance (EU AI Act, ISO 10218, FDA/FAA overlay). PrimusNeo 3-yr TCO: $3M–$12M · All four layers; multi-tenant spatial OS; governance councils per site/region; AZOA federation; Fractal with custom model retraining. Services: $800K–$2M integration; $500K–$1.5M/yr upkeep + infra. Benchmark: NVIDIA/ABB twin programs run $2M–$8M/yr SW + SI.

3. Services Rates

Sustained upkeep 18–22% of annual license value

4. Pricing Mechanics

Robotics responds to five mechanics:

  1. Per-robot/month SaaS — dominant for fleet mgmt + RaaS overlays. Industry $50–$500/robot/mo software; $2K–$5K/robot/mo full RaaS. PrimusNeo positions $200–$800/robot/mo for coordination/intelligence layer (depending on Fractal depth + AZOA connector count).
  2. Per-mission / per-task — emerging for logistics AMRs (cost/pick, cost/delivery). PrimusNeo can meter Fractal inference + AZOA API calls at $0.002–$0.01/mission event.
  3. Enterprise flat annual license — preferred OEMs/Tier-1 integrators for predictability. PrimusNeo offers this for 500+ robot deployments with per-robot-per-yr floor.
  4. RaaS revenue share — Knightscope/Cobalt model. PrimusNeo participates as platform partner (3–8% of operator RaaS revenue) where coordination layer is embedded in partner’s offering.
  5. Per-site SLA — construction, ag, surgical. NEOS governance councils map to per-facility accountability.

5. Regulatory Overlay

Standard / Regulation Scope Implication
ISO 10218-1:2025 / -2:2025 Industrial robots + cells AMP must support safety-zone geofencing; Fractal audit logs cert-ready
ISO 13482:2014 Personal care / service robots (humanoids) NEOS supports hazard accountability traceability
EU AI Act (2024, enforcement 2026) High-risk AI in robots near humans Fractal classified as “AI system” under Annex III; requires conformity assessment, logging, human oversight
FAA Part 107 Commercial UAS <25kg (US) $175 knowledge test + $5/drone registration; 90–120 day waiver. AZOA must support UTM API integration
FDA 510(k) Surgical robotics (Class II) $6,084–$24,335 FDA fee + $20K–$30K consulting; 142-day median review. Fractal as data layer must meet SaMD if processing clinical decision data
RIA TR R15.806 Human-robot collaboration (US) Internal compliance. Fractal risk signals support HRC safety documentation

Any deployment touching surgical, cobot HRC, or drone fleet requires a regulatory posture document as engagement deliverable. Deal gate, not nice-to-have.

6. Procurement Signals

Title Authority Entry Point
VP Robotics / Head of Automation Technical champion; fleet roadmap AZOA interop demo + Fractal predictive maintenance ROI
CTO Architecture sign-off; multi-yr platform Full stack briefing + Tie Town reference arch
COO / VP Ops Budget owner; RaaS opex framing Cost/robot/mo vs current fragmented spend; downtime cost model
Head of Safety / Compliance Veto in regulated contexts ISO 10218 + EU AI Act + FDA compliance posture doc

Sales motion: - RaaS motion (opex framing): Add line item on existing monthly RaaS invoice. “Already paying $3K/robot/mo to Locus — we add $300 for coordination intelligence + cut unplanned downtime 15%.” Requires RaaS vendor partnership or OEM channel. - Pilot-to-fleet funnel: Single-facility pilot (3–6 mo, fixed $50K–$150K) → multi-site → enterprise MSA. McKinsey: 70% of pilots fail to scale — Tie Town reference is the counter-narrative. - OEM embedded licensing: Target ABB, FANUC, KUKA, UR, Boston Dynamics — embed AZOA connector + Fractal as licensed module in their robot mgmt software. Revenue share or $50–$200/robot/yr royalty at scale.

7. Rejection Reasons

  1. Safety/cert gap — “Not ISO 10218 / EU AI Act certified.” Mitigation: compliance posture doc; Fractal audit logs as cert-ready evidence.
  2. Vendor concentration risk — “Already on NVIDIA Isaac + ROS 2.” Mitigation: AZOA is additive, not replacement; runs alongside Isaac without forking ROS graph.
  3. Integration with ROS / proprietary stacks — ROS 2 DDS + proprietary controllers (FANUC KAREL, ABB RAPID, KUKA KRL) need custom connectors. Mitigation: AZOA connector library; scope/price per connector explicitly.
  4. ROI per robot unclear — Mitigation: Fractal predictive maintenance ROI model (1 avoided downtime incident = $5K–$50K in logistics).
  5. Talent shortage — Mitigation: managed deployment service; NVIDIA/AWS SI partner channel.
  6. Humanoid uncertainty — Figure, 1X, Agility, Boston Dynamics Atlas pre-commercial at scale as of 2026; enterprise in “watch and wait.” Avoid over-indexing RaaS models on humanoids until commercial fleet sizes materialize.

Summary

Robotics is PrimusNeo’s highest near-term AMP + Fractal opportunity. Fleet mgmt SaaS: $50–$500/robot/mo (InOrbit anchors at $500). RaaS bundles: $2K–$5K/robot/mo (Locus, Knightscope). Sim + OS tooling (NVIDIA Isaac, ROS 2) free — commoditizes infra layer, creating demand for differentiated coordination/intelligence above. PrimusNeo 3-yr TCO: $120K (small) → $12M (global OEM). Key deal gates: ISO 10218:2025 / EU AI Act compliance posture + clear per-robot ROI. Fastest entry: OEM embedded licensing with NVIDIA partners or major robot OEMs, then RaaS revenue-share with fleet operators. Buyer coalition: VP Robotics + COO + Head of Safety; COO’s opex framing via RaaS = path of least procurement resistance.

Technology Vertical — Market Research Brief

PrimusNeo: AMP (Spatial OS) · NEOS (Governance) · AZOA (Interop Fabric) · Fractal Engine (Intelligence) Date: 2026-05-23

1. Comparable Vendor Pricing

Platform Engineering / IDP

Managed IDP pricing has converged at $22–$78/developer/month:

Vendor Pricing
Roadie (managed Backstage) ~$22/developer/month — entry anchor for managed OSS portals
Port Standard ~$40/seat/mo; Enterprise ~$60–$78/seat/mo
OpsLevel ~$39/user/mo base; Startup ~$30/dev/mo
Cortex ~$65–$69/user/mo at scale (community-reported)
Humanitec Platform Orchestrator From ~$1,979/mo (5 users); orchestration layer above portals; enterprise custom
Backstage (self-hosted) $0 license. Effective TCO: 3–5 senior platform engineers @ $250K loaded = $750K–$1.25M/yr carrying cost + $12K–$24K/yr infra. 3-yr TCO typically exceeds $2M before productivity gains

Federation / Multi-Org Coordination

  • Open Policy Agent (OPA): Free, CNCF-graduated. Implementation engagements $150–$300/hr
  • Styra (OPA commercial wrapper): $100K–$500K ACV typical
  • GitOps (Flux/ArgoCD): OSS; integration services at standard platform-eng rates

Data Fabrics / Data Mesh

  • Snowflake: ~$2–$4/credit; mid-market monthly $10K–$80K; large enterprise $100K–$500K+/month
  • Databricks: ~$0.22–$0.55/DBU-hour SQL/ETL + cloud VM costs; typically 20–40% cheaper than Snowflake at scale but requires FinOps maturity
  • Starburst (Trino federated): Cost-offload from Snowflake; enterprise custom
  • Dremio: ~$75K–$250K ACV estimated from community reports

AI Gateways / Agent Infrastructure

Vendor Pricing
LangSmith (LangChain) Free (1 seat, 5K traces/mo); Plus $39/user/mo; traces $2.50/1K (14-day), $5.00/1K (400-day); enterprise custom
Weights & Biases ~$50/seat/mo (CoreWeave-acquired March 2025); enterprise custom
Modal (serverless GPU) A10G ~$1.10/hr; L40S ~$1.95/hr; H100 ~$3.95/hr
Replicate H100 ~$5.49/hr; overall 1.4–2.9x higher than Modal/Fal for custom models
LlamaIndex OSS framework; commercial Cloud tier custom

Spatial / 3D Dev Platforms

  • NVIDIA Omniverse Enterprise: $4,500/GPU/year subscription; enterprise contracts vary by GPU count
  • Niantic Lightship Spatial SDK: $0.80–$1.00/MAU beyond 100 MAU; shared AR sessions billed separately per multiplayer session
  • Unity Industry / Pro: Pro ~$2,040/seat/yr; Unity Enterprise custom; industrial twin modules add-on
  • Unreal Engine / MetaHuman: Free for games <$1M revenue; enterprise licensing/support $50K–$500K/yr for industrial deployment

Web3 Infrastructure

Vendor Pricing
Alchemy 30M CUs/mo free; pay-as-you-go $0.40–$0.45/1M CUs; 1 eth_call = 26 CUs so effective per-call ~$10.40–$11.70/1M calls
QuickNode Flat-rate RPS from $799/mo (75 RPS EVM chains); per-call ~$3.27–$12.25/1M calls
Infura 55M credits add-on $200/mo; 1 eth_call = 80 units → higher effective per-call cost than Alchemy
Chainstack Pro $199/mo (80M req, 400 RPS); Business $499/mo (200M req, 600 RPS, 20 nodes); enterprise custom

2. Engagement Scale Tiers — 3-Year TCO

Tier Profile IDP (3yr) Agent Infra (3yr) Spatial Collab (3yr) Comparable Market TCO (3yr)
Small <50 engineers, single-product startup $80K–$200K (IDP SaaS, no build) $30K–$80K (LangSmith Plus + Modal spot GPU) $20K–$60K (Unity Pro + basic Omniverse) $130K–$340K
Mid 50–500 engineers, scale-up $350K–$900K (Port/Cortex enterprise + 1–2 platform engineers) $120K–$400K (W&B + LangSmith enterprise + Modal committed) $80K–$200K (Omniverse 5–20 GPUs/yr) $550K–$1.5M
Large 500+ engineers, FAANG/MAANG, telco, hyperscaler $1.5M–$4M (Humanitec + self-hosted portals + 5–8 platform engineers) $500K–$2M+ (enterprise W&B/LangSmith + dedicated GPU fleet) $300K–$1M+ (Omniverse 100+ GPU/yr, Unity Enterprise) $2.3M–$7M+

Large-tier telcos and hyperscalers often embed these costs inside cloud committed-use agreements (EDP/CUD) at 20–40% effective discount off list.

4. Pricing Mechanics Norms

  • Per-developer/seat: $15–$100/dev/mo established IDP norm. $22 (Roadie) low end; $65–$78 (Cortex, Port Enterprise) high end. >500-engineer orgs prefer enterprise commits
  • Per-API-call / per-compute-unit: AI inference (LangSmith $2.50–$5/1K traces) + Web3 RPC (Alchemy, QuickNode, Chainstack). Compute-unit obfuscation common — effective per-call costs 10–200x advertised unit rate
  • Per-compute-hour (GPU): $1.10–$5.49/hr managed (Modal → Replicate) vs $2–$4/hr raw cloud. Serverless premiums 1.4–2.9x raw. Reserved/committed instances reduce 30–50%
  • Per-GB (storage/egress): Snowflake, Databricks. Egress $0.08–$0.12/GB routinely surprises eng teams — common TCO underestimate
  • % of platform spend: Less common in devtools; FinOps SaaS (Apptio, Harness Cloud Cost) typically 1–3% of monitored spend ACV
  • Enterprise commit / MAP: >$500K/year orgs get 12–36 month multi-year commits at 15–30% discounts + private pricing. Hyperscaler EDPs bundle IDP-adjacent tooling at 20–40% below public list
  • Usage-based + seat hybrid: Emerging norm (LangSmith, Humanitec) — base seat for governance + usage overage for compute/traces

5. Regulatory Overlay

Framework Applicability Cost
SOC 2 Type II Mandatory for SaaS to US enterprise / gov-adjacent $35K–$150K first year; $20K–$50K/yr ongoing (with automation)
ISO 27001 EU enterprise + telco + regulated-sector clients $25K–$80K initial cert; $15K–$30K/yr surveillance
FedRAMP (Mod/High) US federal agency sales; govtech-adjacent SaaS $500K–$2M+ to achieve; 12–18 months (post-2025 “20x” acceleration <5 weeks once package complete)
EU AI Act Full application Aug 2026; high-risk (Annex III) potentially deferred to Dec 2027 Conformity assessment $20K–$80K/high-risk system; GPAI docs + transparency ongoing
EU Data Act / DSA / DMA Interop fabrics (AZOA), platform gateways, data portability Legal/compliance advisory $50K–$200K/jurisdiction initial posture
US AI EOs Dual-use foundation model disclosure (above compute thresholds); federal procurement AI risk Internal governance program $100K–$500K+

73% of enterprise AI initiatives cite compliance posture as top-three vendor selection criterion (BCG 2026), up from 41% in 2024. CAIO roles exist in 61% of large enterprises — compliance champion is now a named buyer.

6. Procurement Signals

Buyer personas and triggers:

  • CTO: Platform strategy; build-vs-buy for IDP + agent infra. Responds to architecture credibility, standards alignment (OpenUSD, OpenTelemetry, CNCF), federated multi-org deployment proof. Entry via conf talks, technical whitepapers, OSS presence
  • VP Platform Engineering / Head of DevEx: Owns IDP budget + dev productivity KPIs (DORA metrics, deploy frequency, change failure rate). Responds to ROI on dev time saved — typically modeled at $150–$250K/engineer-year fully loaded. Entry via community (PlatformCon, KubeCon), peer referral
  • Chief AI Officer: Owns agent infra + LLMOps stack. Evaluates observability (LangSmith, W&B), governance (audit trails, policy enforcement), data residency. Entry via analyst relations + AI governance working groups
  • Head of Developer Experience: Onboarding speed, cognitive load, golden path adoption rates. Responds to PLG trials — hands-on before budget conversations

Motion patterns:

  • PLG to enterprise: Dominant dev-tool pattern. Free OSS/SDK inbound (Backstage plugin, AZOA connector, Fractal community tier) → team adoption → VP Platform/CTO expansion. Cursor reached $2B ARR by Feb 2026. PLG companies see ~9% free-to-paid conversion; top quartile reaches 25–30% with PQL-triggered outbound
  • OSS to paid tier upsell: Backstage (free) → Roadie/Port/Cortex ($22–$78/seat/mo). OPA (free) → Styra (enterprise). AZOA open SDK → AZOA federation engine (closed-core). Standard playbook for infrastructure OSS
  • Partner-led (SI channel): For large/MAANG/telco, Accenture or IBM Consulting frames the engagement; PrimusNeo enters as tech layer inside broader transformation. SI takes 20–40% impl margin; platform vendor takes license/commit

7. Rejection Reasons

  • Build-vs-buy / NIH syndrome: Engineering-led orgs (esp FAANG) default to “our requirements are unique.” Counter: top-performing orgs use hybrid buy+extend models, not full builds. Humanitec 2023 DevOps Benchmarking: top performers build IDP from OSS + vendor, not from scratch
  • Vendor lock-in: Federating four layers across four steward orgs raises legitimate portability concerns. Counter: open SDK + CC licensing on NEOS/Fractal; data export guarantees in AZOA; OpenUSD/open standards alignment in AMP
  • Open-source competition: Backstage, Crossplane, Flux, ArgoCD, OPA, MLflow, LangFuse — every commercial layer has credible OSS alt. Counter is TCO (Backstage = $2M+ over 3 yrs for large teams), time-to-value, enterprise support SLA
  • Integration with existing devtools: GitHub Enterprise, Jira, PagerDuty, Datadog, Slack/Teams — deep integrations required. First 90 days of any engagement must demonstrate connector coverage
  • Governance / data residency: EU-based tech (telcos, post-GDPR scale-ups) require in-region data. AI Act readiness + multi-region AZOA deployment must be demonstrable
  • Procurement cycle length: MAANG/telco enterprise procurement averages 6–18 months from first meeting to signed contract. PLG inbound shortens — product usage data substitutes for RFP at team level, escalating to procurement only after internal champions exist

Summary

Technology vertical has highest density of comparable vendor benchmarks + sharpest build-vs-buy instinct of any PrimusNeo target. IDP seat pricing clusters at $22–$78/dev/mo, with self-hosted Backstage carrying hidden $2M+ 3-yr TCO that commercial portals undercut on value. AI agent infra: $39–$50/seat/mo for observability + $1–$5/GPU-hour for inference. Spatial platforms (Omniverse, Unity Industrial): $4,500/GPU/yr and up. 3-yr stack TCO: $130K (50-engineer startup) → $7M+ (hyperscaler). SI rates: $50–$400/hr. Compliance (SOC 2, ISO 27001, EU AI Act) now top-3 vendor selection criterion for 73% of enterprises. Primary rejection vectors: NIH syndrome, open-source competition, data-residency. PrimusNeo’s strongest entry motion: OSS/SDK inbound (PLG) escalating through VP Platform Engineering + CAO champions.
